## The Facts in the Case / 案情始末

## I A Man of Fashion / I 时尚人物

===Original===
“Royal,” said the man’s mother that evening, “are you still thinking of Fanny Price?”
It was in Gramercy Park. As you may or may not know, Gramercy Park is the least noisy spot in the metropolitan Bedlam. Without being unreasonably aristocratic it is sedate and what agents call exclusive.
The park itself is essentially that. Its design is rather English. The use is restricted to adjoining residents. About it is a fence of high iron. Within are trees, paths, grasses, benches, great vases and a fountain. But none of the usual loungers, none of the leprous men, rancid women, and epileptic children that swarm in other New York squares. Yet these squares are open to all. To enter this park you must have a key. By day it is a playground. Nursemaids come there with little boys and girls, the subdued, undemonstrative, beautifully dressed children of the rich. At night it is empty as a vacant bier.

===Chinese===
「罗伊尔，」那天傍晚，这年轻人的母亲问道，「你还在想范妮·普莱斯（Fanny Price）么？」
地点在格拉默西公园（Gramercy Park）。诸位也许知道、也许不知道，格拉默西公园是这座大都会疯人院里最不喧闹的一隅。它并不矜贵得不近人情，却端庄沉静，用房产中介的话说，是「非请勿入」的。
公园本身大抵也是如此。它的格局颇英式。使用权仅限邻近的住户。四围是一道高铁栅栏。里头有树、小径、草地、长椅、大花盆与一座喷泉。却没有寻常那些游手好闲之徒，没有别处纽约广场上成群的、疮痞的男人、酸臭的女人与抽风的孩童。然而那些广场是对所有人开放的。要进这座公园，你得有一把钥匙。白日里它是游乐场。保姆们带着小少爷、小姐们来——那些富人家乖顺、不吵闹、衣饰精美的孩子。到了夜里，它便空荡如一张无人的停尸架。

===Original===
In a house that fronted the north side Royal Loftus lived with his mother, a proud, arrogant woman socially known to all, but who socially knew but few. Behind her, in the shade of the family tree, was her dead lord, Royal’s father and, more impressively still, the latter’s relatives, the entire Loftus contingent, a set of people super-respectable, supernally rich. She too was rich. She wore a wig, walked with a staff, spoke with a Mayfair intonation in a high-pitched voice, and, in the amplitudes of widowhood and wealth, entertained frequently but cared only for her son.
On this evening the two were seated together in a drawing-room that faced the park. The walls, after a fashion of long ago, were frescoed. The ceiling too was frescoed. The furniture belonged also to an earlier day. The modern note in the room was the absence of chandeliers and the appearance of Royal Loftus, who, in a Paris shirt and London clothes, was contemplating his painted nails.
At his feet was an Ardebil rug which originally had cost a small fortune and now was worth a big one. In allusion to it a girl to whom he had handed out the usual “You don’t care for me,” had retorted, “Not care for you! Why, Royal, I worship the rug you tread on.”
That girl was Fanny Price.

===Chinese===
在面朝公园北侧的一幢宅子里，罗伊尔·洛夫特斯（Royal Loftus）与母亲同住——这是个高傲的女人，社交场上无人不识，而她识得的人却寥寥。在她身后、家谱的荫庇之下，是她已故的夫君、罗伊尔的父亲；而更令人侧目的是后者那一脉亲族，整个洛夫特斯一党——一众超乎寻常地体面、超乎寻常地富贵的人。她本人亦富。她戴着假发，拄杖而行，操一口梅费尔（Mayfair）口音，嗓门又高又尖；在寡居与富足的宽裕里，她频繁宴客，却只把心放在独子一人身上。
这天傍晚，母子二人同坐在一间面朝公园的客厅里。墙壁是依着古法绘了壁画的。天花板也是。家具也属于更早的年代。这屋里唯一的现代气息，一是没有枝形吊灯，二是罗伊尔·洛夫特斯本人——他身着巴黎衬衫、伦敦衣裳，正端详自己染了色的指甲。
他脚下是一方阿尔达比勒（Ardebil）地毯，当初买进时便花了一笔小财，如今已值一笔大财。曾有个姑娘，对他照例敷衍的那句「你并不把我放在心上」反唇相讥道：「不把你放在心上！哎，罗伊尔，我崇拜你所踩的这块地毯呢。」
那姑娘便是范妮·普莱斯。

===Original===
“No,” he answered in reply to his mother’s question. The answer was strangely truthful. Fanny Price had tantalized him greatly. Semi-continuously he had thought of her for a long time. But not matrimonially. To him matrimony meant always one woman more and usually one man less. He had no wish to dwindle. When he was fifty he might, perhaps, to make a finish, marry some girl who wanted to begin. But that unselfishness was remote. He was quite young; what is worse, he was abominably good-looking. Fancy Aramis in a Piccadilly coat. That was the way he looked. His features were chiseled. On his lip was a mustache so slight that it might have been made with a crayon. His hair was black. His eyes were blue. Where they were not blue they were white, very white. They were wonderful eyes. With them he had done a great deal of execution.
At the time they rather haunted a young woman who moved in another sphere and whose acquaintance he had made quite adventurously. The name of this young woman was Marie Durand. It was of the latter, not of Fanny Price, that he was thinking.

===Chinese===
「不。」他答母亲的问题。这回答出奇地诚实。范妮·普莱斯曾大大地折磨过他。很长一段时间里，他半不间断地想着她。但并非以婚姻为念。在他看来，婚姻永远意味着多一个女人，通常还少一个男人。他可不想就此萎缩。待他五十岁时，或许——为求一个了局——会娶某个正要开始的姑娘。但那份无私尚遥不可及。他很年轻；更要命的是，他俊美得可恶。想象一下阿拉米斯（Aramis）穿一件皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）剪裁的外套。他那模样便是如此。五官如刻。唇上一抹髭，淡得像是用蜡笔描的。一头黑发。一双蓝眼。蓝眼之外便是眼白，极白。那是双妙极的眼睛。靠它们，他斩获良多。
其时，这双眼睛倒更常常萦绕着另一位活动于另一圈层的年轻女子的梦魂——他是相当冒险地与她结识的。这女子名叫玛丽·杜朗（Marie Durand）。他所想的，正是后者，而非范妮·普莱斯。

===Original===
“No,” he repeated. “But was it for Annandale that you asked her for tonight?”
“How perfectly absurd of you, Royal. Have you forgotten that he is in love with Sylvia? I asked her partly for you, partly for Orr.”
“Is he coming too! Good Lord! it is going to be ghastly.”
But at the side of the room a portière was being drawn and a servant announced:
“Miss Waldron.”

===Chinese===
「不。」他重复道，「可今夜你请她，是为安南代尔（Annandale）么？」
「你这人真是荒唐，罗伊尔。你忘了他爱的是西尔维娅（Sylvia）？我请她，一半为你，一半为奥尔（Orr）。」
「他也来！老天！那可要糟糕透了。」
可这时房间一侧的门帷已被拉开，一个仆人通报：
「沃尔德伦（Waldron）小姐。」

===Original===
With the charming manner of the thoroughbred New York girl a young woman entered. She was tall, willowy, with a face such as those one used to see in keepsakes—delightful things which now, like so many other delightful things, are seen no more.
As she approached Mrs. Loftus, who had risen to greet her, she made a little courtesy.
“Sylvia, this is so dear of you. And is your mother very well?”
Again the portière was drawn. A voice announced:
“Miss Price.”

===Chinese===
一位年轻女子以地道纽约姑娘那般迷人的仪态走了进来。她高挑、苗条，一张脸是往昔纪念册里才见得着的那种——那些可爱之物，如今正如许许多多别的可爱之物一样，再也见不着了。
她走向起身相迎的洛夫特斯太太时，浅浅行了個礼。
「西尔维娅，你能来真是太好了。你母亲身体可好？」
门帷又被拉开。一个声音通报：
「普莱斯小姐。」

===Original===
Then there appeared a girl adorably constructed—constructed, too, to be adored. She was slight and very fair. Her mouth resembled the red pulp of some flower of flesh. Her eyes were pools of purple, her hair a turban of gold.
Cannibalistically Loftus looked the delicious apparition up and down. He could have eaten it.
“Mr. Annandale,” the voice announced.
A man, big and blond, with a cavalry mustache and an amiable, aimless air, strolled in.
“Mr. Melanchthon Orr.”
On the heels of Annandale came a cousin of Miss Waldron, a lawyer by trade, a man with a bulldog face that was positively attractive.

===Chinese===
随后出现的是一个构造得惹人怜爱的姑娘——构造得也正宜被人怜爱。她身段纤细，肤色极白。她的嘴像某朵肉花红色的花心。她的眼是两汪紫色的潭，她的发是一顶金的头巾。
洛夫特斯如食人族一般，把这道可口的倩影上上下下打量了一番。他恨不得把它吃了。
「安南代尔先生。」那声音通报。
一个高大、金发的男子走了进来，唇上一抹骑兵式的髭，神情和蔼而漫无目的。
「梅兰希吞·奥尔（Melanchthon Orr）先生。」
紧随安南代尔之后进来的，是沃尔德伦小姐的一位表兄——以律师为业，一张斗牛犬般的脸，却偏偏生得讨人喜欢。

===Original===
There were more how-do-you-do’s, the usual platitudes, interrupted by the opening of doors at the further end of the room, where a butler, a squad of lackeys behind him, disclosed himself in silent announcement of dinner.
After the general move which then ensued and hosts and guests were seated at table, Orr created an immediate diversion by calling to Fanny Price and telling her that shortly she was to marry.
“Yes,” he continued, “and my cousin Sylvia is to marry also, though not so soon; but either Annandale or Royal will never marry at all.”
Bombarded by sudden questions Orr gazed calmly about.

===Chinese===
又是一场寒暄、几句常套的客套，随后被房间另一头开门的动静打断——一名管家、身后跟着一队侍者，无声地现身，示意开席。
待众人随之起身、主客入座之后，奥尔立刻制造了一阵轰动：他唤范妮·普莱斯，告诉她不久她便要嫁人。
「不错，」他续道，「我的表妹西尔维娅也要嫁人，虽则没那么快；可安南代尔或罗伊尔，二人中总有一个永不娶亲。」
被突如其来的连番追问一轰，奥尔从容四顾。

===Original===
“How do I know? Miranda told me. Miranda the spook. She charged five dollars for the information. If you like to make it up to me, I shall not mind in the least. On the contrary. You see, Mrs. Loftus,” Orr added, turning to his hostess, “I happened, when I went to her, to have your very kind invitation for this evening in my pocket, and, as she wanted something to psychometrize, I gave her that. She held it to her forehead and said, ‘I see you in the house of an elegant lady’—that is you, Mrs. Loftus; yes, there is no doubt about it—‘and there are present two young ladies, one fair, one dark, and two gentlemen; one of the gentlemen will never marry, but the dark young lady will marry in two years and the fair young lady in one. Five dollars. Thank you. Next.’ ”
“Did she say nothing about me except that I am an ‘elegant lady’?” Mrs. Loftus, with a pained laugh and a high voice, inquired.
“Did she say whom I am to marry?” Fanny Price asked, smiling, as she spoke, at Royal.

===Chinese===
「我怎么知道？米兰达告诉我的。通灵师米兰达。这条消息她收了我五块钱。你们若肯补给我，我一点不介意。相反，还求之不得。您瞧，洛夫特斯太太，」奥尔转向女主人补道，「我去她那儿时，碰巧口袋里揣着您今夜这份盛情邀请，她正想要件什么东西来感应，我便把它给了她。她把它贴在额头上，说：『我看见你在一位置高雅致的太太家』——那就是您了，洛夫特斯太太；是的，毫无疑问——『在座有两位年轻小姐，一白一黑，两位先生；其中一位先生永不娶亲，但那位肤色深的年轻小姐两年内出嫁，肤色浅的一年之内。五块钱。谢谢。下一位。』」
「除了说我是『雅致的太太』之外，她没再说我别的？」洛夫特斯太太带着一丝痛苦的笑、声音尖尖地问。
「她可说我要嫁的是谁？」范妮·普莱斯问，说这话时朝罗伊尔笑着。

===Original===
“But, Melanchthon, surely you do not believe in these things?” said Sylvia gravely.
“Of course he does not,” Loftus exclaimed. “He does not believe in anything. Do you, Orr?”
“I believe in a great many things,” the lawyer replied. “I have precisely three hundred and sixty-five beliefs—one for every day in the year.”
“When the twenty-ninth of February comes around how do you manage then?” said Fanny.
“Yes,” said Annandale, “and how about April first?”
Orr raised a finger. “Jest if you will. But beliefs are a great comfort, or would be among people like you who have none except in fashion, and there is the oddity of it, for belief in this sort of thing is very fashionable now, particularly in London. Yes, indeed, Lady Cloden—you remember her, she was Clara Hastings—well, she went to a spook in Tottenham Court Road, and the spook told her that she would have twins. Immediately she had herself insured. In London, you know, you can be insured against anything. The twins appeared and she got £5,000. Belief in this sort of thing is therefore not merely fashionable but convenient.”

===Chinese===
「可是，梅兰希吞，你总不至于信这些罢？」西尔维娅正色道。
「他当然不信。」洛夫特斯叫起来，「他什么都不信。你信么，奥尔？」
「我信的事情可多了。」这位律师答，「我恰好有三百六十五条信念——一年里每天一条。」
「那二月二十九日来的时候你怎么办？」范妮说。
「不错，」安南代尔说，「还有四月一日呢？」
奥尔竖起一根手指。「你们尽管取笑。但信念是一大安慰，或者对你们这种除了时尚什么都不信的人来说本该是一大安慰；而妙就妙在，信这一套如今正时髦得很，尤其在伦敦。可不，克洛登夫人（Lady Cloden）——你们记得她罢，她原是克拉拉·黑斯廷斯（Clara Hastings）——她去托特纳姆宫廷路（Tottenham Court Road）找个通灵师，那通灵师说她要生双胞胎。她立刻给自己保了险。在伦敦你们知道，什么都能保。双胞胎真的来了，她得了五千镑。可见信这一套不仅时髦，而且实惠。」

===Original===
In the ripple of laughter which followed the logic, Orr turned to Mrs. Loftus, Annandale to Miss Waldron, Loftus to Fanny Price.
“You take very kindly to snubbing, don’t you?” said the latter.
“I?”
“Oh, pooh! The other day I saw Mr. Royal Loftus trying to scrape acquaintance with a young person in the street. I never laughed more in my life. She would not look at you. Is that sort of thing amusing? Why don’t you take a girl of your size?”
Loftus looked into Fanny’s eyes. “If you want to know, because you are all so deuced prim.”
“Ah!” and Fanny made a tantalizing little face, showing, as she did so, the point of her tongue. “Now tell me, what makes you think so?”

===Chinese===
这番逻辑引来一阵笑声涟漪，笑声里奥尔转向洛夫特斯太太，安南代尔转向沃尔德伦小姐，洛夫特斯转向范妮·普莱斯。
「你倒是颇能受冷落，是罢？」后者说。
「我？」
「呸！前几天我看见罗伊尔·洛夫特斯先生在街上想跟一个年轻姑娘搭讪。我这辈子没那样笑过。人家连看都不看你一眼。那种事好玩么？你怎么不找个配得上你的姑娘？」
洛夫特斯盯着范妮的眼。「你定要知道的话——因为你们全都规矩得要命。」
「啊！」范妮做了个逗人的小脸，同时露出舌尖一点。「那你倒说说，你怎么会这样想？」

===Original===
Across the table Annandale was talking to Sylvia Waldron. His manner was rather earnest, but his utterance had become a trifle thick.
“Oh, Arthur,” the girl at last interrupted him. “Don’t drink any more. You have had five glasses of champagne already.”
Heroically Annandale put his glass down. “Since you wish it, I won’t. But it does not hurt me. I can stand anything.”
“I am afraid it may grow on you.”
Annandale laughed. “Grow on me,” he repeated. “I like that. Why, I am cultivating it.”
Miss Waldron laughed too. “Yes, but you know you must not. I won’t let you.” Then at once, with that tact which was part of her, she changed the subject. “Doesn’t Fanny look well tonight?”

===Chinese===
桌子那一头，安南代尔正与西尔维娅·沃尔德伦说话。他神态颇为恳切，可吐字已略显含糊。
「唉，阿瑟（Arthur），」姑娘终于打断他，「别再喝了。你已经喝了五杯香槟了。」
安南代尔英勇地放下酒杯。「既然你不愿意，我就不喝了。可这于我无碍。我什么都能扛。」
「我怕你会渐渐上瘾。」
安南代尔笑起来。「上瘾，」他重复，「这话我爱听。怎么说呢，我正培养它呢。」
沃尔德伦小姐也笑了。「是，可你知道你不能。我不许你这样。」随即，她以一种与生俱来的得体，转了话题：「范妮今夜看着气色真好，是罢？」

===Original===
“Very. She is the prettiest girl in New York. But you are the best and the dearest. What is more, you are an angel.”
“To you I want to try to be.”
“Only,” resumed Annandale with a spark of the wit which is born of champagne, “don’t try to be a saint—it is a step backward.”
“Yes, Mrs. Loftus,” Orr was saying, “Miranda is fat, very fat. All mediums are. The fatter they are the more confidence you may have.”
Then there was more small talk. Courses succeeded each other. Sweets came and went. Presently Mrs. Loftus looked circuitously about and slowly arose. When she and the girls had gone and the men were reseated Loftus turned to Orr.

===Chinese===
「极好。她是纽约最美的姑娘。可你是最善良、最亲爱的。更何况，你是个天使。」
「对你，我想努力做一个天使。」
「只是，」安南代尔闪过一星香槟催生的机智，续道，「别想着做圣徒——那可是退了一步。」
「可不是，洛夫特斯太太，」奥尔正说着，「米兰达胖，胖得很。灵媒个个如此。越胖的越信得过。」
随后又是一阵闲谈。一道道菜依次上来。甜点上了又撤。不久，洛夫特斯太太不动声色地四下一顾，缓缓起身。待她与姑娘们离席、男人们重新坐定，洛夫特斯转向奥尔。

===Original===
“Did the spook say anything else?”
Orr was selecting a cigar from a cabinet on wheels which a servant trundled about. He chose and lighted one before he replied. Then he looked at Loftus.
“Yes, she told me that she saw—” Orr paused. The cigar had gone out. He lighted it again. “She told me that she saw death hereabouts.”
Loftus was also lighting a cigar. “Then I too am a spook,” he replied. “I foretold that you would say something ghastly.”
“But, my dear fellow,” Orr rejoined, “truth is always that. People fancy that it is made of lace and pearls like a girl on her wedding day. It is not that at all. It is just what you call it. It is ghastly. Read history. Any reliable work is but a succession of groans. The more reliable it is the more groans there will be.”

===Chinese===
「那通灵师还说了别的没有？」
奥尔正从一只由仆人推着转的轮车烟柜里挑雪茄。他挑了一支、点燃，这才作答。随后他望向洛夫特斯。
「说了，她告诉我她看见——」奥尔顿了顿。雪茄灭了。他重新点上。「她告诉我，她看见这一带有死亡。」
洛夫特斯也正点雪茄。「那么我也算个通灵师了，」他答，「我早预言过你会说些吓人的话。」
「可是，亲爱的朋友，」奥尔应道，「真相向来如此。人们想象它是用花边与珍珠做的，像新娘子在婚礼上那般。完全不是那么回事。它就是你说的那样。它是吓人的。去读读历史罢。任何一部可靠的史书都不过是一连串的呻吟。越可靠的，呻吟越多。」

===Original===
Annandale, who had been helping himself to brandy, interrupted. “Talking of reading things, I saw somewhere that after some dinner or other, when the women had gone, a chap began on a rather—well, don’t you know, a sort of barnyard story and the host, who could not quite stomach it, said: ‘Suppose we continue the conversation in the drawing-room?’ So, Royal, what do you say? If Orr is going to shock us, suppose we do.”
Loftus with a painted fingertip flipped the ashes from his cigar.
“I fear that I have lost the ability to be shocked, but not the ability to be bored.”
Yet presently, after another cigar and conscious perhaps that Fanny Price, though often exasperating, never bored, he returned with his guests to the drawing-room.

===Chinese===
正自斟白兰地的安南代尔插了进来。「说起读东西，我在哪儿见过，某次晚宴之后，女宾离席，有个家伙讲起一个颇为——呃，你懂的，那种农家院子式的故事，主人有点听不下去，便说：『我们不如到客厅里继续谈话罢？』所以，罗伊尔，你怎么说？奥尔既要吓我们，我们不如也照办。」
洛夫特斯用一根染色的指尖弹了弹雪茄的灰。
「只怕我已经失了被吓着的能力，却没失被闷着的能力。」
然而不久，又抽过一支雪茄之后——也许是意识到范妮·普莱斯虽常气人，却从不闷人——他便随客人们一同回到了客厅。

## II The Pocket Venus / II 掌上维纳斯

===Original===
“How do you like my hat?” said Fanny to Sylvia. Since the dinner a week had gone. The two girls were in Irving Place.
Irving Place is south of Gramercy Park. To the west are the multiple atrocities of Union Square, to the east are the nameless shames of Third Avenue. Between the two Irving Place lies, a survival of the peace of old New York. At the lower end are the encroaching menaces of trade, but at the upper end, from which you enter Gramercy Park, there is a quiet, pervasive and almost provincial. It was here that Sylvia Waldron lived.
People take a house for six months or an apartment for a year and call it a home. That is a base use for a sacred word. A home is a place in which you are born, in which your people die and your progeny emerge. A home predicates the present, but particularly the past and with it the future. Any other variety of residence may be agreeable or the reverse, but it is not a home.

===Chinese===
「我这帽子你觉得如何？」范妮对西尔维娅说。自那场晚宴以来，一星期已过。两个姑娘在欧文广场（Irving Place）。
欧文广场在格拉默西公园以南。其西是联合广场（Union Square）那层出不穷的丑态，其东是第三大道（Third Avenue）那不可名状的腌臜。欧文广场横亘其间，是旧日纽约那一份宁静的遗存。其下端是步步进逼的商贾之患，可上端——由那儿你才进得了格拉默西公园——却有一种静谧，弥漫的、近乎外省的静谧。西尔维娅·沃尔德伦便住在这里。
人们租一幢半年的宅子、或一套一年的公寓，便称之为「家」。这是对神圣之词的亵渎。所谓家，是你生于斯、你的亲人死于斯、你的后嗣出于斯的地方。家意味着现在，更意味着过去，并连同未来。除此之外任何一种居所，或惬意或反之，都不是家。

===Original===
In Irving Place there are homes. Among them was the house in which Sylvia Waldron lived. It had been in her family for sixty years. In London a tenure such as that is common. In New York it is phenomenal.
To Sylvia the phenomenon was a matter of course. What amazed her were the migrations of others. Of Fanny, for instance. Each autumn Sylvia would say to her, “Where are you to be?” And Fanny, who at the time might be lodged in a hotel, or camping with a relative, or visiting at Tuxedo, or stopping in Westchester, never could tell. But by December the girl and her mother would find quarters somewhere and there remain until that acrobat, summer, turned handsprings into town and frightened them off.
Summer had not yet come. But May had and, with it, an eager glitter, skies of silk, all the caresses and surrenders of spring.
The season was becoming to Fanny. She fitted in it. She was a young Venus in Paris clothes—Aphrodite a maiden, touched up by Doucet. In her manner was a charm quite incandescent. In her voice were intonations that conveyed the sensation of a kiss. Her eyes were very loquacious. She could, when she chose, flood them with languors. She could, too, charge them with rebuke. You never knew, though, just what she would do. But you always did know what Sylvia would not.

===Chinese===
欧文广场是有「家」的。西尔维娅·沃尔德伦所住的那幢便是其中之一。那宅子在她家已传了六十年。在伦敦，如此长的居业司空见惯。在纽约，则可谓奇观。
对西尔维娅而言，这奇观是理所当然。令她惊奇的倒是旁人的迁徙。譬如范妮。每年秋天西尔维娅总问她：「你打算住哪儿？」而范妮——其时她也许住旅馆，也许寄身某亲戚处，也许在塔克西多（Tuxedo）做客，也许在韦斯特切斯特（Westchester）暂住——总是答不上来。可一到十二月，姑娘与母亲总会在某处觅得下处，便一直在那里住着，直到那个杂技演员——夏天——一个跟头翻进城里，把她们吓走。
夏天尚未来。可五月来了，随之一道来的，是热切的闪光、绸缎似的天空，以及春天所有的爱抚与委身。
这季节宜于范妮。她与它相得益彰。她是位身着巴黎时装的年轻的维纳斯——少女阿芙洛狄忒，经杜塞（Doucet）一手点染。她的仪态有一种近乎白炽的魅力。她的嗓音里有某种声调，传达出吻的意味。她的眼睛极善诉说。她高兴时，能让其中溢满慵懒；也能让其中蓄满呵责。不过你永远拿不准她接下来要做什么。可你永远拿得准西尔维娅不会做什么。

===Original===
Sylvia suggested the immateriality that the painters of long ago gave to certain figures which they wished to represent as floating from the canvas into space. You felt that her mind was clean as wholesome fruit, that her speech could no more weary than could a star, that her heart, like her house, was a home. She detained, but Fanny allured.
In spite of which or perhaps precisely because of their sheer dissimilarity the two girls were friends. But association weaves mysterious ties. It unites people who otherwise would come to blows. Fanny and Sylvia had been brought up together. The mesh woven in younger days was about them still, and on this particular noon in May they made a picture which, while contrasting, was charmful.
“You really like my hat?”
The hat was gray. The girl’s dress was gray. The skirt was of the variety known as trotabout. The whole thing was severely plain, yet astonishingly smart. Sylvia was also in street dress. The latter was black.

===Chinese===
西尔维娅令人想起古时的画家为某些人物所赋予的那种空灵——他们想让那些人物看上去正从画布上飘入太空。你会觉得她的心灵如卫生的果子般洁净，她的言语如星辰般不会叫人厌倦，她的心，也如她的宅子一般，是个「家」。她令人留步，而范妮引人追逐。
尽管如此——或许恰恰因为两人截然不同——这两个姑娘是朋友。可交往会织就神秘的纽带。它把那些否则会拳脚相向的人联结起来。范妮与西尔维娅是一同长大的。少时织就的网仍旧笼着她们，而在这五月里这个特定的正午，她们构成了一幅画面——虽则对照鲜明，却满是韵致。
「你当真喜欢我这帽子？」
那帽子是灰的。姑娘的衣裙是灰的。裙子是俗称「闲逛裙」的那一种。整一身极为素净，却惊人地俏挺。西尔维娅也着街装。她那身是黑的。

===Original===
They were in the parlor. For in New York there are still parlors. And why not? A parlor—or *parloir*—is a talking-place. Yet in this instance not a gay place. It was spacious, sombre, severe.
“And now,” said Fanny, after the hat had been properly praised, “tell me when it is to be?”
“When is what to be?”
“You and Arthur?”
“Next autumn.”
“I shall send a fish knife,” said Fanny, savorously. “A fish knife always looks so big and costs so little. Though if I could I would give you a diamond crown.”

===Chinese===
她们在客厅里。因为纽约还是有客厅的。这有何不可？客厅——或云 *parloir*——本是说话之所。然而眼下这一间却不怎么欢快。它宽敞、幽暗、肃穆。
「如今，」帽子被得体地夸过之后，范妮说，「告诉我，事在何时？」
「什么事在何时？」
「你与阿瑟。」
「明年秋天。」
「我要送你一把鱼刀，」范妮津津有味地说，「鱼刀这东西，看着总是很大，花钱总是很少。虽说我若能办到，倒想送你一顶钻石冠冕。」

===Original===
“Give me your promise to be bridesmaid, and you will have given me what I want from you most.”
“But what am I to wear? And oh, Sylvia, how am I to get it? I don’t dare any more to so much as look in on Annette, or Juliette, or Marguerite. There are streets into which I can no longer go. I told Loftus that, and he said—so sympathetically too—‘Ah, is it memories that prevent you?’ ‘Rubbish,’ I told him, ‘it’s bills.’ ”
“Fanny! How could you? He might have offered to pay them.”
“If he had only offered to owe them!” Fanny laughed as she spoke and patted her perfect skirt.
“But he has other fish to fry. Do you know the other day I saw him—”

===Chinese===
「你应承做我的伴娘，便是给了我我最想要的。」
「可我穿什么去呢？唉，西尔维娅，我哪弄去？我如今连安奈特（Annette）、或朱丽叶特（Juliette）、或玛格丽特（Marguerite）的店都不敢踏进一步了。有些街我是再也不能去了。我把这话对洛夫特斯说了，他——一副同情得很的模样——说：『啊，是回忆拦着你不成？』『胡说，』我对他说，『是账单。』」
「范妮！你怎么能这样？他没准会替你还的。」
「他要肯替我欠着就好了！」范妮说着笑起来，拍了拍她那完美的裙摆。
「可他另有鱼要煎。你知道前几天我看见他——」

===Original===
But what Fanny had seen was never told, or at least not then. Annandale was invading the parlor.
“Conquering hero!” cried Fanny. “I am here congratulating Sylvia.”
“I congratulate myself that you are. I have a motor at the door, and I propose to take you both to Sherry’s, afterward, if you like, to the races. There you may congratulate me.”
“What is this about Sherry’s?” Again the parlor was invaded. This time by Sylvia’s mother. She had bright cheeks, bright eyes, bright hair. In her voice was indulgence, in her manner ease. She gave a hand to Fanny, the other to Annandale.

===Chinese===
可范妮所见的，终未说出——至少当时未说。安南代尔正闯进客厅来。
「凯旋的英雄！」范妮叫道，「我正这儿祝贺西尔维娅呢。」
「你在这儿，我先自贺一番。门口我停了辆汽车，我打算带你们俩去谢里餐厅（Sherry's），之后若你们愿意，再去看赛马。在那儿，你们便可贺我了。」
「谢里餐厅是怎么一回事？」客厅再度被闯入。这次是西尔维娅的母亲。她双颊红润、双目明亮、头发鲜亮。她嗓音里是宽容，仪态里是从容。她把一只手伸给范妮，另一只伸给安南代尔。

===Original===
“In my day,” she resumed, “girls did not go lunching without someone to look after them.”
“They certainly did not go to Sherry’s,” said Annandale. “There was no Sherry’s to go to. But why won’t you come with us?”
“Thank you, Arthur. It is not because Fanny or Sylvia needs looking after. But when I was their age anything of the sort would have been thought so common. Yet then, what was common at that time seems to have been accepted since. Now, there is a chance to call me old-fashioned.”
“I can do better than that,” said Annandale, “I can call you grande dame.”
“Yes,” Fanny threw in, “and that, don’t you think, is so superior to being merely—ahem—demned grand.”

===Chinese===
「我年轻那会儿，」她续道，「姑娘们没有个照应的人，是不去外面用午餐的。」
「她们当然也不去谢里餐厅，」安南代尔说，「那会儿还没谢里餐厅呢。可您为何不愿与我们同去？」
「谢谢你，阿瑟。这并非因为范妮或西尔维娅需要人照应。只是我像她们这年纪时，这种事会被人看作太俗气。然而那时显得俗的，如今似乎已被接受了。好罢，这下你们可以叫我老古板了。」
「我能比这说得更妙，」安南代尔说，「我能叫您贵妇人（grande dame）。」
「可不，」范妮插嘴，「您不觉得，这可比仅仅——咳——「他奶奶的」气派要高明多么？」

===Original===
“Why, Fanny!” And Mrs. Waldron, at once amused at the jest and startled at the expression, shook her finger at her.
But Annandale hastened to her rescue. “Fanny is quite right, Mrs. Waldron. You meet women nowadays whose grandfathers, if they had any, were paving the streets while your own were governing the country and who, just because they happen to be beastly rich, put on airs that would be comical in an empress. Now, won’t you change your mind and come with us? At Sherry’s there are always some choice selections on view.”
“You are not very tempting, Arthur. But if the girls think otherwise, take them. And don’t forget. You dine with us tonight.”
Thereat, presently, after a scurry through sunshine and streets, Sherry’s was reached.

===Chinese===
「哎，范妮！」沃尔德伦太太（Mrs. Waldron）既觉这打趣好笑、又被这措辞吓一跳，便朝她摇了摇指头。
可安南代尔赶忙替她解围。「范妮说得一点不错，沃尔德伦太太。如今您尽遇着些女人，她们的祖父——若是有祖父的话——还在铺马路的时候，您的祖父们已在治理这个国家了；而她们只因为富得流油，便摆出一副连女皇摆了都显滑稽的架子。怎么样，您不改改主意，与我们同去？谢里餐厅里总陈列着几件精选的样品。」
「你这诱惑力可不怎么够，阿瑟。可姑娘们若另有想法，就带她们去罢。还有，别忘了。今晚你在我们家吃饭。」
于是，不久之后，一阵在阳光与街道间的疾行，谢里餐厅便到了。

===Original===
There Annandale wanted to order a châteaubriand. The girls rebelled. A maître d’hôtel suggested melons and a *suprême* with a bombe to follow.
Annandale turned to him severely. “Ferdinand, I object to your telling me what you want me to eat.”
“Let me order,” said Sylvia. “Fanny, what would you like?”
“Cucumbers, asparagus, strawberries.”
“Chicken?”
Fanny nodded.
“Yes,” said Annandale to the chastened waiter, “order that and some moselle, and I want a Scotch and soda. There’s Orr,” he interrupted himself to announce. “I wonder what he is doing uptown? And there’s Loftus.”

===Chinese===
在那里，安南代尔想点一份夏多布里昂牛排。姑娘们反对。一位领班提议甜瓜、一道 *suprême*（胸肉），再来一道冰淇淋盅。
安南代尔严肃地转向他。「费迪南（Ferdinand），我反对你来告诉我你想让我吃什么。」
「让我来点，」西尔维娅说，「范妮，你想要什么？」
「黄瓜、芦笋、草莓。」
「鸡肉？」
范妮点头。
「好，」安南代尔对受了挫的侍者说，「就点这些，再来点摩泽尔酒，我要一杯威士忌苏打。哟，奥尔在那儿，」他打断自己通报一声，「他进城北来干什么？还有洛夫特斯也在。」

===Original===
At the mention of Orr, Fanny, who had been eying an adjacent gown, evinced no interest. But at the mention of Loftus she glanced about the room.
It was large, high-ceiled, peopled with actresses and men-about-town, smart women and stupid boys, young girls and old beaux. From a balcony there dripped the twang of mandolins. In the air was the savor of pineapple, the smell of orris, the odor of food and flowers.
On entering Sylvia had stopped to say a word at one table, Fanny had loitered at another. Then in their trip to a table already reserved, a trip conducted by the maître d’hôtel whom Annandale had rebuked, murmurs trailed after them, the echo of their names, observations profoundly analytical. “That’s Fanny Price, the great beauty.” “That’s Miss Waldron, who is engaged to Arthur Annandale.” “That is Annandale there”—the usual subtleties of the small people of a big city. Now, at the entrance, Orr and Loftus appeared.

===Chinese===
一提到奥尔，方才正打量邻桌一件长袍的范妮，毫无反应。可一提到洛夫特斯，她便朝室内四下一顾。
那厅堂甚大、天花板甚高，满是女演员与城中纨绔、时兴女子与蠢笨少年、年轻姑娘与老风流浪子。一处露台上滴下曼陀林的琴声。空气里是菠萝的香、鸢尾根的气息、食物与花的气味。
进门时西尔维娅在一张桌子前停下说了句话，范妮在另一张桌前磨蹭。随后，在安南代尔申斥过的那位领班引领下，她们走向一张已留好的桌子，身后拖着一串窃语——她们名字的回响、些个入木三分的议论。「那是范妮·普莱斯，出名的大美人。」「那是沃尔德伦小姐，跟阿瑟·安南代尔订了婚的。」「那边那个就是安南代尔」——大城里小人物一向的精明。此刻，在入口处，奥尔与洛夫特斯出现了。

===Original===
“Shall I ask them to join us?” Annandale asked.
“Yes, do,” said Fanny. “I like Mr. Orr so much.”
But Loftus, who, with his hands in his pockets, a monocle in his eye, had been looking about with an air of great contempt for everybody, already with Orr was approaching. On reaching the table very little urging was required to induce them to sit, and, when seated they were, Loftus was next to Fanny.
“What are you doing uptown at this hour?” Annandale asked Orr, who had got between Loftus and Sylvia. “I thought you lawyers were all so infernally busy.”
“Everybody ought to be,” Orr replied. “Although an anarchist who had managed to get himself locked up, and whom I succeeded in getting out, confided to me that only imbeciles work. By way of exchange I had to confide to him that it is only imbeciles that do not.”

===Chinese===
「我请他们过来同坐？」安南代尔问。
「请罢，」范妮说，「我真喜欢奥尔先生。」
可洛夫特斯——双手插袋、单眼镜夹在眼里，正一脸对众人都极为不屑的神气四下张望——已与奥尔一道走来。到了桌前，几乎不消劝，他们便坐下了；落座之后，洛夫特斯挨着范妮。
「这个时辰你进城北来做什么？」安南代尔问奥尔——后者已挤到洛夫特斯与西尔维娅之间。「我以为你们律师个个都忙得要死。」
「人人都该忙，」奥尔回答，「虽说有个设法把自己关进去、又被我弄出来的无政府主义者，对我吐露说只有笨蛋才做工。作为交换，我只好对他吐露：只有笨蛋才不做工。」

===Original===
“Now that,” said Annandale, who had never done a stroke of work in his life, “is what I call a very dangerous theory.”
“A theory that is not dangerous,” Orr retorted, “can hardly be called a theory at all.”
With superior tact Sylvia intervened. “But what is anarchy, Melanchthon? Socialism I know about, but anarchy—?”
“To put it vulgarly, I drink and you pay.”
“But suppose I am an anarchist?”
“Then Sherry pays.”
“But supposing he is an anarchist?”
“Then there is a row. And there will be one. The country is drifting that way. It will, I think, be bloody, but I think, too, it will be brief. Anarchists, you know, maintain that of all prejudices capital and matrimony are the stupidest. What they demand is the free circulation of money and women. As a nation, we are great at entertaining, but we will never entertain that.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，」一辈子没做过一天活的安南代尔说，「我倒要称之为一种很危险的理论。」
「不危险的理论，」奥尔反驳，「简直算不得理论。」
西尔维娅以高超的得体插了进来。「可无政府主义究竟是什么，梅兰希吞？社会主义我懂，可无政府主义——？」
「说得俗一点：我喝酒，你付钱。」
「可我要是无政府主义者呢？」
「那就是谢里付钱。」
「可谢里要是无政府主义者呢？」
「那就要闹起来。而且会闹的。这国家正朝那方向漂。我想，那会流血，可我想，那也会短暂。要知道，无政府主义者主张，在所有偏见里头，资本与婚姻是最蠢的。他们所要求的是金钱与女子的自由流通。作为一个民族，我们擅长待客，可我们绝不会待见这一套。」

===Original===
“Why, then, did you not let the beggar rot where he was?” Annandale swiftly and severely inquired.
“Oh, you know, if I had not got him out someone else would have, and I thought it better that the circulation of money should proceed directly from his pocket to mine.”
“You haven’t any stupid prejudices yourself, that’s clear,” said Annandale, helping himself as he spoke to more Scotch. “Sylvia,” he continued, “if I am ever up for murder I will retain Melanchthon Orr.”
Orr laughed. “That retainer will never reach me. You would not hurt a fly.”
“Wouldn’t I?” And Annandale assumed an expression of great ferocity. “You don’t know me. I can imagine circumstances in which I could wade in gore. By the way, I have ordered a revolver.”

===Chinese===
「那你为何不让那家伙烂在里头？」安南代尔又快又严厉地问。
「唉，你知道，我若不弄他出来，别人也会弄，我想，金钱的流通还是直接从他的口袋流进我的口袋为好。」
「你自己显然没什么蠢偏见，」安南代尔边说边又给自己斟了些威士忌，「西尔维娅，」他续道，「我万一哪天因谋杀受审，就聘梅兰希吞·奥尔。」
奥尔笑起来。「那笔聘金永远到不了我手。你连只苍蝇都不肯伤。」
「我不肯？」安南代尔装出一脸凶相，「你不识得我。我想象得出某种情形下我能蹚着血水走。顺便说一句，我已定了一支左轮手枪。」

===Original===
“What!”
“Yes, a burglar got in my place the night before last and woke me up. If he comes back and wakes me up again I’ll blow his head off.”
Sylvia looked at him much as she might at a boastful child. “Yes, yes, Arthur, but please don’t take so much of that whisky.”
“I think I will have a drop of it, if I may,” said Loftus, who meanwhile had been talking to Fanny. In a moment he turned to her anew.
“Where are you going this summer?”
“To Narragansett. It is cool and cheap. Why don’t you come?”
“It is such a beastly hole.”

===Chinese===
「什么！」
「不错，前天夜里有个贼闯进我住处，把我吵醒了。他若再来、再把我吵醒，我就轰掉他的脑袋。」
西尔维娅看着他，如同看一个吹牛的孩子。「是、是，阿瑟，可请别喝那么多威士忌了。」
「我想我也来一点，可以么？」说这话的是洛夫特斯——他方才一直在与范妮说话。过了一会儿他又转向她。
「今年夏天你去哪里？」
「去纳拉甘西特（Narragansett）。那儿又凉快又便宜。你何不同去？」
「那是个糟糕透顶的窟窿。」

===Original===
“Well, perhaps. But do you think you would think so if I were there?”
“That would rather depend on how you treated me.”
“You mean, don’t you, that it would rather depend on how I let you treat me?” Fanny, as she spoke, looked Loftus in the eyes and made a face at him.
That face, Loftus, after a momentary interlude with knife and fork, tried to mimic. “If a chap gave you the chance you would drive him to the devil.”
On Fanny’s lips a smile bubbled. She shook her pretty head. “No, not half so far. Not even so far as the other end of Fifth Avenue, where I saw you trying to scrape acquaintance with that girl. Apropos. You might tell me. How are matters progressing? Has the castle capitulated?”
“I haven’t an idea what you are talking about.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，也许是。可你以为，要是有我在那里，你还会这样想么？」
「那要看你怎么待我了。」
「你的意思是，那要看我怎么让你待我，是罢？」范妮说这话时，直视洛夫特斯的眼睛，冲他做了个鬼脸。
那张脸，洛夫特斯在一阵刀叉的短暂间隙之后，试着模仿了一番。「哪个家伙若给了你这机会，你会把他逼到魔鬼那里去。」
范妮唇边冒出一个笑。她摇摇漂亮的头。「不，没那么远。连第五大道（Fifth Avenue）那一头都没那么远——在那儿我看见你跟那个姑娘搭讪。顺便说一句。你不妨告诉我。事情进展如何？那座城堡可曾投降？」
「我完全不懂你在说什么。」

===Original===
“That’s right. Assume a virtue though you have it not. It’s a good plan.”
“It does not appear to be yours.”
“Appearances may be deceptive.”
“And even may not be.”
Sylvia interrupted them. “What are you two quarreling about?”
“Mr. Loftus does not like my hat. Don’t you like it, Mr. Orr?”
“I like everything about you, everything, from the crown of your head to the soles of your feet.”
“There!” exclaimed Fanny. “That is the way I like to have a man talk.”

===Chinese===
「这就对了。纵使没有，也装出一副美德的样子。这是个好法子。」
「这法子显然不是你的。」
「表象或能骗人。」
「也或不骗人。」
西尔维娅打断他们。「你俩吵什么呢？」
「洛夫特斯先生不喜欢我的帽子。你喜欢么，奥尔先生？」
「你身上的一切我都喜欢，一切，从头顶到脚底。」
「瞧！」范妮叫起来，「我就爱听男人这样说话。」

===Original===
“It is dreadfully difficult,” Loftus threw in.
“You seem to find it so,” Fanny threw back.
Sylvia raised a finger. “Mr. Loftus, if you do not stop quarreling with Fanny I will make you come and sit by me.”
“If I am to look upon that as a punishment, Miss Waldron,” Loftus with negligent gallantry replied, “what would you have me regard as a reward?”
“Arthur! Arthur!” Fanny cried. “Did you hear that? This man is making up to Sylvia.”
But Annandale did not seem in the least alarmed. He was looking about for Ferdinand. “Here,” he began, when at last the waiter appeared. “You neglect us shamefully. We want some more moselle and more Scotch.”

===Chinese===
「那可难得很。」洛夫特斯插嘴。
「你似乎觉得难得很。」范妮回敬。
西尔维娅竖起一根指头。「洛夫特斯先生，你若再不住口与范妮吵，我就叫你过来坐我旁边。」
「我若把这当作惩罚，沃尔德伦小姐，」洛夫特斯以漫不经心的殷勤答道，「那您要我把什么当作奖赏呢？」
「阿瑟！阿瑟！」范妮叫道，「你听见没有？这人在向西尔维娅献殷勤呢。」
可安南代尔似乎一点也不慌。他正四下找费迪南。「这儿，」那侍者终于出现时，他开口道，「你把我们怠慢得不轻。我们还要些摩泽尔酒，还要威士忌。」

===Original===
“None for me,” said Loftus rising. “I have an appointment.”
“Appointment,” Fanny announced, “is very good English for *rendezvous*.”
“And *taisez-vous, mademoiselle*, is very good French for I wish it were with yourself.”
“I have not a doubt of it.”
“Fanny!” Sylvia objected. “You are impossible.”
“Yes,” Fanny indolently replied. “Yet then, to be impossible and seem the reverse is the proper caper for a debutante. Heigho! I wish girls smoked here. I would give a little of my small change for a cigarette. Are you really off, Royal. Well, my love to the lady.”

===Chinese===
「我不要了，」洛夫特斯起身道，「我有约会。」
「『约会』，」范妮宣称，「是 *rendezvous* 很地道的英文。」
「而 *taisez-vous, mademoiselle*（小姐请闭嘴），是『但愿是跟你约会』很地道的法文。」
「这我毫不怀疑。」
「范妮！」西尔维娅抗议，「你真叫人受不了。」
「是，」范妮懒洋洋地答，「不过话说回来，做叫人受不了之人、却显得恰恰相反，正是一个初入社交场姑娘该玩的花样。嘿嗬！我真希望这里的姑娘也抽烟。我愿拿我的一点零钱换一支烟。你当真要走么，罗伊尔。好吧，替我向那位女士问好。」

## III The Ex-First Lady / III 昔日首席女伶

===Original===
Loftus, letting himself into a hansom, sailed away. At Morris Park that afternoon there were to be races, and up the maelstrom of Fifth Avenue came scudding motors, fleeting traps.
As the hansom descended the current Loftus nodded to this acquaintance and to that, occasionally raising his hat as women smiled and bowed. Occasionally, too, he contemplated what he could of himself in the little mirror at the side of the cab. He looked triumphant and treacherous.
Fanny, he reflected, was ideal. But exacting, ambitious even. She had a perfect mania for matrimony. There was another girl that he had in mind whom he fancied more reasonable. This other was Marie Durand.
In just what way he had met her was never quite clear. Fanny, who had witnessed the preliminary skirmish, always believed that he had picked her up. Afterward, at the time of the trial, it was so reported. The report was false in addition to being vulgar. Marie Durand was not of that sort. There was nothing fast or flirtatious about her. But she was a human being. She had eyes. She had a heart. By nature she was sensitive. Moreover, she was but nineteen. Being human, sensitive, and not very old, having eyes to see and a heart that throbbed, she was impressionable and, to her misfortune, Loftus impressed her.

===Chinese===
洛夫特斯钻进一辆双轮马车，扬长而去。那天下午莫里斯公园（Morris Park）有赛马，第五大道这道漩涡里，汽车飞驰、轻便马车疾掠。
马车顺流而下，洛夫特斯朝这个点头、朝那个颔首，遇有女子含笑致意便偶尔举帽回礼。偶尔，他也端详马车侧镜里所能照见的自己。他模样得意洋洋，又透着狡黠。
范妮，他寻思，是理想的。只是苛求，甚至野心勃勃。她对婚姻有一种十足的痴狂。他心中另有一个姑娘，他觉得更通情达理些。这另一个便是玛丽·杜朗。
他究竟是怎么结识她的，从未完全清楚。目睹了那场前哨战的范妮，一向认定他是搭讪来的。后来审判之时，也是这么传的。这传闻既俗又假。玛丽·杜朗不是那一类人。她身上毫无轻佻或卖弄之处。可她是个活人。她有眼。她有心。她天生敏感。再者，她不过十九岁。既是人、又敏感、又不算大，有眼能视、有心能搏，她便容易动情，而不幸的是，打动她的正是洛夫特斯。

===Original===
Loftus was rather used to impressing people. He saw the girl on Fifth Avenue, followed her home, learned her name—or thought he did—and sent her flowers every day until he saw her again, when he presumed to accost her. At that impertinence Marie tilted her nose and trotted on, distant, disdainful, demure.
But not indifferent. Not oblivious either. Often she had seen him. Occasionally on a high drag behind a piebald four-in-hand. In crowded Fifth Avenue, drags, with or without piebalds, are infrequent. This drag Marie had seen not merely tooling along the street but pictured in the press. With, of course, full accounts of the driver. As a consequence she knew who he was, knew that he was one of the rich young men of New York and that he moved and had his being in the upper circles.

===Chinese===
洛夫特斯对打动旁人可谓习以为常。他在第五大道上看见那姑娘，尾随她回家，打听到她的名字——或自以为打听到了——便每日送花给她，直到再度见她，那时他竟冒失地搭起讪来。面对这放肆，玛丽翘起鼻子，继续前行，疏远、矜持、端庄。
可并非无动于衷。也并非全无觉察。她常常看见他。偶尔见他驾着一辆高座四马马车，驭着四匹花斑马。在熙攘的第五大道，四马马车——花斑的也罢、不花斑的也罢——是少见的。这辆马车玛丽不仅见过在街上辚辚而行，也见过报上刊的画。自然，附有御者的详尽介绍。故而她知道他是谁，知道他是纽约的富家子弟之一，知道他在最上层的圈子里营生度日。

===Original===
Marie’s own sphere of life was obscure. She lived with her father in Gay Street. Her father, a tailor by trade, was a naturalized Frenchman, a gaunt Gaul, who had a sallow face, walked with a stoop, complained of his heart and adored his daughter. To him she was a pearl, a *perle*, rather. For though he had been long in New York and spoke English well, he had never quite acquired the accent.
Marie spoke English without any accent whatever. She also spoke French, sang in it, too, sang in Italian and, with a view to the lyric stage, or, more exactly, with the hope of studying for it abroad, was, at the time when this drama begins, taking lessons in what is termed the *bel canto*.

===Chinese===
玛丽自己的生活圈子则颇为幽暗。她与父亲同住盖伊街（Gay Street）。她父亲以裁缝为业，是个入了美籍的法国人，一个瘦削的高卢人，面有菜色，伛偻而行，常抱怨自己心脏不好，却钟爱女儿如命。在他眼里她是一颗珍珠——更确切地说，一颗 *perle*。因他虽在纽约住了许久、英语也讲得不错，口音却始终改不干净。
玛丽讲英语不带任何口音。她也讲法语，也用法语唱歌，也用意大利语唱歌；她志在抒情舞台，或更确切地说，盼着能出国为之深造，故而在这出戏开场之时，正学着那叫作 *bel canto*（美声唱法）的东西。

===Original===
But her aspirations, in so far as they concerned Europe, her father was unable to gratify. He could not let her go alone and he could not afford to throw up what he called his beesness. Here, then, was this girl, pretty as a picture, with a lovely contralto voice, with aspirations entirely worldly, with wings, you might say, cooped in Gay Street, spiritually and mentally starved there.
Gay Street lies back of Jefferson Market. In shape a crescent, it curves briefly in a lost and dismal way through a region which, though but a block or two from Fifth Avenue, is almost squalid. At one end of its short curve is a saloon, at the other an apothecary.
It was from this apothecary that Loftus learned Marie’s name—or thought he did. For inadvertently the man got things mixed as his drugs and supplied Loftus with the name of a young woman who lived in a house next to the one in which Loftus had seen the girl enter.

===Chinese===
然而她那些志向中涉欧的部分，她父亲无力满足。他不能让她独自去，也舍不得丢下他所谓的「毕生事业」。于是便有这么一个姑娘，美如图画，有一副可人的女低音嗓子，志向完完全全是世俗的，可谓长了翅膀，却被困在盖伊街，在那里精神上、心智上都饥渴着。
盖伊街藏在杰斐逊市场（Jefferson Market）背后。形如弯月，以一种失落而阴郁的姿态短短地弯过一片区域——那地方虽离第五大道不过一两个街区，却近乎破败。它那道短弯的一头是家酒馆，另一头是家药铺。
洛夫特斯便是从这药铺打听到玛丽的名字——或自以为打听到了。因为那掌柜像混淆自家药一般把事情搅混了，给洛夫特斯报了一个姑娘的名字，那姑娘住的宅子，与洛夫特斯亲眼看见那女孩进去的那幢紧挨着。

===Original===
What is more interesting is the fact that, though, while he was following her there, she had looked neither to the right nor to the left, or anywhere save straight ahead, she had been fully aware that he was behind her. How? We cannot tell. It is one of the mysteries of femininity. But once safely in, boldly she peeked out. Loftus was crossing the street. Presently he entered the shop. For what, it did not take Marie more than a minute to conjecture.
Later in the day a motor van appeared in that street. On it was the name of a Broadway florist. Since the memory of man never before had such a thing happened. From the van a groom had hopped and, if you please, with roses. That, too, was phenomenal. Yet thereafter every day for a week there was the motor, the groom and flowers at a dollar and a half apiece. The recipient of these attentions was Miss Rebecca Cohen, the daughter of Mr. Abraham Cohen, who also, like Marie’s father, was a tailor.

===Chinese===
更有意思的是这一桩：当他尾随她至此之时，她既不左顾、也不右盼，目不旁视，只朝前看，可她其实一清二楚他在身后。怎么知道的？说不上来。这是女性诸般神秘之一。可她一进了门，便大胆地往外偷瞧。洛夫特斯正穿过街。不久他进了那家铺子。所为何来，玛丽不消一分钟便猜中了。
当日稍晚，那条街上出现了一辆送货汽车。车身上印着百老汇（Broadway）一家花店的名号。自有记忆以来，这等事从未在那街发生过。一个马夫从车上跳下，您瞧，带着玫瑰。这也堪称奇观。然而此后整整一星期，每天都是那汽车、那马夫、那一支一块半的花。这些殷勤的受主，是丽贝卡·科恩（Rebecca Cohen）小姐，亚伯拉罕·科恩（Abraham Cohen）先生的千金——这位科恩先生与玛丽的父亲一样，也是个裁缝。

===Original===
Marie saw the van, divined the mistake, and, being as full of fun as a kitten, greatly enjoyed the continued humor of it. For still into that sordid street the flowers poured. Every day, to the unhallowed surprise of Mr. Cohen and to the equal bewilderment of his offspring, a box of radiant roses was handed out.
In that surprise and bewilderment the neighborhood joined. Scandalized at the scandal Cohen questioned the groom, questioned the chauffeur. He might have saved himself the trouble. Then he inquired at the florist’s. But there no one could be found who knew anything at all about anything whatever. Already he had questioned Rebecca. It seemed to him that in spite of her protests she must be engaged in some fathomless intrigue. But Rebecca, whose commercial instinct was beautifully developed, not only protested but appeased. She told her father that the roses were worth money. Furthermore, that which is worth money can be sold. Thereupon sold they were. But quite as inexplicably as the van had appeared so did its visits cease. When that happened Mr. Cohen felt and declared that he was robbed. He had come to regard the roses as assets.

===Chinese===
玛丽看见那车，猜出了这错，便如小猫般满是顽皮，对这持续不断的滑稽津津乐道。因那些花仍源源不绝地涌进那条寒酸的街。每天，叫科恩先生莫名其妙地吃惊、叫他女儿同样地摸不着头脑的，是一盒光彩照人的玫瑰被送了出来。
左邻右舍也加入了这场惊奇与困惑。对这桩丑闻深感愤慨的科恩，盘问马夫、盘问司机。他本可省了这番事。随后他到花店去查问。可那里找不到一个人对任何事晓得半点。他早已盘问过丽贝卡。他觉得，任她如何否认，她也必是卷入了什么深不可测的风月案。可丽贝卡——她的商业本能发育得极美——不仅否认，还安抚父亲。她告诉父亲，这些玫瑰值钱。再者，值钱的东西便能卖。于是便卖了。可那车之消失，与它之出现同样地莫明其妙。待它不再来，科恩先生觉得自己遭了劫。他已把那些玫瑰当作了家产。

===Original===
Marie meanwhile, whom the humor of the situation had amused, ended by worrying over it. She was a good girl, as such conscientious, and it troubled her, at first only a little and then very much, to think that Loftus must believe that she was knowingly accepting his flowers. Moreover, her father had commented upon them; in commenting he had wondered. Marie began to fear that Loftus might discover the mistake and turn in and inundate her. She did not know quite what to do. She thought of writing to him, very distantly, in the third person, or else anonymously. But the letter did not seem to get itself framed. Then, from thinking of that, she fell to thinking of him.
To see him she had only to close her eyes. Once he visited her in dream. He came accompanied by butterflies that fluttered about her and changed into kisses on her lips. Again she fancied him much sought after by ladies and became hotly and unaccountably vexed at the idea. It would be so lovely to really know him, she always decided. But she did not see at all how that ever could come about.
Yet, of course, it did come about. It came about, moreover, in a fashion as sordid as the street she lived in.

===Chinese===
玛丽这边，起初被这局面的滑稽逗乐，末了倒为它发起愁来。她是个好姑娘，凡事有良心，一想到洛夫特斯必以为她是心甘情愿地收他的花，便觉不安——起初只一点点，后来很厉害。再者，她父亲也说起过这些花；说起之时也犯过疑。玛丽开始怕洛夫特斯终会发现这差错、转而把花倾泻到她头上来。她不知该如何是好。她想写信给他，极冷淡地、用第三人称，要不就匿名。可这信怎么也写不成。随后，从想这件事，她落到想他这个人上头去。
要见他，她只消闭上眼。有一回他在梦中造访她。他来时伴有蝴蝶，蝴蝶在她身畔翩跹，化作她唇上的吻。又有一回，她想象他颇受女士们追逐，想到这念头便无端地火起来。要真能结识他该多好，她总这样想。可她全然看不出这事怎么可能发生。
然而，它当然还是发生了。而且，发生得与她所住的那条街同样地寒碜。

===Original===
That street, though sordid, is relatively silent. It is beyond, in Sixth Avenue, that you get a sample of real New York noise. The slam-bang of the trains overhead, the grinding grunt of the surface cars, the demon draymen, the clanging motors, the ceaseless crowds, collaborate in an uproar beside which a bombardment is restful. But though the entire thoroughfare is appalling, Jefferson Market, behind which Gay Street squats, is infernal.
Loftus loathed it. Until he pursued the girl into its horrors never before had he been there. Nor, save for her, would he have returned. But return he did. For recompense he beheld her. She was strolling along, a roll of music under her arm, in the direction of Fifth Avenue.
It was there he attempted to accost her. Without deigning to seem even aware that he had presumed to do so, she passed on and, in passing, turned into Washington Square, where, ascending the steps of a house, she vanished. It was then three by the clock of a beautiful day in April.

===Chinese===
那条街虽寒碜，却相对安静。真正的纽约喧嚣样本，要到那边第六大道（Sixth Avenue）去领教。头顶火车的哐啷、地面电车磨牙般的哼、恶魔似的赶车夫、叮当作响的汽车、川流不息的人群，合力造出一片喧嚣，相比之下连炮轰都显得安谧。可整条通衢虽骇人，盖伊街所蹲踞其后的杰斐逊市场，则简直是地狱。
洛夫特斯深恶之。直至他追那姑娘追进它的恐怖里，他从未到过这里。若不是为她，他也不会再来。可他还是来了。作为报偿，他望见了她。她正踽踽而行，腋下夹着一卷乐谱，朝第五大道方向去。
他便在那里试图搭讪。她不屑得连像是察觉了他的冒失都不屑装，径自走过去，走时拐进了华盛顿广场（Washington Square），登上某宅的台阶，便消失了。其时四月里一个美景之日，钟上正三点。

===Original===
Loftus was as well able as another to put two and two together. He knew that young girls do not stroll about with a music roll under their arm for the fun of it. A music roll predicates lessons, and there where lessons are must also be a teacher.
From that teacher he was unaware of any good and valid reason why he should not himself take lessons. But fate is not unrelenting. Of such toil he was spared. He spared himself too any further toil that day. He felt that he had done enough. He had quarried the girl again, stalked her to what was obviously a boardinghouse. He turned on his heel.
The next day he was back at that house, inquiring at the door. As a result he was shown into a shabby back parlor where he made the acquaintance of Mme. Machin, a tired old Frenchwoman, who, with rouge on her yellow cheeks, powder on her pointed nose, confided to him that she had been prima donna, though whether *assoluta* or *dissoluta* she omitted to state.

===Chinese===
洛夫特斯和别人一样会顺藤摸瓜。他知道年轻姑娘不会腋下夹卷乐谱逛街是为好玩。乐谱意味着功课，有功课之处必有老师。
他想不出有什么正当理由不能自己也去上这位老师的课。可命运并非全然无情。这类劳顿他被免了。那天他也免了自己再进一步的劳顿。他觉得已做得够多。他重新猎到了姑娘，跟踪她到了一处显然是供膳公寓的地方。他转身便走。
第二天他又回到那宅子，在门口打听。结果他被领进一间寒酸的后客厅，在那里结识了马尚夫人（Mme. Machin）——一个疲惫的法国老妇人，黄脸颊上搽着胭脂、尖鼻子上扑着粉，向他吐露自己从前是首席女伶，只是究竟是 *assoluta*（无二的）还是 *dissoluta*（放荡的），她未加说明。

===Original===
But her antecedents, her possibilities as well, Loftus divined at a glance and, while he was at it, divining too, that, personally, she was no better, and, financially, no better off than the law allows, asked point-blank about the Miss Cohen who had come there at three the day before. Learning then from the ex-first lady that the girl’s name was not Cohen but Durand, he damned the apothecary and offered a hundred dollars to be introduced. Poverty is not a crime. But it is rumored to be an incentive. The crime which Loftus proposed to Mme. Machin is one which the code does not specify and the law cannot reach. Knowing which, the woman may have been guilty of it before and, the opportunity occurring, was guilty again—salving her conscience, if she had a conscience, with the convenient, “*Mon Dieu, il faut vivre!*”
Anyway, at the offer she did not so much as blink. She smiled very receptively and declared that she would be charmed.
When, therefore, two days later Marie reentered that shabby back parlor she found Loftus there. Generally the girl and the ex-first lady got to work at once, sometimes with the *brindisi* from *Lucrezia Borgia*, sometimes with arias from *Aïda*. Save themselves no one was ever present.

===Chinese===
她的来历与可能，洛夫特斯一眼便看穿；既在看穿，又看出她论人品不见得好、论财力也不过法律所许的那点，便直截了当地问起前一天三点钟来的那位科恩小姐。从这位昔日首席女伶口中得知那姑娘不姓科恩、而姓杜朗，他便咒了那药铺掌柜一顿，出一百块求引见。贫穷不是罪。可据说它是种激励。洛夫特斯向马尚夫人所提议的那桩罪，是法典未载、法律难及的。既知此，这女人从前许已犯过，如今机会一来，便又犯了——若她还有良心，便以一句现成话抚慰之：「*Mon Dieu, il faut vivre!*」（我的天，总要活命啊！）
无论如何，听了这出价，她眼皮也没眨一下。她极有接受之意地笑了笑，宣称自己再乐意不过。
于是，两天之后玛丽再走进那间寒酸的后客厅时，发现洛夫特斯竟在那里。寻常姑娘与这位昔日首席女伶一上来便开工，有时唱《卢克雷齐亚·博尔贾》（Lucrezia Borgia）里的祝酒歌，有时唱《阿依达》（Aïda）里的咏叹调。除她俩之外，从不曾有旁人在场。

===Original===
Now at the unexpected spectacle of the man the cream of the girl’s delicate skin suffused. It was as though there were claret in it. She had not an idea what to do and, before she could decide, ceremoniously, with due regard for the pomps of etiquette, Loftus had been introduced.
If abrupt, the introduction was at least conventional, and Marie, who had not the remotest suspicion that it was all bought and paid for and who, if consciously startled, subconsciously was pleased, attributing the whole thing to accident and, flushing still, smiled and sat down.
“I think,” said Loftus, “that I have had the pleasure of seeing you before.”
At this inanity Marie looked first at him, then at the carpet. She did not know at all what he was saying. But in his voice was a deference, in his manner a sorcery and in his bearing and appearance something that went to her head. It was all very novel and delightful, and she flushed again.

===Chinese===
此刻，这男人出乎意料地出现在眼前，姑娘那细腻肌肤的奶白一下子泛了红。仿佛里头掺了红酒。她全然不知如何是好，未及拿定主意，洛夫特斯已被郑重其事地、依足了礼数的繁文缛节，介绍给了她。
这引见虽突兀，至少合规矩；而玛丽丝毫未疑这全是花钱买来的，又明明一惊、暗暗一喜，把整件事都归于巧合，便仍红着脸，笑了笑，坐下了。
「我想，」洛夫特斯说，「我曾有幸见过您。」
听了这句无谓的话，玛丽先看他、再看地毯。她全然不懂他在说什么。可他的嗓音里有敬意，他的仪态里有妖术，他的风度与容貌里有某种直冲她脑门的东西。这一切都很新奇、很愉快，她又红了脸。

===Original===
“Yes,” Loftus resumed, “and when I did see you I committed a very grave offense. Can you forgive me?”
For countenance sake the girl turned to Mme. Machin. But the ex-first lady, pretexting a pretext, had gone.
“Can you?” Loftus requested. “Can you forgive?”
Forgive indeed! Had she not so forgiven that she had almost wished a renewal of that grave offense? She did not answer. It was her face that spoke for her. But the silence Loftus affected to misconstrue.
“Couldn’t you try?”
“Yes.” The monosyllable fell from her softly, almost inaudibly. Yet for his purpose it sufficed.
“Thank you. I hoped that you would. But will you let me tell you now how I came to behave as I did?”
To this, timorously, with the slightest movement of her pretty head, the girl assented.

===Chinese===
「不错，」洛夫特斯续道，「而我见到您之时，犯了一桩很重的罪过。您能原谅我么？」
为着颜面，姑娘转向马尚夫人。可这位昔日首席女伶，托了个故，已经走了。
「您能么？」洛夫特斯央道，「您能原谅么？」
原谅！她岂不已经原谅到几乎盼望那重罪再犯一次了么？她没答。是她那张脸替她答了。可洛夫特斯佯装误解了她的沉默。
「您不能试试么？」
「能。」这一字音轻轻落下，几不可闻。于他的目的而言却已够了。
「谢谢您。我原就盼着您会的。可您现在愿不愿让我告诉您，我当初怎会那样行事？」
对此，姑娘怯怯地、把那漂亮的头微微一动，应允了。

===Original===
“Because I could not help myself. Because at the first sight of you I knew that I loved you. Because I felt that I could never love anyone else.”
Marie started. She was crimson. Starting, she half got from her seat. Loftus caught at her hand. She disengaged it. But he caught at it again.
“I love you,” he continued, burning her with his words, with the contact of his fingers, that had intertwisted with hers. “Look at me, I love your eyes. Speak to me, I love your voice.”
But the door opened. Preceded by a precautionary roulade, the ex-first lady reappeared.
“*Allons!*” she remarked to the ceiling. “*Et maintenant, mademoiselle, au travail.*”

===Chinese===
「因为我情不自禁。因为一见您我便知我爱您。因为我觉着我再也爱不了旁人。」
玛丽一惊。她满脸通红。惊着便半从座上起。洛夫特斯去抓她的手。她挣脱了。可他又抓了。
「我爱你，」他续道，以他的言语、以他那与她十指交缠的指头的接触灼烧着她，「看着我，我爱你的眼睛。对我说话，我爱你的声音。」
可门开了。那位昔日首席女伶在一串未雨绸缪的华彩之前先行出现。
「*Allons!*（来罢！）」她冲着天花板说，「*Et maintenant, mademoiselle, au travail.*（现在，小姐，开工了。）」

===Original===
Loftus stood up, took Marie’s hand again, held it a second, nodded at the woman. In a moment he had gone.
“*Au revoir*,” the ex-first lady called after him. She turned to the girl. “A gallant monsieur. And good to look at.” Then seating herself at the piano she attacked the *brindisi* from *Lucrezia*. “Ah! the *segreto*!” she interrupted herself to exclaim, “*il segreto per esser felice*—the secret of happiness! *Mais!* There is but one! *C’est l’amour!* And with a gallant monsieur like that! And rich! *C’est le rêve! N’est ce pas, mon enfant?*”
“*Je vous en prie, madame*,” said Marie severely, or rather as severely as she could, for she was trembling with emotion, saturated with the love that had been thrown at her head, drenched with it, frightened too at the apperception of the secret which the aria that her teacher was strumming revealed.

===Chinese===
洛夫特斯起身，又握了玛丽的手，握了一秒，朝那妇人点点头。转眼他便走了。
「*Au revoir*（再会），」昔日首席女伶在他身后唤道。她转向姑娘。「一位多情的先生。长得又好。」随即她在钢琴前坐下，弹起了《卢克雷齐亚》里的祝酒歌。「啊！那个 *segreto*（秘密）！」她打断自己叫道，「*il segreto per esser felice*——幸福的秘密！*Mais!*（可是！）只有一个！*C’est l’amour!*（那就是爱！）还是跟那样一位多情的先生！又有钱！*C’est le rêve! N’est ce pas, mon enfant?*（这简直是梦！可不是么，我的孩子？）」
「*Je vous en prie, madame*（我求您，夫人），」玛丽肃然说——或者说尽力肃然地说——因她正激动得发抖，被那劈头盖脸砸向她的爱所浸透、所淋透，又为她老师信手弹出的那支咏叹调所揭示的秘密之觉察而心惊。

## IV Enchantment / IV 着魔

===Original===
Sailing in the hansom down Fifth Avenue, Loftus thought of that first interview with the girl, of the den in which it had occurred and of his subsequent visits there. Since the introduction he had seen her three times, seen, too, of course, that she was not up to Fanny, but he had seen also that she was less ambitious, more tractable in every way. Besides, one is not loved every afternoon. To him that was the main point, and of that point he was now tolerably sure.
Suddenly the hansom tacked, veered and landed him at the ex-first lady’s door.
“*Bonjour, mon beau seigneur*,” the woman began when, presently, he reached her lair. “The little one will not delay.”
“And then?”
“Be tranquil. I have other cats to whip.”

===Chinese===
乘着双轮马车顺第五大道而下，洛夫特斯想着与那姑娘的头一回会面，想着会面所在的那间窟穴，以及他此后在那里的几次造访。自那次引见之后他见过她三次，自然也看出她比不上范妮，可他也看出她野心较小、处处更易驾驭。再者，人并不是每天下午都被爱的。这于他才是要紧之处，而这一点他如今已相当有把握。
忽然马车一拐、一转，便把他送到了昔日首席女伶的门前。
「*Bonjour, mon beau seigneur*（日安，我英俊的老爷），」他不久走进她的窟穴时，这女人开口道，「那小丫头不会耽搁的。」
「然后呢？」
「放心。我还有别的猫要鞭。」
（译注：法文「别的猫要鞭」意为「另有要事」。）

===Original===
Mme. Machin was hatted and gloved. Loftus stuck his hand in his pocket. Mme. Machin was too genteel to notice. From the pocket he drew a roll of yellow bills. Mme. Machin affected entire unconcern. The bills he put in her paw. Mme. Machin was so entirely unconscious of the liberty that she turned to the mantel, picked up a bag of bead, opened it, took from it a little puff with which she dusted her nose. Then the puff went back into the bag. With it went the bills.
“I run,” she announced. She moved to the door. There, looking at Loftus over her shoulder, she stopped. “You come again?”
For reply Loftus made a gesture.
“Yes,” said the woman. “Naturally. It depends. But let me know. It is more commodious. *Pas de scandale, eh?*”
To this Loftus made no reply whatever. But his expression was translatable into “what do you take me for?”
“*Allez!*” the ex-first lady resumed. “I have confidence.”

===Chinese===
马尚夫人已戴好帽、戴好手套。洛夫特斯把手伸进口袋。马尚夫人太文雅了，竟未留意。他从口袋里抽出一卷黄澄澄的钞票。马尚夫人装作全然漠然。他把钞票塞进她的爪子。马尚夫人对这放肆浑然不觉，转身走向壁炉，拾起一只珠串包，打开它，从中取出一小团粉扑，掸了掸鼻子。随后粉扑回到包里。与它一道回去的还有那些钞票。
「我走啦。」她宣称。她走向门。在那里，她越过肩头望着洛夫特斯，停下脚步。「您还来？」
作为回答，洛夫特斯做了个手势。
「是，」这女人说，「自然。看情况。不过先告诉我一声。那样方便些。*Pas de scandale, eh?*（不惹风波，嗯？）」
对此洛夫特斯半个字也没答。但他的表情译出来便是：「你把我当什么人？」
「*Allez!*（去罢！）」昔日首席女伶续道，「我信得过您。」

===Original===
She opened the door and through it vanished. Loftus removed his gloves, seated himself at the piano, ran his fingers over the keys, struck a note which suggested another and attacked the waltz from *Faust*. The appropriateness of it appealed to him. As he played he hummed. Then, passing upward with the score, he reached the “Salve Dimora,” Faust’s salute to Marguerite’s home. But in the den where he sat the aria did not fit. He went back again to the waltz. Then, precisely as on the stage Marguerite appears, Marie entered.
Loftus jumped up, went to her, took her hand. It was trembling. He led her to a sofa, seating himself at her side, her hand still in his.

===Chinese===
她开门，从门里消失了。洛夫特斯脱下手套，在钢琴前坐下，手指从琴键上掠过，敲出一个音符、引出另一个，便弹起了《浮士德》（Faust）的圆舞曲。它的应景叫他得意。他弹着，嘴里也哼着。随后，随乐谱上行，他弹到了「Salve Dimora（万福，居所）」——浮士德向玛格丽特之家的致意。可在他所坐的这间窟穴里，这支咏叹调并不相宜。他又回到圆舞曲上去。于是，正像台上玛格丽特出场的那一刻，玛丽走了进来。
洛夫特斯跳起来，走到她跟前，握住她的手。那手在颤抖。他把她引到一张沙发前，在她身旁坐下，她的手仍在他手里。

===Original===
He looked at her. She had the prettiness and timidity of a kitten, a kitten’s grace as well. Like a kitten, she could not have been vulgar or awkward had she tried. But association and environment had wrapped about her one of the invisible yet obvious mantles that differentiate class from class. Loftus was quite aware of that. He was, though, equally aware that love is a famous costumer. There are few mantles that it cannot remove and remake. That the girl loved him he knew. The tremor of her hand assured him more surely than words.
None the less he asked her. It seemed to him only civil. But she did not answer. The dinginess of the den oppressed him. It occurred to him that it might be oppressing her. Again he inquired. Only the tremor of the hand replied.
“Tell me,” he repeated.

===Chinese===
他看着她。她有如小猫般的俏丽与怯懦，也有小猫的优雅。像小猫一样，她就是想俗气、想笨拙也办不到。可交往与环境，已在她身上裹了一件看不见却显眼的斗篷——那种把阶级与阶级区分开来的斗篷。洛夫特斯十分清楚这一点。可他同样清楚，爱情是个有名的裁缝。没有几件斗篷是它脱不下、改不成的。这姑娘爱他，他知道。她手的颤抖比言语更确凿地向他保证了这一点。
尽管如此，他还是问她。他觉得这才是客气。可她不答。这窟穴的破败压迫着他。他忽然想到它也许也压迫着她。他又问。回答他的只有那手的颤抖。
「告诉我。」他重复。

===Original===
The girl disengaged her hand. She looked down and away.
“Won’t you?” he insisted.
“I ought not to,” she said at last.
“But why?”
With her parasol the girl poked at the carpet. “Because it is not right. It is not right that I should.” But at once, with a little convulsive intake of the breath, she added, “Yet I do.”
Then it seemed to her that the room was turning around, that the walls had receded, that there was but blankness. His lips were on hers. In their contact everything ceased to be save the consciousness of something so poignant, so new, that to still the pain of the joy of it she struggled to be free.

===Chinese===
姑娘抽回手。她低着头，望向别处。
「你不肯么？」他坚持。
「我不该。」她终于说。
「可为什么？」
姑娘用阳伞拨弄着地毯。「因为这不合宜。我不该合宜。」可随即，她一阵痉挛般地吸了口气，补道，「然而我肯。」
随即她只觉屋子在打转、墙壁在退去、四围一片空白。他的唇已压上她的唇。在那接触里，一切都消失了，唯独剩下对某种如此锥心、如此新鲜之物的觉知——为平息那喜悦所带来的痛，她挣扎着想脱身。

===Original===
Kissing her again Loftus let her go. Dizzily she got from the sofa. The parasol had fallen. Her hat was awry. To straighten it she moved to a mirror. Her face was scarlet. Instantly fear possessed her, fear not of him but of herself. With uncertain fingers she tried to adjust the hat.
“I must go.”
But Loftus came to her. Bending a bit he whispered in her ear: “Don’t go—don’t go ever.”
Do what she might she could not manage with her hat. In the glass it was no longer that which she saw, nor her face, but an abyss, suddenly precipitate, that had opened there.

===Chinese===
又吻了她一次，洛夫特斯放开她。她晕眩地从沙发上起身。阳伞掉了。帽子歪了。她走到镜前去正它。她满脸通红。恐惧立时攫住了她——不是怕他，而是怕她自己。她用不稳的手指去扶那帽子。
「我得走了。」
可洛夫特斯朝她走来。微微俯身，在她耳畔低语：「别走——永远别走。」
她怎么弄也弄不好那帽子。镜子里她看见的已不再是帽子、不再是她的脸，而是一道深渊——骤然陡峭、就在那里张开的深渊。

===Original===
“No, don’t go,” Loftus was saying. “I love you and you love me.”
It was, though, not love that was emotionalizing her then. It was fear. A fear of that abyss and of the lower depths beneath.
“Don’t go,” Loftus reiterated. “Don’t, that is, if you do love me; and if you do, tell me, will you be my wife?”
At this, before her, in abrupt enchantment, the abyss disappeared. Where its depths had been were parterres of gems, slopes of asphodel, the gleam and brilliance of the gates of paradise.
“Your wife!” The wonder of it was in her voice and marveling eyes.

===Chinese===
「不，别走，」洛夫特斯正说着，「我爱你，你也爱我。」
然而其时使她激动的并非爱。是恐惧。对那深渊、对深渊之下更深处的一种恐惧。
「别走，」洛夫特斯重申，「也就是说，你若真爱我，就别走；你若真爱，就告诉我，你愿做我的妻子么？」
闻此，在她眼前，猝然的一阵着魔里，深渊消失了。原来深渊之处，成了宝石的花坛、常春花的斜坡、乐园之门那熠熠的辉光。
「你的妻子！」她声音里、惊异的眼里满是这不可思议。

===Original===
“Come.” Taking her hand, Loftus led her to their former seat.
“But—”
“But what?”
“How can I be your wife? I am nobody.”
“You are perfect. There is only one thing I fear—” Loftus hesitated. Nervously the girl looked at him.
“Only one,” he continued. “I am not and never shall be half good enough for you.”
“Oh!”
“Never half enough.”
“Oh! How can you say that? It is not true. Could I care for you if it were?”
“And you do?”
“Don’t you know it?”

===Chinese===
「来。」洛夫特斯握住她的手，把她引回方才的座上。
「可是——」
「可是什么？」
「我怎能做你的妻子？我什么也不是。」
「你是完美的。只有一桩事我怕——」洛夫特斯迟疑。姑娘紧张地望着他。
「只有一桩，」他续道，「我不、也永远不会好到配你的一半。」
「啊！」
「连一半也不会。」
「啊！你怎么能这样说？这不是真的。若是真的，我还能在乎你么？」
「那么你在乎？」
「你还不知道么？」

===Original===
“Then don’t go, don’t go from me ever.”
“But—”
“Yes, I know. You are thinking of your father, of whom you have told me; perhaps, too, of my mother, of whom I told you. When she knows you and learns to love you, as she will, we can be married before all the world. We could now were I not dependent on her. Yet then, am I not dependent too on you? Come with me, and afterward—”
“I cannot,” the girl cried; “it would kill my father.”
“You have but to wire him that you have gone to be married, and it will be the truth.”
“I cannot,” the girl repeated. “Oh, what are you asking me to do?”

===Chinese===
「那就别走，永远别离开我。」
「可是——」
「是，我知道。你在想你父亲，你曾对我说过他；也许也在想我母亲，我与你说过她。等她认识了你、爱上了你——她一定会的——我们便能当着天下人的面成婚。如今便能，若非我仰赖于她。可话说回来，我不也仰赖于你么？随我来，之后——」
「我不能，」姑娘叫道，「那会要了我父亲的命。」
「你只消给他发封电报，说你前去成婚了，那便是实话。」
「我不能，」姑娘重复，「唉，你叫我做什么？」

===Original===
“I am asking you to be my wife. What is the ceremony to you? What are a few words mumbled by a hired priest? Love, love alone, is marriage.”
“No, no. To you perhaps. But not to me.”
“And the ceremony shall follow as soon as we can manage. Can you not trust me for that?”
“But—”
“Will you not trust me? If you are to put your whole life into my keeping you should at least begin by doing that.”
The girl looked at the man and then away, at vistas he could not see, the winding slopes of asphodel, the sudden and precipitate abyss. Yet he spoke so fair, she told herself. Surely it was to the slopes he meant to take her, not to that blackening pit.

===Chinese===
「我请你做我的妻子。仪式于你又算什么？一个雇来的神甫嘟囔几句又算什么？爱，唯有爱，才是婚姻。」
「不、不。于你也许是。于我不是。」
「仪式会尽快补办的，一俟我们办得到。这事你难道信不过我？」
「可是——」
「你信不过我么？你既要把一生交我掌管，总该至少先从此信起。」
姑娘看了看这男子，又望向别处，望向他看不见的景象——那蜿蜒的常春花斜坡、那骤然陡峭的深渊。然而他说得这样好听，她对自己说。他要带她去的，定是那斜坡，而非那变黑的坑。

===Original===
“Yet if you won’t,” Loftus continued, “it is best for both that we should part.”
“For—for always?”
“Yes.”
Just why he omitted to explain. But then there are explanations that explain nothing. Yet to her, for a moment, the threat was like a flash in darkness. For a moment she thought that she could not let him go. About her swarmed her dreams. Through them his kisses pierced. For a moment only. The flash had passed. She was in darkness again. Before her was the precipitate abyss. Shudderingly she drew from it.
But Loftus was very resolute. “If you will you have my promise.”

===Chinese===
「可你若不肯，」洛夫特斯续道，「我们最好就此分手，于你我二人都是。」
「分——永远？」
「是。」
究竟为何，他未加说明。可有些解释本就解释不了什么。然而于她，有一瞬，这威胁像黑暗中的一道闪光。有一瞬她想，她不能放他走。她的梦围着她蜂拥。他的吻穿过那些梦刺进来。只是一瞬。闪光已过。她又落入黑暗。面前是那陡峭的深渊。她战栗着从它退开。
可洛夫特斯十分坚决。「你若肯，便有我的诺言。」

===Original===
For answer she looked at him, looked into his eyes, peered into them, deep down, striving to see what was there, trying to mirror her soul in his own.
“Before God and man I swear you shall be my wife.”
At that, suddenly within her, fear melted away. If she had not seen his soul she had heard it. Where fear had been was faith. Dumb with the enchantment of a dream come true, she half arose. But his arms went about her and in them she lay like seaweed in the tide.

===Chinese===
作为回答，她望着他，望进他的眼，深深地向里窥，竭力要看清里面是什么，要让自己的灵魂映照在他的灵魂里。
「在神与人面前我发誓，你将是我的妻子。」
闻此，她心中的恐惧忽然消融。她若未曾见着他的灵魂，也已听见了它。恐惧之处，成了信念。在美梦成真的着魔里默然无语，她半立起身。可他的双臂已绕住她，她躺在其中，如海藻在潮水里。

## V Marie Changes Her Name / V 玛丽改了名字

===Original===
Gay Street knew Marie no more. Twenty-second Street made her acquaintance. There, in the Arundel, an apartment house which is just around the corner from Gramercy Park, Loftus secured quarters for her.
These quarters, convenient for him, to her were temporary. She regarded them as a tent on the road to the slopes. Even in that light they were attractive. Though small, they were fastidiously furnished and formed what agents call a “bijou.” Loftus, who had whims which the girl thought poetic, preferred “aviary.” He preferred, too, that she should change her name. Durand seemed to him extremely plebeian. Mentally he cast about. Leroy suggested itself. It had in it an echo of France and also of old New York. As such it appealed to him and, therefore, to her. There and then Marie became known as Miss Leroy and, incidentally, very busy.

===Chinese===
盖伊街再不识玛丽了。第二十二街与她结了识。在那里，阿伦德尔公寓——一幢就在格拉默西公园拐角处的公寓楼——洛夫特斯为她觅了下处。
这处下处，于他方便，于她则只是暂栖。她把它看作通往那片斜坡路上的一顶帐篷。即便如此看，它也颇为动人。屋子虽小，陈设却考究至极，凑成中介人所称的「小珍品」。洛夫特斯有些怪念头，姑娘以为那是诗意，他倒更爱叫它「鸟舍」。他也更愿她改个名字。杜朗这姓在他看来太鄙俗。他心里盘算。勒鲁瓦（Leroy）自行浮现。它带有法国的回响，也带有旧纽约的回响。这样它合他意，因而也合她意。就在那儿、就在那时，玛丽以勒鲁瓦小姐之名行世，顺带也忙得不可开交。

===Original===
Every day Annette, Juliette and Marguerite had frocks for her to try on. There were hats to go with those frocks. There was lingerie to be selected, stuffs immaterial as moonbeams, cambrics that could be drawn through a ring. In addition, there was Signor Tambourini, who was to teach her how to handle her voice, and Baron Mesnilmontant, who was to teach her to handle a horse. When she so desired she had but to telephone and in five minutes there was a victoria at the door. For her sitting-room the florist who had so disturbed Mr. Cohen fetched flowers every other day.
In the flowers there were thorns, of course. Marie worried about many things, yet mainly because Mrs. Loftus had not yet “seen and learned to love her.” Against that, though, there were difficulties. At first Mrs. Loftus had a dreadful cold. Then she had gone out of town to recuperate. This was very unfortunate, but like the quarters, only temporary. Loftus assured her of that. What he said was gospel.

===Chinese===
每天安奈特、朱丽叶特与玛格丽特都有衣裙送来给她试。与衣裙相配的帽子也有。内衣也要挑，那料子轻如月光，细布薄得能从一枚戒指中穿过。此外还有坦博里尼先生（Signor Tambourini），负责教她如何驾驭自己的嗓音；还有梅尼蒙当男爵（Baron Mesnilmontant），负责教她如何驾驭一匹马。她想出门时，只消打个电话，五分钟内门口便有一辆维多利亚式马车候着。那位曾搅得科恩先生不得安宁的花商，每隔一日便为她的起居室送花来。
花中自有刺，这是自然。玛丽为许多事发愁，可主要还是因为洛夫特斯太太至今尚未「认识她、爱上她」。不过此事也有难处。起初洛夫特斯太太患了重感冒。随后她又出城养病去了。这实在不幸，可如同那下处一样，只是暂时的。洛夫特斯如此向她保证。他所说的话便是福音。

===Original===
The position in which the girl was placed worried her nevertheless. She knew it was wrong. But always she consoled herself with the belief that shortly it would be righted. On that belief she would have staked her soul. Had he not sworn it? Precisely how she would have acted had she realized that he had lied like a thief one may surmise and never know. The misery of life is the necessity of becoming accustomed to certain things. There are natures that adapt themselves more readily than others. There are also natures that cannot adapt themselves at all. Had Marie realized the truth it may be that she would have beaten her head against the walls. Yet it may also be that in the end adaptability would have come. But not happiness. Happiness consists, if it consists in anything, in being on good terms with oneself. Had Marie known the truth never could she have been that. In the circumstances it was considerate of Loftus to withhold it from her. But Loftus was a very considerate person. He hated tears, and scenes he frankly abominated.

===Chinese===
然而姑娘所处的境地仍叫她忧心。她知道这不合礼。但她总以「不久便会拨正」之念自慰。这份信念她敢以灵魂相赌。他不是发过誓么？倘若她发觉他如贼一般撒了谎，她究竟会怎样做——人可以揣度，却永无从知晓。人生的苦楚之一，便是不得不习惯某些事。有些天性比旁的更易自适。也有些天性全然不能自适。玛丽若知真相，也许会把头往墙上撞。可也许到头来她也会适应。但不会是幸福。幸福——若它存在于什么之中的话——在于与自己相安。玛丽若知真相，永不能如此。在这种情况下，洛夫特斯不告诉她，倒算体贴了。可洛夫特斯本就是个极体贴的人。他厌恶眼泪，至于大闹一场，他更是公然深恶痛绝。

===Original===
Loftus, though considerate, was vain. It was regrettable to him that he could not parade Marie about. But social New York is severe. Among its members it refuses to countenance any open disregard for what’s what. Though what occurs behind its back it is too high-bred to notice.
Loftus, unable to parade Marie about, paraded her in. To the aviary he brought men, some of whom having otherwise nothing to do with this drama need not delay its recital, but, among others, he brought Annandale and Orr.
Annandale, who could not keep a thing from Sylvia, told her about it. The story so shocked her that she first made a point of his not going there again and then debated whether she ought to recognize Loftus any more. In the process she confided the story to Fanny Price, who got suddenly red—a phenomenon rare with her and which annoyed her very much, so much that she bit her lip, desisting only through fear of making it bleed. What is the use of spoiling one’s looks?

===Chinese===
洛夫特斯虽体贴，却爱虚荣。不能带玛丽四处招摇，于他颇为遗憾。可社交界的纽约是严厉的。它不许其成员公然无视何者为是、何者为非。至于背后发生的事，它太高贵了，不屑一顾。
洛夫特斯既不能带玛丽出去招摇，便把她往里招摇。他把男人带到鸟舍来，其中有些与本戏无干、不劳赘述，可他还带来了安南代尔与奥尔。
安南代尔瞒不住西尔维娅，便告诉了她。这故事把她惊得——先逼他答应不再去那里，继而斟酌自己是否还该再理洛夫特斯。斟酌之间，她把这故事吐露给了范妮·普莱斯，后者倏地红了脸——这对她可是罕事，也令她十分恼怒，恼得咬住嘴唇，只因怕咬出血才住口。糟蹋自己的容貌有什么好处呢？

===Original===
Marie, meanwhile, rather liked Annandale. She also rather liked Orr. One evening both were bidden to the aviary. At the bidding Annandale had hesitated. He did not wish to offend Sylvia. But reflecting that she need never know, that, anyway, it was none of her business and, besides, what the deuce! he was not tied to her apron strings, was he? he concluded to go.
To that conclusion he was assisted by a cocktail. At the time he was in Madison Square, where on a ground floor he occupied a set of chambers, a suite of long, large rooms, sumptuously but soberly furnished with things massive and plain. Here he lived in much luxury and entire peace, save recently when he had lost a retainer and found a burglar. The memory of that intrusion recurring, he touched a bell.

===Chinese===
与此同时，玛丽倒是颇喜欢安南代尔。她也颇喜欢奥尔。一日傍晚，两人都被邀至鸟舍。接到邀请时安南代尔犹豫过。他不愿开罪西尔维娅。可转念又想，她何须知道；再说，这又不关她事；何况，见鬼！他又不是被拴在她围裙带上的，是罢？他便决意去。
助他下了这决心的，是一杯鸡尾酒。其时他在麦迪逊广场（Madison Square），在一楼占着几间屋子——一长溜宽敞的房间，陈设华贵而不浮夸，厚重而素净。他在此过着奢靡而清静的日子，只除了近来失了一名老仆、又撞见一名窃贼。那回闯入的记忆重现，他按了按铃。

===Original===
A man appeared, smug and solemn, a new valet that he had got in to replace an old family servant whom an accident had eliminated.
“Harris, I forgot to ask. Did you get the revolver I told you to buy?”
“Yes, sir. A 32 calibre. It is in the pantry, sir.”
“Put it in the drawer of my dressing-table.”
“Yes, sir. Thank you, sir.”
“And Harris, make me another cocktail.” As the man was leaving Annandale called after him, “Make two.”

===Chinese===
一个人出现了，矜持而严肃，是他新雇的一名贴身男仆，顶替一位因事故而去的老家人。
「哈里斯（Harris），我忘了问。我叫你买的那支左轮手枪买了吗？」
「买了，先生。三十二口径的。在餐具室里，先生。」
「放到我梳妆台的抽屉里。」
「是，先生。谢谢您，先生。」
「还有，哈里斯，再给我调一杯鸡尾酒。」那人正要走，安南代尔又在他身后唤道，「调两杯。」

===Original===
It was these that assisted Annandale to his decision. A man of means, without immediate relatives, without ponderable cares, under their influence he felt rather free to do as he saw fit. The bidding to the aviary, telephoned for him to Harris, was for that evening. Yet that evening he was also expected in Irving Place. But Marie’s invitation was for dinner, whereas he was not due at Sylvia’s until later on.
It was not necessary, he told himself, to be in two places at once. He could dovetail one with another. Then presently, having dressed, he issued forth. But he had omitted to look at the evening papers. He was interested in certain stocks, and to learn what they were doing he stopped in at a neighboring club. There encountering men who asked him to drink, he accepted—though how much he was on the morrow unable to recall. Yet at the time the effect of the stuff, while insidious, was not apparent. When ultimately he reached the aviary he was feeling merely fit, a feeling which the dinner increased.

===Chinese===
正是这两杯助安南代尔下了决心。一个有产业、无近亲、无实在挂虑的人，在它们的影响下，颇觉不妨自行其是。去鸟舍的邀请是替他电话转给哈里斯的，约的是当晚。可当晚他也该在欧文广场露面。不过玛丽的邀请是赴晚宴，而他到西尔维娅那里则是更晚的事。
他不必同时分身两处，他对自己说。他可以把两件事错开。随后不久，换好衣裳，他便出门。可他忘了看晚报。他关心几只股票，为看它们走势如何，他顺脚进了一家附近的俱乐部。在那里遇见几个男人邀他喝酒，他应了——至于喝了多少，次日他便想不起来。可在当时，那酒力虽暗暗侵人，却尚未显形。待他终于到了鸟舍，只觉状态正好——而那顿晚宴更增之。

===Original===
The dinner, perfect in itself, was perfectly served. The appointments were superior and the table a delight. Loftus when he did things did them well. Marie, in a creation of Paquin, imported by Annette, was a pleasure to behold. She had Orr at her right, Annandale at her left. Between them and Loftus were half a dozen other men. All were decorous and beautifully behaved. Except for the absence of feminine guests and one thing else, there was nothing to denote that they were not at the house of some smart young married woman. There was not a word uttered that could not have been bawled through a ballroom. There was not a suggestion not eminently discreet. In this respect only did the dinner differ from any other at which you might assist in the upper circles of New York life.
During the preliminary courses stocks were the sole topic. There was a boom on in the Street. Everybody was making money, including Marie, for whom Loftus had bought a few hundred A.O.T.

===Chinese===
晚宴本身尽善尽美，侍应也无可挑剔。陈设上乘，餐桌令人悦目。洛夫特斯做事，要么不做，做便做得漂亮。玛丽身着帕坎（Paquin）的一袭新作——由安奈特从海外购来——观之赏心悦目。她右边坐着奥尔，左边是安南代尔。他们与洛夫特斯之间，另有六七位男士。众人皆端庄有礼、举止得体。除开没有女宾、以及另有一桩之外，毫无迹象表明他们不是在某个时髦的年轻已婚妇人家中。所说的话，没有一句不能在舞厅里高声嚷出。所含的暗示，没有一丝不是极为得体的。唯有在这一层上，这顿晚宴才与你在纽约上层圈子里可能赴的任何一场不同。
头几道菜上时，股票是唯一的话题。华尔街正逢涨潮。人人都在赚钱，玛丽也包括在内——洛夫特斯替她买了几百股 A.O.T.。

===Original===
Orr alone had sold. “You are all mad,” he declared. “The whole city is crazy. The country is on a debauch. Bulls cannot live forever. The corridas of the Street are just like those of Spain. It is the climax that differs. There the ring is swept by a supe, here it is struck by a crisis. That crisis may come next week, next month, next year. But it will come. It can no more desert the heavens of political economy than the stars can deviate from their course. It is not here yet, the bull is very lively, he is tossing everything sky high, but just when he is at his best and fiercest, just when you are shouting yourselves hoarse, the great espada, whose name is Time, with one swift thrust will transfix him. That is the fate of bulls.”
“We are to be transfixed, are we?” said Annandale.
Marie looked over at Loftus. “Had I not better sell?”
Orr turned to her. “No; hold on and lose. A loss, particularly a first loss, is always a good investment. Besides, if you will permit me to say, you should have no heed of such things. No, Miss Leroy. You should content yourself with continuing to be. A woman who does only that acquires a charm almost supernatural. This was the occupation of the young goddesses of old Greece. How delightful they were! The rose was their model. They had learned the secret of its witchery. They charmed and did nothing. To charm is never easy, but to do nothing is the most difficult of all things, as it is, too, the most intellectual. Yes,” Orr added after a moment, “it is also a thing which the rest of us sadly neglect.”

===Chinese===
唯独奥尔卖了。「你们都疯了，」他宣称，「全城都疯了。这国家正醉酒纵乐。牛市不会永生。华尔街的斗牛，正像西班牙的斗牛。不同的只是高潮。那里，牛场由一名杂役清扫；这里，牛场被一场危机击中。那危机也许下周、下月、明年来。但它必来。它不会背离政治经济学的天宇，一如星辰不会偏离其轨。它尚未到此，那牛十分活泼，把一切都顶上了天，可正当它最壮、最猛之时，正当你们喊哑了嗓子之时，那位伟大的 *espada*（斗牛士）——其名曰时间——将以一记迅捷的刺击将它钉穿。这便是牛的命运。」
「我们要被钉穿，是么？」安南代尔说。
玛丽望向洛夫特斯。「我是不是该卖？」
奥尔转向她。「不；攥着，然后赔。一次赔，尤其头一次赔，永远是桩好投资。再者，恕我直言，您不该操心这些。不，勒鲁瓦小姐。您只须继续做您自己便够了。一个只做这件事的女子，会获得一种近乎超自然的魅力。古希腊年轻的女神们从事的便是此业。她们何等可爱！玫瑰是她们的楷模。她们习得了它魅惑的秘密。她们迷人，却无所事事。迷人从来不易，而无所事事则是万事之中最难的一件，也是最有智性的一件。不错，」奥尔顿了顿又补道，「这也是我们其余的人可悲地疏忽了的一件事。」

===Original===
“Oh, I say,” Loftus threw in. “It is not so long ago that I heard you maintaining that only imbeciles were idle, that everybody should have something to do. You are rather contradictory, don’t you think?”
“No, not a bit; and for the reason that then I was speaking of the generality of people, and now of the exceptional few. The idleness of the imbecile is always imbecilic, but the dreams of a poet have spells that enthrall. Try to fancy a busy poet. You cannot. It is an anomaly at which the imagination balks. By the same token you cannot fancy a useful Venus. You cannot fancy Psyche occupied with anything but love. Love is—or rather, should be—woman’s sole occupation. The perfume of Eros should be about them all.”
“The perfume of Eros!” muttered Annandale, to whom the phrase appealed. “The perfume of Eros!” he repeated and helped himself to wine. “I say, Orr, what the dickens is that?”
“Only the motor force of the universe.”

===Chinese===
「哎，我说，」洛夫特斯插话，「不久前我还听你主张只有笨蛋才游手好闲、人人都该有点事做。你这人挺自相矛盾，是罢？」
「不，一点也不；原因在于，那时我说的是一般人，此刻说的是那凤毛麟角的少数。笨蛋的闲散永远愚蠢，可诗人的梦却有摄人心魄的魔力。试着想象一个忙碌的诗人罢。你办不到。那是一种想象力都难以企及的异常。同理，你也想象不出一个有用的维纳斯。你想象不出普赛克（Psyche）从事除爱之外的任何事。爱是——或者说应该是——女子唯一的职业。爱神之香（the perfume of Eros）本该萦绕在她们每一个人身畔。」
「爱神之香！」安南代尔嘟囔道，这话很合他意。「爱神之香！」他重复着，又给自己斟了酒。「我说，奥尔，那到底是个什么鬼东西？」
「不过是宇宙的原动力罢了。」

===Original===
“What?”
“Yes, indeed. It is the sublimate of love. And love is the source of human activity. It has no other. Without it civilization would retrograde and society return to the woods. Love is the basis of tragedy, the woof of romance, the incentive of commerce, of crime too, of heroism as well.”
“My!” said Marie, whom the brief deluge of words amazed. “My!”
“I must get that off,” Annandale muttered. In the sotto voce of thought he added, “to Sylvia.” Obviously, he had had his fill. He stood up, making an excuse, imperceptibility lurching as he did so.
It was after ten. Long since coffee had been served. Orr, too, got up. He thanked his hostess. The other men imitated him. Loftus and Marie were alone.

===Chinese===
「什么？」
「不错。它是爱的升华。而爱是人类活动的源头。此外别无源头。没有它，文明将倒退，社会将重返丛林。爱是悲剧的基底，是传奇的纬线，是商业的诱因，是犯罪的诱因，也是英雄主义的诱因。」
「哎呀！」玛丽说，被这一阵短促的词瀑惊到。「哎呀！」
「我得把它记下来。」安南代尔嘟囔。在思想的不出声里他补了一句：「记给西尔维娅。」显然，他已喝够。他站起来，找了个借口，起身时不经察觉地晃了一下。
已过十点。咖啡早前已上过。奥尔也起身。他向女主人致谢。其余男士纷纷效仿。洛夫特斯与玛丽单独留下。

===Original===
Loftus went to a window. Then he turned. “Put on your hat, little girl, and we will go out; though, after all, I do not see that you need bother with a hat, unless you prefer.”
“I will do as you wish, dear.”
Presently they were in Lexington Avenue, a moment more, in Gramercy Park. Loftus, after fumbling for his key, opened one of the little gates. Within was silence. Occasionally, from the pavement without came the sound of footsteps.
Loftus and Marie seated themselves on a bench near the gate through which they had entered. Loftus was smoking. A boy passed; stopped, and sticking his nose through the railings, called: “Hi, mister, will you give me a light?”
Loftus made no answer. The boy called again. “Will you? And a cigar with it?”
Then he laughed and passed on. The silence increased. In the air was a fragrance, the clinging odor of the honeysuckle, the clean smell of fresh turf. Beyond, the great dim houses that front the park gave the place and the hour an accent of their own.

===Chinese===
洛夫特斯走到窗前。随后他转过身。「戴上帽子，小丫头，我们出去走走；不过说到底，我看你也未必费事戴帽子，除非你乐意。」
「你怎样说我便怎样，亲爱的。」
不多时他们已到了列克星敦大道（Lexington Avenue），再一转眼，便在格拉默西公园里了。洛夫特斯摸索着钥匙，开了其中一道小门。门内是寂静。偶尔，从外面人行道传来脚步声。
洛夫特斯与玛丽在他们进来的那道门附近的一条长椅上坐下。洛夫特斯抽着烟。一个男孩走过；停下，把鼻子贴着栏杆，叫道：「喂，先生，借个火？」
洛夫特斯没答。那男孩又叫。「借不借？再搭支雪茄？」
然后他笑着一溜烟走了。寂静更甚。空气里有香气——忍冬花那缠绕的气息、新草皮那干净的气味。再过去，面朝公园的那些幽暗大宅，给这地方、这时辰添了一笔独属于它们的重音。

===Original===
“I like it here,” said Marie, “it is so elegant.”
“Never let me hear you use that word again. It is provincial, suburban and, worse, it is shopgirl.”
“Yes, dear.”
“This evening I saw you eat an ice with a spoon. Never do that. Use a fork.”
“Yes, dear.”
Appeased by this docility, Loftus condescended to agree in turn with her. He, too, liked the park. At night, when the weather was decent, always he sat there a bit quite by himself. He had done so for years. He told her this, adding confidentially, “It is a habit.”
To Marie the habit seemed most poetic. She said so, explaining that she was very fond of poetry.
Loftus looked up at the stars. “The only real poetry is there. By the way, do you believe in God?”
Marie, uncertain of her lover’s creeds, hesitatingly glanced at him. “Yes—in a way. But I won’t, if you object.”

===Chinese===
「我喜欢这里，」玛丽说，「真雅致。」
「再不许我听你用这个词。它土气、乡气，更糟的是，它是女店员的词。」
「是，亲爱的。」
「今晚我见你用勺子吃冰淇淋。再不可这样。要用叉子。」
「是，亲爱的。」
这番顺从平息了洛夫特斯，他便也屈尊附和起她来。他也喜欢这公园。夜里天气好时，他总独自在此坐上一会儿。多年来他都如此。他这般告诉她，又附耳添了一句：「这是个习惯。」
在玛丽看来，这习惯颇具诗意。她这样说，还解释自己很喜欢诗。
洛夫特斯仰观星辰。「唯一的真诗在那里。顺便问一句，你信上帝么？」
玛丽拿不准情郎的信条，迟疑地瞥了他一眼。「信——某种程度。可你若反对，我便不信。」

===Original===
