This self-abnegation pleased Loftus. He twisted his mustache and smiled. “But no, you little goose, I don’t object in the least. On the contrary. It is right and proper that you should.”
Gratified at this encouraging indulgence the girl’s hand stole into his. Then for awhile they sat and talked about nothing whatever, which, of all subjects, is, perhaps, the least disagreeable. Wearying at last even of that, they got up to go.
At the gate Marie drew back. A man was passing, swaying uncertainly, arguing with himself.
“Why! it is Mr. Annandale,” the girl in a frightened whisper murmured.
“I wonder where he got all that liquor?” Loftus queried. “Not at Sylvia Waldron’s, I’ll wager.”
“Sylvia Waldron! What a sweet name,” said Marie. “Who is she?”
“The girl he is engaged to.”
“Is she pretty?”
“Oh, tall and dark, don’t you know. Not at all my style.”
But now night had swallowed Annandale. Loftus and Marie passed on.

===Chinese===
这份自弃令洛夫特斯欢喜。他捻着髭，笑了。「哎，才不呢，你这只小鹅，我一点也不反对。恰恰相反。你本该信，这是对的、是合宜的。」
姑娘受这鼓励的宽纵所抚慰，悄悄把手伸进他手里。随后他们坐了一阵，谈着全然的虚无——在所有话题里，这许是最不惹人厌的一个。末了连这也谈腻了，他们起身要走。
在门口玛丽缩了回来。一个男人正走过，摇摇晃晃、似在自相争论。
「咦！是安南代尔先生。」姑娘惊惶地低语。
「不知他这些酒是哪里喝来的？」洛夫特斯问，「我敢赌，不是在西尔维娅·沃尔德伦家喝的。」
「西尔维娅·沃尔德伦！多甜的名字，」玛丽说，「她是谁？」
「他订了婚的姑娘。」
「她美么？」
「哦，高挑、肤色深，你懂的。完全不是我的型。」
可此刻夜已吞没了安南代尔。洛夫特斯与玛丽继续前行。

## VI The Yellow Fay / VI 黄衣仙子

===Original===
At noon the next day Annandale was not awake nor was he asleep. Through spaces in which memories met, entangled and sank, he was groping in search of himself. In these spaces there were things, some formless, others half-formed, that got between consciousness and interfered with the search.
These things pulled at him, tripped him, shoved him down to the memories that were sinking below. The spaces themselves were very dark. But, in the deeper depths, where memories swooned, the darkness was punctuated by slender flames the size of pins. They burned him.
Up and away he tried to rise. When he nearly succeeded, the things above, the things half-formed and formless that were waiting there, pushed him back. Again he tried. But the darkness was thick, the depths were thunderous, the things above pounded on his head, the thin flames lapped at him.

===Chinese===
次日正午，安南代尔既未醒、也未睡。在记忆交错、缠结、下沉的那些空隙里，他摸索着寻找自己。这些空隙里有些东西，有的无形、有的半成形，横亘在意识之间，妨碍着这场寻找。
这些东西拽他、绊他、把他推向那些正沉下去的记忆。这些空隙本身极黑。可是在更深处、记忆昏厥之处，黑暗之中点缀着针尖大的细长火苗。它们灼他。
他努力向上、向外升腾。将要成功之时，上方那些东西——那些半成形的、无形的、正候在那里的——又把他推回。他再试。可黑暗浓稠，深处雷鸣般作响，上方的东西敲他的头，细长的火苗舔他。

===Original===
A force took and lifted him high, very high, and suddenly dropped him. In the abrupt descent he clutched at the things, but he was whirled through them to receding plains and up again, higher, still higher. There a ray filtered, in the light of which a memory staggered. He saw himself drinking in the rooms of that girl of Loftus. From there he passed into blankness at the end of which stood Sylvia, her face white and drawn. The vision vanished. Then it seemed to him that he was drinking with a fat man who had prominent teeth which he took from his mouth and changed into dice. But where? In hell, perhaps. Annandale was uncertain. He knew merely that he had been beastly drunk and that his head was simply splitting.
It continued to split. Hours later, sedatives and his servant aiding, the splitting ceased. But the blanks did not fill and though behind them he could not look, yet the subconscious self that registers and retains everything we do and hear and say prompted him dumbly that behind the blanks there lurked the dismal and, perhaps, the dire.

===Chinese===
一股力量将他攫起、举高、举得极高，又猛地一抛。在陡然的下坠中他去抓那些东西，却被卷着穿过它们、掠过退去的平原，又再上升，更高、更高。那里有一道光线滤入，在它的光里，一段记忆踉跄而出。他看见自己在洛夫特斯那个姑娘的房间里喝酒。从那里他坠入一片空白，空白的尽头立着西尔维娅，脸色苍白、消瘦。这幻象消失了。随后他似乎觉得，自己正与一个胖子对饮，那胖子长着暴牙，把牙从嘴里取出来、变成骰子。可在哪儿？许在地狱。安南代尔拿不准。他只知自己醉得不成样子，头痛欲裂。
头痛继续。几小时后，靠镇静剂与仆人之助，头痛止住了。可那些空白未填上，他虽不能看透其后，然而那个记录并留存我们一切所行所闻所言的潜意识自我，却默然暗示他：空白之后潜伏着可悲的、也许可怖的事。

===Original===
This foreboding he attributed to his nerves. As a matter of fact they were rather shaky. But inaction was intolerable. He tried to write a note to Sylvia, but his hand was insufficiently steady. Failing in that he told Harris to get some flowers, take them to Miss Waldron, and say that he would call that evening. When later the man returned he brought no answer to the message.
“Was Miss Waldron out?” Annandale asked.
“I could not say, sir. I gave the flowers to the maid, and said as how you would call this evening, sir. The maid came back and said Miss Waldron would not be at ’ome.”
At this Annandale flushed. It is true he was flushed already. But the affront was a little more than he could stand. Was he not engaged to her? What did she mean? Yet, then, too, what had he done? He wished to the devil he could tell. Try, though, as he might, he could not recall a thing except a vision of the girl’s face, white, drawn and angered. The rest was not blurred, it was blank. It was extremely unfortunate, and Annandale decided that he was both unhappy and misused.

===Chinese===
他把这预感归咎于神经。事实上他的神经确实颇为不稳。可无所作为又难以忍受。他想给西尔维娅写张便条，可手不够稳。写不成，他便吩咐哈里斯弄些花、送给沃尔德伦小姐，并说他当晚登门。后来仆人回来，却没带回任何回话。
「沃尔德伦小姐出去了？」安南代尔问。
「我说不准，先生。我把花交给了女仆，又照您的吩咐说您今晚登门，先生。女仆回来说沃尔德伦小姐不在家。」
闻此安南代尔脸红了。诚然他本就已红了。可这份冒犯他多少有些受不住。他不是与她订了婚么？她是什么意思？可话说回来，他又做了什么？他巴不得能告诉自己才好。可任他怎么试，除了那姑娘的一张脸——苍白、消瘦、愠怒——之外，他什么也记不起。其余并非模糊，而是空白。这实在太不幸了，安南代尔认定自己既不幸、又受了亏待。

===Original===
These meditations Harris interrupted.
“Mr. Orr, sir.”
Annandale, who had been far away, looked up. Then he nodded.
A moment and Orr entered, eying Annandale curiously as he came.
“What a deuce of a chap you are,” he began.
“Who? I? Why? Why do you say that?”
Orr looked about the room, contemplated a wide lounge of black leather, selected a straight-backed chair instead and seated himself, his hat and stick in his hand.
“You know well enough,” he answered. “But there,” he added at a protest from Annandale, “I don’t propose to scold you. My visit is purely official. Sylvia has asked me to inform you that the engagement is at an end.”

===Chinese===
这些冥想被哈里斯打断。
「奥尔先生，先生。」
方才神游远方的安南代尔抬起头。随后他点点头。
片刻奥尔进来，进门时好奇地打量着安南代尔。
「你这人可真够呛。」他开口。
「谁？我？怎么？你为何这样说？」
奥尔环顾室内，端详了一下那张宽大的黑皮躺椅，却另选了一把直背椅坐下，手里仍拿着帽子和手杖。
「你心里明白得很。」他答。可安南代尔一抗议，他便补道，「好了，我不打算训你。我此来纯系公务。西尔维娅要我通知你：婚约到此为止。」

===Original===
Had any little dog which Annandale did not possess run out from nowhere and bit him fiercely on the leg, he could not have started more. He stared at Orr, who stared at him.
“But! It is impossible! What have I done?”
“It would be more to the point,” Orr cheerfully replied, “to ask what you have not done. Though just what you did do Sylvia omitted to state. She said she could not.”
“Could not tell you?”
“Could not or would not.”
“Then I can’t,” said Annandale helplessly. “I went there last evening, I remember that. I remember, too, that she was angry. But why I do not know. Though, to be candid, she had cause to be. I was drunk.”

===Chinese===
纵使安南代尔并不拥有的某只小狗不知从何处蹿出来、狠狠咬他一口腿，他也吃不了这么大一惊。他瞪着奥尔，奥尔也瞪着他。
「可是！这不可能！我做了什么？」
「更切题的问法，」奥尔愉快地答，「该问你没做什么。至于你究竟做了什么，西尔维娅未加说明。她说她说不出。」
「说不出告诉你？」
「说不出，或不愿说。」
「那我也说不出，」安南代尔无助地说，「昨晚我去了那里，这我记得。我也记得她在生气。可为何生气，我不知道。不过，说句实话，她有理由生气。我醉了。」

===Original===
“You seemed all right at the Arundel,” Orr objected.
“At all events, drunk or sober, I cannot recall a thing. I have tried. I have tried hard. It has gone.”
“Does it happen to you often?”
“What?”
“To forget like that?”
Annandale shook his head. He stood up and stalked about. Orr eyed him. He saw he was not shamming.
“You know, Annandale,” he said at last, “you could not get many to accept that. But I can and do. I have seen cases of the kind before. Will you permit me to advise you?”
“Advise me? I wish to God you would.”

===Chinese===
「在阿伦德尔你看着还好啊。」奥尔反驳。
「无论如何，醉也罢醒也罢，我什么也记不起。我试过了。我拼命试过。它就是没了。」
「这种事你常犯么？」
「什么？」
「这样忘记？」
安南代尔摇摇头。他站起身，来回踱步。奥尔端详他。他看得出他不是装的。
「你晓得，安南代尔，」他终于说，「换作旁人，没几个会信你这话。可我信，也确实信了。我从前见过这类病例。可否容我劝你一句？」
「劝我？我求之不得。」

===Original===
“Littré, who was the wisest and ugliest of men, stated that Hippocrates recommended everybody to get tight once a month, asserting that it was hygienic, good for the system, that it relaxed the nerves. Littré must have known what he was talking about. He put Hippocrates in French, into ten volumes at that! But what is good for everybody is bad for you. Don’t drink, Annandale. It will get you into mischief.”
“As if it had not? Look at the box I am in. But could you not get Sylvia to reconsider the matter? If she will, I pledge my word never to touch another drop. Of course, I apologize for everything I did. I am only too anxious to. You must understand that I am profoundly humiliated at the idea that I could have done anything she did not like. Certainly I did not intend to. Won’t you say that to her?”

===Chinese===
「利特雷（Littré）——最智慧也最丑的男人——曾说，希波克拉底劝人每月大醉一次，称此举卫生、有益身体、可松弛神经。利特雷说的必是有所本的。他把希波克拉底译成法文，还译成了十卷！可对人人皆宜之事，于你却是有害的。别喝了，安南代尔。它会叫你惹祸。」
「说得好像还没惹过似的？瞧我陷的这窘境。可你能不能叫西尔维娅再考虑考虑？她若肯，我发誓再不沾一滴。当然，我为我所做的一切道歉。我是巴不得道歉。你必须明白，一想到自己可能做了任何她不喜欢的事，我便深感屈辱。我当然不是有意的。你能不能把这话说与她听？」

===Original===
“Oh, I appreciate your position,” said Orr. “To me the essence of crime is the intent. But, then, you see, I am a man. Now girls are different, and my cousin is very different even from most girls. Her views are very strict. Even otherwise, to any decent girl, a man in his cups is not agreeable. But then, you know, it is not merely a question of that. It is a question of matrimony. Matrimony generally means children. It is on them that the sins of the father are visited. There is the rub. Sylvia, I have not a doubt, will in the end forgive you, but were she to marry you and her children have your sins visited on them, never would she forgive herself. That I am sure you can realize. Anyway, for the moment argument with her would be futile. Besides, she has gone from town.”
“Gone?”
“Yes, she left for Newport today. If I were you I would not attempt to follow. But I will write. I will tell her what you say, and I will tell you her reply.”

===Chinese===
「噢，我体谅你的处境，」奥尔说，「在我看来，犯罪的本质在于动机。可话说回来，你瞧，我是个男人。女子则不同，我这位表妹更与大多数女子都不同。她的见解极严。即便不提这个，对任何体面的姑娘而言，一个醉醺醺的男人都不叫人愉快。可你也知道，这不仅仅是那一桩事的问题。这是婚姻的问题。婚姻一般意味着孩子。父亲的罪是要报应到他们身上的。难便难在这里。西尔维娅，我毫不怀疑，终会原谅你；可她若嫁了你、她的孩子又承了你那些罪，她便永远不会原谅自己。这一点我想你能明白。无论如何，眼下与她争辩是徒劳的。再者，她已离城。」
「离城？」
「是，她今日动身去了纽波特（Newport）。若我是你，我不会追去。但我会写信。我会把你的话告诉她，再把她的回话告诉你。」

===Original===
Orr stood up. As he did so Annandale sat down. He cared for Sylvia Waldron, absolutely, uniquely. He felt, too, that she had cared for him. But while Orr had been speaking he told himself that her caring had ceased. Had any affection remained she could not have gone. It was his fault, though. He had shocked it out of existence. At the thought of that he felt unutterably miserable. What he felt he looked.
Orr saw his dejection. “Annandale,” he said, “I hardly suppose that it will console you now to have me tell you that nothing earthly is of any consequence, but, if you let the idea permeate you, ultimately perhaps it may. By the way, that is a new man you have, isn’t it?”
In the wreckage amid which Annandale was floundering the question was like a rope; he caught at it and swam up.
“Who? Harris? Yes, the other poor devil I had was run over and died in an ambulance.”
Orr tapped at his foot with his stick. “I may be in error,” he said, “but I think I have seen him before.”

===Chinese===
奥尔站起身。他一站，安南代尔便坐下。他爱西尔维娅·沃尔德伦，全心全意、独一无二地爱。他也曾觉得她爱他。可在奥尔说话的当儿，他对自己说，她的爱已经止了。若还有半分情，她便不会走。可这是他的错。是他把那份情惊得烟消云散。想到此处，他觉得苦不堪言。他所感的，也写在了脸上。
奥尔看出他的颓丧。「安南代尔，」他说，「我几乎不以为此刻对你说「尘世之事无一要紧」能给你什么安慰，可你若让这念头渗入你，终究也许会有用的。顺便问一句，你新换了个人，是罢？」
在安南代尔扑腾其间的这片残骸里，这个问题像根绳子；他一把抓住，游了上来。
「谁？哈里斯？是，我原来那个可怜虫被车轧了，死在救护车上。」
奥尔用手杖敲敲自己的脚。「也许我看错了，」他说，「可我觉得从前见过他。」

===Original===
“Then it must have been in London. He has been here only a short time. He tells me he used to be with Catty.”
Catty was a relative of Annandale, a New York girl who had married the Duke of Kincardine.
“Possibly,” said Orr. “Well,” he added, reverting to the episode that had brought him there, “I am sorry for all this. I know you are. I will write to Sylvia and tell her so.”
“Please do.”
Annandale stood up and accompanied him to the door. When he turned life seemed blank as the blanks of the night.

===Chinese===
「那想必是在伦敦。他来此地不久。他告诉我他从前在卡蒂（Catty）家做事。」
卡蒂是安南代尔的亲戚，一个嫁了金卡丁公爵（Duke of Kincardine）的纽约姑娘。
「也许吧。」奥尔说。「好了，」他重又转回带他来此的那件事上，「这一切我很抱歉。我知道你也一样。我会给西尔维娅写信，也如此告诉她。」
「拜托了。」
安南代尔起身送他到门口。待他转身，生活便如昨夜那些空白一般空了。

## VII Sweet-and-Twenty / VII 双十佳人

===Original===
What Sylvia replied to Orr’s communication, whether indeed she replied at all, Annandale was not informed. He himself wrote to her. The letter was long; it was also abject. But he got no answer. He wrote again. The result was the same.
Then both at her and at himself he rebelled. He had supped on humiliations. He had no appetite for more. With some bravery, yet without bravado, he tore a leaf from his life and on it wrote Finis. The epitaph was figurative, but he thought it final. He thought that he could dictate to Fate. It is a mistake that many make.
Presently it surprised him to find how laborious is the task of putting people out of your life. If you have cared for them they will come back. In the pages of a book, in the pauses of speech, suddenly you behold them. In sleep they will not let you be. When you awake, there they are. However detestable their behavior may have been, in dream they visit and caress you. It takes time and vigilance; it takes more, it takes other faces to disperse them.

===Chinese===
西尔维娅如何答复奥尔所传的话，她究竟有没有答复，安南代尔都未被告知。他亲自给她写信。信很长；也很卑微。可他没收到回音。他又写。结果一样。
于是他对她、也对自己都反叛起来。屈辱他已尝够了。再无胃口尝更多。他鼓起几分勇气——却非虚张声势——从自己的人生里撕下一页，在上头写下「终」。这墓志铭是比喻，他却当作了结。他以为他能对命运发号施令。许多人犯的正是这个错。
不久他便惊讶地发现，要把人从你生命中赶出去，是件多么吃力的事。你若曾爱过他们，他们便会回来。在一本书的书页里，在说话的停顿里，你忽然看见他们。睡眠中他们也不放过你。你醒来，他们就在那里。无论他们的作为曾多么可憎，在梦里他们却来造访你、爱抚你。这需要时间与警觉；更需要——需要别的脸孔才能把他们驱散。

===Original===
In spite of the Finis, Sylvia Waldron declined to be dismissed. She haunted Annandale. To memories of her he could not always show the door. Sometimes they were masked. Occasionally they reproached him. Again they seemed to say that did he but find out how, all might yet be well between them. But usually they came and stood gazing at him in love and grief eternal.
Then he would start. But what could he do? Besides, there was the Finis.
June meanwhile had come and gone. Summer with a frenzy quasi-maniacal had battened on the town. It is said that the hottest place in the world is a port on the Persian Gulf. But it is wrong to believe everything we hear. When New York decides to be hot, the temperature of the Persian port must be agreeable by comparison.
One fetid noon Annandale fled. When he stopped it was at Narragansett.

===Chinese===
尽管写了「终」，西尔维娅·沃尔德伦却不肯被就此打发。她萦绕着安南代尔。对关于她的记忆，他不能总是送客。有时它们戴着面具。偶尔它们责备他。又有时它们似乎在说：只要他找出办法，他们之间也许还能重归于好。可通常，它们只是来了，以永恒的爱与悲凝视着他。
他便一惊。可他能做什么？再说，还有那个「终」字。
与此同时，六月来了又去。夏天以一种近乎癫狂的狂热，在这城里饕餮肆虐。据说世界上最热之处是波斯湾某港口。可听到什么便信什么，是要不得的。纽约一旦决意要热，那波斯港口的气温一比之下必觉宜人。
一个闷臭的正午，安南代尔出逃了。他停下来时，已到了纳拉甘西特。

===Original===
Before August comes and with it the mob, Narragansett is charming. There is a mile of empty hotels, a stretch of sand fine as face powder, a heaving, heavenly desert of blue and an atmosphere charged with ozone and desire.
In August the hotels are packed. The stretch of sand is a stage. Every day a ballet is given there. The coryphées are the prettiest girls in the world—girls from Baltimore, girls from Philadelphia, girls from everywhere, girls with the Occident in their eyes and lips that say “Drink me.”
At high noon, from the greenroom of the bathhouses, Sweet-and-twenty floats down, clasps the sea to the hum of harps, breasts the waves to the laugh of brass and reemerges to the sound of trumpets.
After the dip, other diversions. Primarily flirtations on the lawns; later, polo at the Country Club; at night, dancing in the ballrooms, more flirtations on the galleries of the Casino, supper on the terrace below.

===Chinese===
八月来临、乌合之众随之而来之前，纳拉甘西特是迷人的。一英里空荡的旅馆，一片细如脂粉的沙滩，一片起伏荡漾的蓝色天堂般的荒漠，还有那饱含臭氧与欲望的空气。
八月里旅馆爆满。那片沙滩成了舞台。每日都上演一出芭蕾。群舞的姑娘是世上最美的姑娘——巴尔的摩（Baltimore）来的、费城（Philadelphia）来的、四海来的姑娘，眼里带着西方，唇上说「饮我」。
正午时分，从更衣室的后台，双十佳人飘然而下，在竖琴的嗡鸣里把大海拥入怀中，在铜管的笑声里迎浪而前，又在号角声中重新浮现。
洗浴之后，另有消遣。先是草坪上的调情；随后是乡村俱乐部（Country Club）的马球；夜里是舞厅里的舞蹈，赌场（Casino）回廊里更多的调情，以及下面露台上的夜宵。

===Original===
The terrace resembles, or, more exactly, on this particular summer did resemble, a roof garden on the ground floor. From a kiosk a band of Hungarians distributed selections of popular rot, sometimes their own delirious czardas. There, circled by variegated lights, fanned by the violins, girls and men sat beneath the high, wide, flowerful umbrellas of Japan.
Sometimes some of them, wearying of that, wandered into silences and shadows and lingered there, occupied with the crops, with strikes and other subjects of national interest which young people always discuss when holding hands in the dark.
To Newport, which squats disdainfully over the way, this is all too free and easy. To Annandale, it was distressing. Everywhere there was love, yet none for him. He had come to the Pier, as Narragansett is locally termed, because of Newport’s propinquity. If Sylvia so much as signaled he could join her at once.

===Chinese===
这露台像——或者更确切地说，在这个特定的夏天曾经像——一座设在底层的屋顶花园。一座凉亭里，一支匈牙利乐队分发着流行的陈腔滥调，有时是他们自己那令人发狂的查尔达什（czardas）。在那里，在五彩灯光的环绕下、在小提琴的扇动下，姑娘与小伙们坐在日本那又高又大、花团锦簇的伞下。
有时他们中有人腻了这些，便踱进寂静与阴影里逗留，忙着谈论收成、罢工以及别的国家大事——这些正是年轻人黑夜里手牵着手时总爱谈论的话题。
在隔路蹲踞、一脸不屑的纽波特看来，这一切都太放任随意了。对安南代尔，则是折磨。到处都是爱，唯独没有给他的。他来这码头——纳拉甘西特当地如此称呼——正是冲着纽波特近在咫尺。西尔维娅只要稍示信号，他便能立时与她相聚。

===Original===
As yet no signaling was apparent. In its place was an influx of a reflection of fashion. The influx made Annandale swear. He hated to be seen stalking moodily about. He hated still more to have the rupture of his engagement discussed. The ballet on the beach irritated him. He told himself that he had come to the wrong shop. One day he thought of joining friends in Canada. The next he thought of joining friends who had gone abroad. The day after he thought that still he might be signaled.
In these uncertainties he loitered, annoyed but sober. Since the visit from Orr he had not touched a drop. Then, it so fell about that one evening he looked in at a dance at the Casino. Madness was in the air. The savors of the sea, the tonic of the dip, the stare of the harvest moon, go to the head, stir the heart, excite the pulse in a manner really Boccaccian.

===Chinese===
然而尚无任何信号。取而代之的，是时髦倒影的一股涌入。这股涌入叫安南代尔直骂娘。他恨被人瞧见郁郁地踱来踱去。他更恨自己婚约破裂一事被人议论。沙滩上那出芭蕾惹恼他。他对自己说，他走错了门。一日他想，不如去加拿大找朋友。次日他想，不如去找那些出了国的朋友。又过了一日他想，也许还是会有信号的。
在这些不定里他游荡着，烦躁却清醒。自奥尔那次来访之后，他未再沾一滴酒。后来，事情偏偏如此凑巧，一天傍晚他顺脚进了赌场的一场舞会。空气里弥漫着疯狂。海的气息、洗浴的提神、秋分圆月的凝视，都叫人上头、搅动人心、激动脉搏，其方式真可谓薄伽丘式（Boccaccian）。

===Original===
Madness is contagious. It seemed particularly catching that night. The hall was filled, the gallery flushed. On a stage, at the end of the ballroom, musicians were tossing out in trailing rhythm the sorcery of “Il Bacio,” the invitation of the Cent Vierges, the muffled riot of El Capitan.
To these incentives couples turned. Beneath the gallery where Annandale stood there was a vision of white arms, bare necks, slender waists circled by the blackness of men’s sleeves. Three hundred girls and men were waltzing together, interchanging partners, clasping hands, gazing into each other’s eyes.
Behind Annandale a group had gathered. They were talking, yet of what he did not heed. But, presently, into the conversation filtered the freshness of another voice.
“I quite believe, you know,” the voice was saying, “that a girl who stops here this summer will stop at nothing next.”

===Chinese===
疯狂是会传染的。那夜它似乎格外易染。舞厅满了，回廊涨红了脸。在舞厅尽头的一处舞台上，乐师们以拖曳的节奏抛出《吻》（Il Bacio）的妖术、《百童女》（Cent Vierges）的邀约、《埃尔·卡皮坦》（El Capitan）那闷闷的狂欢。
在这些鼓动下，一对对舞伴转过身来。在安南代尔所立的回廊之下，是一片雪白的手臂、裸露的颈项、为男子黑色衣袖所环的纤腰的景象。三百个姑娘与小伙子一同跳着华尔兹，交换舞伴、紧握双手、彼此凝视。
安南代尔身后聚起一群人。他们在谈话，谈的什么他没在意。可不久，一段清新的嗓音渗进了这场谈话。
「我倒相信，你晓得，」那嗓音正说着，「今年夏天在这儿逗留的姑娘，明年便什么也不搁在眼里了。」

===Original===
At the jest Annandale turned. There, pretty as a peach but rather more amusing, stood Fanny Price.
“Hamlet!” she exclaimed.
Annandale resembled the Dane as little as he did the devil. He was fully aware of that. But he was equally aware that he must seem blue. He straightened himself and smiled. Then at once it occurred to him that Fanny might be a signal bearer.
“How do you do?” he said. “Don’t you want to come and sit on the terrace? When did you get here?”
“Just now. I am over from Newport. They told me there that I ought to come in disguise. They call it slumming.”
“Yes,” Annandale inanely and eagerly replied. Of the little speech he had caught but one word—Newport.

===Chinese===
听了这句玩笑，安南代尔转过身。那里立着范妮·普莱斯，俏如一颗桃子，却比桃子有趣得多。
「哈姆雷特！」她叫道。
安南代尔既不像那丹麦人，也不像那魔鬼。他对此一清二楚。可他同样清楚，自己看上去必是一脸忧郁。他挺直身子，笑了笑。随即他想，范妮也许是送信号的人。
「你好么？」他说，「你愿不愿意到露台上去坐坐？你什么时候到的？」
「方才到的。我从纽波特过来。他们跟我说，我该乔装而来。他们管这叫探访贫民窟。」
「是。」安南代尔痴痴地、急切地答。那一小段话他只听住一个词——纽波特。

===Original===
“Now, if I go with you, will you give me something pink, something with raspberries in it?”
Fanny, as she spoke, disengaged herself from the people with whom she had come.
“You saw Sylvia, didn’t you?” he asked, when at last through coils of girls and men they reached the terrace below.
Fanny nodded. “Suppose we sit here,” she said, indicating a table from which grew a big parasol.
“Did she say anything?”
Fanny sat down. Annandale seated himself by her. “You know? Don’t you—?”
“Oh, yes,” Fanny interrupted. “But then—”
“Then what?”
“Nothing. Only it is so much better so, don’t you think?”
“Better!” Annandale fiercely repeated.

===Chinese===
「那，我若与你同去，你可愿给我一点粉红色的东西，带覆盆子的那种？」
范妮说这话时，从与她同来的人堆里脱身出来。
「你见过西尔维娅了，是么？」他问，当他们终于穿过层层姑娘与小伙、到了下面的露台时。
范妮点点头。「我们坐这儿罢。」她说，指了指一张撑着大阳伞的桌子。
「她可说什么了？」
范妮坐下。安南代尔坐在她身旁。「你知道的，是罢——？」
「噢，是，」范妮打断，「可是——」
「可是什么？」
「没什么。只是这样更好，你不觉得么？」
「更好！」安南代尔激烈地重复。

===Original===
“Why, yes. You and Sylvia were totally unsuited for each other. She is the best and dearest girl in the world. But—here is the waiter. Will you tell him to fetch me a lemon squash?”
Annandale gave the order.
“With raspberries in it,” Fanny called at the waiter’s retreating back. “Aren’t you going to take anything?”
In deep gloom Annandale shook his head.
Fanny laughed. “Drink delights you not; no, nor woman either.”
“You see—”
“Yes, yes, yes. Of course I see. But why cannot you? Why can’t you see that you and Sylvia stood as much chance of hitting it off as though you both spoke a different language? A break was bound to come.”
But now the man appeared with the squash. Fanny looked at it. “Only two raspberries,” she cried. “And such little ones.”

===Chinese===
「可不是。你和西尔维娅彼此全不相宜。她是世上最好、最亲爱的姑娘。可是——服务员来了。你叫他去给我拿一杯柠檬苏打来好么？」
安南代尔下了单。
「加覆盆子的。」范妮朝那退去的背影叫道。「你什么也不要？」
安南代尔在深深的沮丧里摇摇头。
范妮笑了。「酒不能悦你；唉，女人也不能。」
「你瞧——」
「是、是、是。我当然瞧见了。可你为何瞧不见呢？为何你瞧不见，你和西尔维娅相处得来的指望，跟你们各说各话差不了多少？分手是注定要来的。」
可这时那人端来了苏打。范妮看着它。「才两颗覆盆子，」她叫道，「还这么小。」

===Original===
“Bring a dish of them,” said Annandale. “I suppose,” he resumed as the waiter again retreated, “I suppose she will find somebody with whom she can hit it off.”
“Yes, of course. There is me and there are other girls. But the men will be few. They will be elderly, I think, and I think, too, tame enough to eat out of her hand.”
“You think, then, that I am out of the running?”
Fanny did not answer. She was drinking the squash. When she put it down she put with it the subject. It bored her.
“Are you going to be here long?” she asked. Until a moment before Annandale had been wavering. But now his mind was made up. Or he thought it was.
“No. I am off tomorrow.”
“Where to?”
“The North Woods, perhaps. I am not sure.”
“If you are not sure, you cannot be in any very tearing haste. Why not stop a day or two longer and take me about?”

===Chinese===
「来一碟。」安南代尔说。「我想，」服务员再退去时他续道，「我想她会找到一个相处得来的人的。」
「自然。有我，还有别的姑娘。可男人会少。会是上了年纪的，我想，而且我想，也会温驯得任她摆在手心里。」
「那么你觉得我已经出局了？」
范妮没答。她正喝着苏打。放下杯子，她也把这一话题放下了。它叫她腻烦。
「你要在这儿久留么？」她问。方才安南代尔还在犹豫。可此刻他主意已定。或者说，他以为已定。
「不。我明日便走。」
「去哪儿？」
「也许去北林（North Woods）。不一定。」
「你若不一定，便不会太急着赶路。何不多留一两日，带我四处转转？」

===Original===
Annandale looked at her. In the look was surprise; inquiry, too.
“Yes. Why not?”
Annandale’s look deepened into a stare.
“Now, don’t be stupid,” said Fanny, to whom such stares were familiar. “I am not trying to get up a flirtation with you. But I must have someone to talk to.”
“I like to hear you talk.”
“Yes; men always like nonsense.”
“Only from a pretty girl, though.”
“Do you know,” said Fanny, rising from beneath the big parasol, “the waiter didn’t bring the raspberries. No matter now, though. I must go and find mother. This is no place for her to be out alone.”

===Chinese===
安南代尔看着她。那目光里有惊讶；也有探究。
「是啊。有何不可？」
安南代尔的目光加深成了凝视。
「哎，别犯傻，」范妮说，她对此类凝视司空见惯，「我不是要跟你调情。可我总得有个人说话。」
「我喜欢听你说话。」
「是；男人总爱听胡话。」
「只限从漂亮姑娘嘴里说出来。」
「你可知，」范妮从大阳伞下站起身道，「那服务员没把覆盆子端来。不过此刻也无所谓了。我得去找母亲了。这地方不是她一个人独自出来的去处。」

## VIII Two in a Turret / VIII 塔中二人

===Original===
From a back gallery of the Casino a narrow stair leads to a tower. Up that stair Annandale one afternoon invited Fanny Price.

A fortnight had gone, two weeks of dressing and undressing, of dinners, dances and dips; a succession of mellow mornings; long, green afternoons, dusks stabbed by sudden stars and nights lit by a moon that painted the ocean, penetrated the shadows, checkered the underbrush with silver spots.

But now, though the mornings were as mellow and the afternoons as green, though in the air the same madness subsisted and the nights were as languid as before, verandas were emptying, there were wide spaces where once were thick crowds. The end of the season had come.

===Chinese===
从卡西诺（Casino）的后廊，一道窄梯通向一座塔楼。某日下午，安南代尔便是沿着这道梯，邀请范妮·普莱斯上去的。

两个星期过去了——两周的更衣与卸妆，晚宴、舞会与海水浴交相轮转；一连串醇厚的清晨；悠长、葱郁的午后；被骤然冒出的星辰刺破的暮霭；还有被月亮照亮的夜晚——那月光描摹着海洋，穿透暗影，在矮树丛间撒下斑驳的银点。

可如今，尽管清晨依旧醇厚，午后依旧葱郁，尽管空气中依旧弥漫着那股狂热，夜晚也同先前一般慵懒，游廊却在一点点空下去，从前人潮摩肩接踵之处，如今已是空旷一片。社交季到了尾声。

===Original===
In the procession of these things Annandale had put the North Woods from him; he had put, too, the thought of journeying abroad. With them he had put also any hope that Sylvia would signal him back.

For awhile the hope had persisted, as the light of a candle persists. Then it had dwindled, flickered and sunk. That is the way with hope. Though sometimes it is snuffed. You are in darkness. But through that darkness occasionally another light will be upheld. It may not, perhaps, be intended for you, but it may enable you to see.

Aided by another light, Annandale had begun to discern his way. He should, of course, have remained in darkness. To darkness, were this fiction, he would be condemned. But this is not fiction. The drama with which these pages deal is documented from life. It was Fanny who held the light.

===Chinese===
在这一切的流转之中，安南代尔已把北部林区抛在脑后；他也打消了出洋远行的念头。连同这两者一并抛开的，还有西尔维娅会向他发出回头的暗号那一丝指望。

这指望曾持续过一段时日，像烛光那样执拗。随后它渐弱、摇曳、沉了下去。指望向来如此。不过有时它会被一把掐灭。你于是坠入黑暗。可透过那黑暗，偶尔会有另一盏灯举起来。它或许并非为你而举，却也许能让你看见些什么。

借着另一盏灯的光，安南代尔已开始辨出自己的路。当然，按理他本该留在黑暗里。若这是一部小说，他必会被判回黑暗之中。然而这不是小说。本书所载的那出戏，桩桩件件皆有生活为证。举着那盏灯的，是范妮。

===Original===
During the month that had gone he had been almost constantly at her side. The fact that one light may be replaced by another had not at first occurred to him. Presently the ease with which such substitution can be effected had mystified him very much. He was not prepared for anything of the kind. He had arranged to be a gloomy, disappointed man. He kept telling himself that if Sylvia had stuck to him he would have been true to her his whole life through. But she had not stuck to him, and the withdrawal of herself had left existence so empty that, unknown even to him, Nature had been filling the vacuum which she abhors.

In this, Nature had been greatly aided. Fanny Price was a remarkably fetching young girl. To a man out of court and consequently out of sorts the companionship of a pocket Venus is tonifying in the extreme. It is not merely that, it is recuperative. It banishes the blues. It establishes a new court, and with it a new code of its own.

The censorious allege that this is all wrong. It may be that they are right. But Nature is not censorious. Nature is not even ethical. She has no standards of right, no canons of wrong. What she does have is her way. A saint may defy her. Annandale was not that by a long shot. He was simply a human being, one that had been punished, and, as he thought, unjustly punished, for that which might have been condoned. Injustice humiliates. Saints may welcome humiliation, but human beings resent it.

===Chinese===
过去这一个月里，他几乎无时无刻不待在她身边。一盏灯竟能由另一盏替代——这个事实起初并未进入他的意识。不久，这种替代竟能如此轻而易举地完成，反倒叫他大为迷惑。他对这种事毫无准备。他原本已安排自己去做一个阴郁、失意的人。他不断对自己说，倘若西尔维娅不曾离弃他，他必会对她忠贞一生。可她到底离弃了他，而她的抽身离去使生活变得如此空旷，以至于连他自己都未察觉，造化便已动手填满那片它所厌恶的真空。

在这件事上，造化得到了极大的助力。范妮·普莱斯是个极为动人的姑娘。对一个被爱之法庭逐出、因而也意兴阑珊的男人而言，一位袖珍维纳斯（pocket Venus）的陪伴是大有裨补的。不仅止于此，它还能使人复原。它驱散愁绪。它另立一座新的法庭，随之而来的，是它自有的一套新律法。

好发议论的人指斥这全然不对。也许他们是对的。可造化并不好发议论。造化甚至连道德都谈不上。她没有是非的标准，没有善恶的典则。她所有的只是她自己的路。圣徒或许能抗拒她。安南代尔则差得远。他不过是个凡人，一个受过罚的人，而且自以为是受了不当之罚——为那本可宽宥之事。不公使人蒙羞。圣徒或许甘愿受辱，可凡人只会愤懑。

===Original===
Over the emptiness which Sylvia had created there brooded therefore two things. One was darkness, the other pique. In the light which Fanny upheld it seemed to Annandale that they might be dispersed. This idea, which he regarded as his very own, and consequently as highly original, was not his in the least. It was Nature prompting him to fill the vacuum which she so dislikes.

Instigated by her, Annandale invited Fanny up a stair and into a tower, a place remote, aloof, furnished with seats for just two.

Fanny had not been there before. She had heard, though, of its aloofness; it was regarded as a dangerous spot. But Fanny was a brave girl. Besides, Annandale was at his worst, and even at his best was not very alarming.

===Chinese===
因此，在西尔维娅所造成的空旷之上，盘踞着两样东西。一样是黑暗，另一样是怨愤。在范妮所举的灯光里，安南代尔觉得它们也许能被驱散。这个念头，他视作自己独有的、因而也极富创见，实则与他毫不相干。那不过是造化在怂恿他去填满她所厌恶的那片真空。

受她怂恿，安南代尔请范妮沿梯登上一座塔——一个偏僻、幽绝的去处，里面只备了刚好容两人的座席。

范妮从前没来过这里。不过她听说过它的幽僻；人们把它看作一个危险的去处。可范妮是个有胆量的姑娘。何况安南代尔正处于最不成样子的状态，而即便是他状态最好的时候，也并不可怕。

===Original===
The ascent effected, Fanny peeped from a casement. “Why,” she exclaimed, “you can see everywhere!” She looked about. “But no one can see you.”

Assured of that, she produced a little gold box. On the back were her initials in jewels. She opened it, took a cigarette and lit it. “Will you have one?” she asked.

“This is a deuced nice case,” said Annandale.

Fanny puffed and smiled.

===Chinese===
登上去之后，范妮从一扇窗扉向外张望了一下。「哎呀，」她叫道，「这里哪儿都看得见！」她四下环顾。「可谁也看不见你。」

确信如此之后，她摸出一只小小的金匣。匣背上用珠宝镶着她的姓名首字母。她打开匣子，取出一支烟点上。「你也来一支？」她问。

「这匣子精致得要命，」安南代尔说。

范妮吐了口烟，笑了笑。

===Original===
“A present, I suppose.”

“Yes. But you must not ask from whom.”

Annandale looked out at the landscape, then in at the girl. “There is something else I want to ask.”

So grave was his tone that Fanny deployed for action.

“Will you marry me?”

===Chinese===
「是别人送的礼物吧。」

「是啊。不过你可不许问是谁送的。」

安南代尔望了望外面的景色，又转而望向这姑娘。「我还想问另一件事。」

他语气如此郑重，范妮便摆好了应战的架势。

「你愿意嫁给我吗？」

===Original===
Though Fanny had deployed, the shot bowled her over. Into one of the chairs she dropped. Already Annandale had captured the other.

“Will you?”

But Fanny was recovering. With an air of vexation in which there was amusement, she puffed at her cigarette and then at him.

“Now, honestly, have I ever given you the slightest encouragement to ask me that?” She hesitated a moment, puffed again and added: “We have been friends, I think; let us remain so.”

===Chinese===
范妮虽已摆好架势，这一炮还是把她击翻了。她一屁股坐进其中一张椅子。安南代尔则已抢先占了另一张。

「你愿意吗？」

但范妮正缓过劲来。她带着一种恼怒中掺着趣味的神情，朝烟喷了一口，又朝他喷了一口。

「说真的，我可曾给过你一丁点儿鼓励，让你来问这话？」她顿了一顿，又吸了一口烟，接着说，「我们一直是朋友，我想；就让我们这样下去吧。」

===Original===
Annandale, who was in loose white flannels, contemplated his tight white shoes. Then his eyes sought hers. “Are you interested in Loftus?”

“That is none of your business,” Fanny proudly and promptly replied. As she spoke she got from her seat, approached the casement, gazed out and away.

“I do not believe you are,” Annandale announced to her slender waist. “But if I am wrong, it is hardly disloyalty to him to say that he is not good enough for you.”

===Chinese===
安南代尔穿着宽松的白法兰绒衣裤，此时端详起自己紧绷的白鞋。然后他的目光去寻她的眼睛。「你对洛夫特斯有意？」

「这不干你的事，」范妮骄傲而利落地答道。说话间她从座上起身，走到窗扉前，凝神向外、向远处望去。

「我不信你有，」安南代尔对她那纤细的腰说道。「不过就算我错了，说他配不上你，也算不得对他不忠。」

===Original===
Beneath the tower was a tennis court. Fanny made a face at it. But the face must have been insufficient. Looking over her shoulder at Annandale, she showed her teeth.

“Do you fancy a girl cares for a man because he is or is not good enough? When a girl cares she cares because she cannot help herself.”

“I know that is the way with a man, or at least with me. I cannot help caring for you.”

“Nor could you help caring for Sylvia.”

===Chinese===
塔楼底下是一片网球场。范妮朝它做了个鬼脸。可这鬼脸想必还不够。她回过头来望着安南代尔，龇了龇牙。

「你以为一个姑娘在乎一个男人，是因为他配得上还是配不上吗？姑娘一旦动了情，那是因为她自己把持不住。」

「我知道男人正是这样，至少我就是这样。我把持不住自己不去在乎你。」

「你把持不住自己不去在乎西尔维娅，也是一样。」

===Original===
“She is so different.”

“Yes,” said Fanny dreamily, “and so are you.” Though to whom she referred she did not say, nor did Annandale ask. She gave him no chance. “Next month you will not be able to help caring for some other girl.”

“Not if you would take me.”

“But, you see, I don’t care for you.”

“But couldn’t you?” Annandale persisted. “Couldn’t you if you tried? Of course, in saying that Loftus is not good enough for you I don’t mean that I am. But if you could try I would.”

===Chinese===
「她是那样不同。」

「是啊，」范妮如梦似幻地说，「你也是。」至于她说的是谁跟谁不同，她没有说，安南代尔也没有问。她也没给他问的机会。「下个月，你又会把持不住自己去在乎别的什么姑娘。」

「只要你能接纳我，就不会。」

「可是你瞧，我并不在乎你。」

「可你难道不能吗？」安南代尔执拗道。「你若试一试，难道不能吗？当然，我说洛夫特斯配不上你，并不等于说我配得上。可你若肯试，我亦肯。」

===Original===
At this program Fanny laughed. “We should be a pair of Christian Endeavorers, shouldn’t we?”

To the levity of that Annandale found no immediate reply. Yet presently, with an irrelevance more obvious than real, he threw out: “He has gone abroad, you know.”

“Who? Loftus?”

“Yes, for a year, I believe.”

===Chinese===
听了这番宏图，范妮笑了起来。「我们俩倒要做一对『基督教奋进会』（Christian Endeavorers）的会员了，是不是？」

对这份轻佻，安南代尔一时找不出话来回。然而过了一会儿，他抛出一句——其不着边际是表面多过实质：「他出国去了，你是知道的。」

「谁？洛夫特斯？」

「嗯，去了大概一年，我想。」

===Original===
Fanny turned to the tennis court again. It was, though, not that which she saw, but a hope that was slipping away, sinking away, sinking down into death dishonored. For a moment she was very still. A movement of Annandale’s aroused her.

“Come,” she said. “It is hot here. Let us go.”

Gathering a fold of her skirt, Fanny descended the stair. Annandale filed after. On a balcony below a lady with faded hair and gimlet eyes pounced at her.

===Chinese===
范妮又转向那片网球场。然而她所见到的并非球场，而是一个正在溜走的指望——它不断下沉、下沉，沉入一桩不名誉的死亡。有一瞬她一动不动。安南代尔的一个动作惊醒了她。

「走吧，」她说，「这里太热了。我们下去。」

范妮提起裙裾的一道褶，下了楼梯。安南代尔鱼贯跟在后面。在下面一道阳台上，一位头发褪色、目光如锥的妇人向她扑了过来。

===Original===
“I have been hunting for you everywhere,” the lady exclaimed. “Aren’t you going to dress?” Then she nodded to Annandale.

Annandale touched his cap. “How do you do, Mrs. Price?”

He would have lingered, but Fanny dismissed him.

“Goodbye,” she said. “I may see you this evening.”

===Chinese===
「我到处找你，」那妇人叫道，「你还不去换装？」接着她朝安南代尔点了点头。

安南代尔触了触帽檐。「您好，普莱斯太太？」

他本想再多留一会儿，却被范妮打发走了。

「再见，」她说，「今晚或许能见到你。」

===Original===
As he ambled off Mrs. Price returned to the charge. “Where have you been?”

Fanny patted a yawn. “Listening to sweet nothings.”

“From him? Why, he hasn’t anything, has he? What did you do?”

Fanny patted another yawn or else another sigh. “I fell on his neck and sobbed for joy.”

===Chinese===
待他踱着步走开，普莱斯太太又卷土重来。「你上哪儿去了？」

范妮拍了拍一个呵欠。「在听些甜言蜜语。」

「听他说？嗳，他可没什么钱哪，是吧？你都做了些什么？」

范妮又拍了拍一个呵欠——也许是一声叹息。「我扑到他脖子上，喜极而泣。」

===Original===
“Nonsense. Has he anything, tell me?”

“Not enough to entertain on. Twenty-five thousand a year, I think.”

“The impertinence of it!” said the lady.

Had her daughter been an heiress a duke would hardly have satisfied her. As things were, or more exactly, since the girl began to grow in beauty she had dreamed for her but one dream—a brilliant match. To Mrs. Price there could be no brilliance if the party of the second part had a dollar less than ten million.

===Chinese===
「胡说。他到底有没有钱，快告诉我？」

「不够拿来摆阔请客。一年两万五千，我想。」

「真是不知天高地厚！」妇人说。

倘若她女儿是个继承人，怕是一位公爵都难填她的胃口。按眼前的情形，或者更确切地说，自从这姑娘的姿色一日日盛起来，她便只替她做过一个梦——一门显赫的亲事。在普莱斯太太看来，婚约的另一方若少了一千万，便算不得什么显赫。

===Original===
“You might have had Loftus,” she declared at last. “Where is he, do you know?”

“Abroad, I hear.”

“With that creature?”

Mrs. Price in common with many others had heard of Marie Leroy. But though others in hearing had not heeded, Mrs. Price took it as a personal affront.

“Then it is your fault,” she snarled. “You could have had him if you had wanted. Don’t tell me. He was in love with you. I could see it.”

===Chinese===
「你本可以拿下洛夫特斯的，」她终于宣告，「他在哪儿，你可知道？」

「听说出国去了。」

「跟那个东西一起？」

普莱斯太太和许多人一样，听说过玛丽·勒鲁瓦（Marie Leroy）。旁人听见了未必上心，普莱斯太太却把它视作对个人的冒犯。

「那就是你的不是了，」她咬牙道，「你想要他的话，本可以要到手的。别跟我分辩。他爱慕你。我看得出来。」

===Original===
Fanny was looking at the ocean. A white sail was fainting in the distance. Like it, a hope she had had was fading away. She watched it go. It had been very fair, very dear, more dear and fair than any she had known. But it was going. It was out of reach and now out of sight. She could not beckon to it.

“What are you staring at?” Mrs. Price asked.

“A sail out there,” the girl answered.

Then presently mother and daughter passed into an adjoining corridor where they had rooms.

===Chinese===
范妮正望着大海。远处一面白帆正一点点淡去。像它一样，她曾抱有的一个指望也在消逝。她看着它远去。它原是那样美好、那样珍贵，比她所见过的任何指望都更珍贵、更美好。可它正在远去。它已触它不到，如今也看不见了。她无法再向它招手。

「你盯着看什么？」普莱斯太太问。

「外头的一面帆，」姑娘答道。

随后不久，母女俩便走进相邻的一道走廊——她们的房间就在那里。

## IX Fanny Changes Her Clothes / IX 范妮换了衣裳

===Original===
Fanny did not appear that evening. In search of her Annandale prowled vainly around. But on the morrow he ran into her on the beach.

It was still as fine as powder. To have found elbow room there a few days previous you would have had to go out to sea. Now, in and on it children were making hillocks and holes. Near them a few groups of older people loitered. But the coryphées that had danced there were migrating. Already the Rockingham, a big hotel which faced the beach, had closed. Sweet-and-twenty was packing her trunk.

The morning itself was of the quality which Lowell has catalogued as from the Gulf adrift. In the air was a caress. Fanny, in a frock the color of pale pastel pink, a wide hat in which that color was repeated, her eyes blue as the sea and bluer, added to its charm.

===Chinese===
那天晚上范妮没有露面。安南代尔四处转悠找她，徒劳无功。可第二天清晨，他在海滩上撞见了她。

沙滩仍像粉末一般细软。几日前，若想在那上面觅得一处容身之所，你怕是要一直走到海里去。如今，孩子们在沙里堆起小丘、挖出小坑。在他们附近，几簇上了年纪的人在那里闲荡。然而曾在那儿翩翩起舞的舞女们，正像候鸟般纷纷离去。面朝海滩的大旅店罗金厄姆（Rockingham）已经关门歇业。那娇艳的「二十芳龄」正在收拾她的衣箱。

这早晨本身，正属洛厄尔（Lowell）所归类的「自墨西哥湾漂流而来」的那种天气。空气里带着一缕抚慰。范妮穿一件淡粉彩颜色的衣裙，戴一顶宽檐帽，帽上重复着那一抹颜色，她的眼睛蓝得像海，且比海更蓝——这一切为清晨平添了几分风韵。

===Original===
As Annandale approached she smiled and gave him a finger. But at once the smile fell from her. With the finger which he had released she pointed at the big hotel. Annandale turned. Other people were turning. Some were running. A child that had been at play in the sand jumped and clapped his hands. About one side of the hotel a sheet of flame was climbing, crackling in and out. A cry of “Fire!” caught up and renewed, mounted in the crystalline air.

“Damn!” said Annandale. “If that goes—”

Fanny said nothing. Her eyes widened. Through the windows that front the beach more flames were leaping. From the side the first flames passed to shops over the way, passed back with fresh ones created and joined the others beyond. Above was smoke. Higher yet the tender blue of the sky. But below was a whirlwind of ochre, scarlet and gamboge, a fierce yet compact tornado of oscillant hues, shot with green and shuttled with black. Then suddenly, with a roar, the tornado doubled, the roof had fallen. The child that had jumped and clapped his hands, feebly now was beginning to cry.

“It is glorious,” said Fanny.

===Chinese===
安南代尔走近时，她笑了笑，递给他一根手指。可那笑容立刻从她脸上落下。她用他刚刚松开的那根手指，指向那家大旅店。安南代尔转过身去。旁人也在转。有人开始跑。一个方才在沙里玩耍的孩子又蹦又拍手。只见一道火舌沿着旅店的一侧攀升，噼啪作响地蹿进蹿出。「失火啦！」一声呐喊被接起、被反复传递，在澄澈的空气里升腾。

「该死！」安南代尔道，「要是那火——」

范妮没作声。她的眼睛睁大了。透过朝向海滩的那排窗子，又有火焰蹿跃出来。从侧面，最初的火舌蔓延到路对面的店铺，又反扑回来，卷起新的火舌，与更远处另一些汇合。上面是烟。再往上，是天空柔嫩的蓝。可下面，是赭石、猩红与藤黄交织的旋风，一团凶猛却紧密的龙卷，色调摇摆不定，时而闪出绿光，时而穿插着黑影。接着，随着一声轰响，那龙卷对折起来——屋顶塌了。方才又蹦又拍手的那个孩子，如今有气无力地哭了起来。

「真壮观，」范妮说。

===Original===
“I am afraid—” Annandale muttered.

Fanny glanced at him. Yet at once she understood. On the other side of the hotel, across the road, the Casino stood. Her mother, of course, would be safe. But her clothes! At thought of them her hand went to her throat.

“Do you think the Casino will catch?” she gasped.

Annandale nodded.

“Oh,” she continued, “I shan’t have a stitch, not one.”

“Yes, you shall,” Annandale heroically retorted. “I will see to them. But I must run. Find your mother if you can and take her to the Inn.”

===Chinese===
「我怕——」安南代尔喃喃道。

范妮瞥了他一眼。可她立刻就明白了。旅店的另一侧，隔着马路，便是卡西诺。她母亲当然会平安无事。可她的衣裳！一念及此，她的手便抬到了喉头。

「你看卡西诺会不会着起来？」她喘着气问。

安南代尔点了点头。

「哎呀，」她接着说，「那我可就连一针一线都没有了，一针一线都没有。」

「不，你会有，」安南代尔英雄气概十足地回道，「我去办。可我得赶紧跑一趟。你若找得到你母亲，就把她带到客栈（Inn）去。」

===Original===
The Inn, a hotel half a mile away, was where Annandale lodged. At once he was off. Shortly, by a detour, he got to the other side of the fire. As he swung about he saw that the Casino’s ballroom had caught. But that part of the place was of wood. The other end, where Fanny lodged, was of wood also, but it was also partly of stone. To this part as yet the flames had not reached.

As Annandale ran he told himself that he would have time to get in and get out, but he told himself too that it was a ridiculous job. Fanny’s clothes a stroke of his pen could replace. But now the crowd impeded him. Lines had formed. Buckets were being passed. There were throngs of natives and resorters. Through them he pushed.

===Chinese===
那客栈是半英里外的一家旅店，安南代尔便下榻于此。他立刻飞奔而去。不久，他绕了个弯，赶到火场的另一侧。他一转身，便看见卡西诺的舞厅已经着了。不过那一边的建筑是木结构。范妮所住的另一端也是木结构，但有一部分是石头的。火焰尚未烧到这一部分。

安南代尔一边跑，一边对自己说，他来得及冲进去再冲出来，可他也对自己说，这差事实在荒唐。范妮的衣裳，他大笔一挥就能补上。然而此刻人群挡住了他的去路。人们已排成人链，水桶正一桶桶传过去。本地的居民和避暑的游客挤成一团。他从他们中间挤过去。

===Original===
At the further entrance to the Casino, above which he knew the Prices lodged, a fat policeman stood, blocking the way. Annandale shoved him aside, sprang up the stairs, reached the room, fumbled with the door. It was locked.

Annandale swore deeply, tried the door with his shoulder, kicked at it till it cracked, kicked again, throwing himself against it with all his weight, then, not the door, but the fastenings of the lock broke and he went sprawling in. Through the open window he could see the flames, he could hear them, he could hear too the cries of the crowd. But he had no time to waste. He tore around the room.

In one corner was a deep closet, full of clothes. He took them and threw them in armfuls on the bed. In another corner was a bureau, the drawers packed with scented lingerie. These on the bed he emptied also. What else did women wear? he wondered. Oh, yes, he remembered; hats certainly and probably shoes. Around the room he tore again. But already the bed was mountainous. He turned it all over on the floor, gathered up as much as the coverlid would hold and made a hasty bundle of it. Beneath was a blanket; he filled that, made a bundle of it also, repeated the operation with a sheet. Into another sheet he threw hats which meanwhile had loomed in boxes on a shelf, and dragging a curtain down filled that with shoes which also he had found, changed his mind and stuffed them into a pillow case, tossing in after them articles from a dressing-table, brushes and combs, odds and ends, helter skelter.

===Chinese===
在卡西诺另一头的入口处——他知道普莱斯母女便住在这上面——一个胖警察站着堵住了去路。安南代尔一把将他推开，三步并作两步冲上楼，奔到那间房前，去拧门把手。门锁着。

安南代尔狠狠骂了一声，用肩去撞门，一脚脚踹过去，直踹得门板喀嚓作响，再踹，把全身重量都压上去——终于，不是门，而是锁扣断了，他一头栽了进去。从敞开的窗口望出去，能看见火焰，听得见火焰的声音，也听得见人群的喊叫。可他没工夫耽搁。他在屋里四处翻找起来。

一个角落里有只深壁橱，塞满了衣裳。他把它们一抱一抱地抱出来，扔到床上。另一个角落里有只五斗橱，抽屉里塞满了熏香的贴身衣物。他把这些也一股脑儿倒在床上。女人还要穿戴什么？他想。哦，对了，他想起来了；帽子肯定要，鞋子大约也要。他又在屋里团团转起来。可床上已经堆成了小山。他把这一切翻倒在地板上，抓起床罩能兜住的那许多，匆匆打成一个包袱。底下有条毯子；他把毯子装满，又打成一个包袱，又拿一条床单照方抓药。他把架子上几个盒子里冒出来的帽子扔进另一条床单；又把窗帘扯下来，去装他搜出来的鞋子，转念一想，改塞进一个枕套，随手又把梳妆台上的零碎——刷子、梳子、杂七杂八——一股脑儿往里扔，乱作一团。

===Original===
But in dragging the curtain from the window he had noticed a writing-desk. After he had finished with the pillow case he returned to it. Like the door it was locked. He kicked at it, kicked it open, discovered in it loose money and trinkets, stuck them in his pockets, grabbed at the bundles and dashed from the room just as with a roar the flames leaped in.

In the corridor he tripped, but he was up again with the tightly tied bundles and down the stair before the flames and the smoke of them could catch him. Once on the road without he turned to look, but the flames pirouetting in increasing size made it too hot to linger. Down the road he went, not overweighted but impeded by the awkward bundles, and staggered first into an engulfing, shouting crowd, then into a convenient hack, in which he reached the Inn, minus his cap and perspiring profusely.

The Prices as yet had not turned up. Annandale secured rooms for them, had the bundles taken there, went to his own quarters, reemerged shortly fresh as paint, hungry as a wolf.

===Chinese===
可当初扯下窗帘时，他留意到一张写字台。收拾完枕套，他折回到它跟前。和那扇门一样，它也锁着。他一脚踹开，发现里面有零散的钱币和首饰，便一概塞进口袋，抓起那几个包袱，冲出房门——就在这一刻，火焰轰的一声蹿了进来。

走廊里他绊了一跤，可他抱着捆得紧紧的包袱立刻爬起，在火焰与浓烟追上他之前冲下了楼梯。一到外面的路上，他回头看了一眼，可那越旋越大的火舌已烫得人没法停留。他顺着路往下走，分量倒不很重，只是那几个累赘的包袱碍手碍脚，先撞进了一团呼喊的人海，继而又撞进一辆凑巧停着的马车，就这么回到了客栈——帽子丢了，汗如雨下。

普莱斯母女尚未露面。安南代尔替她们订下房间，让人把包袱送上去，回自己屋里一钻，不多时又现身——容光焕发，饿得像头狼。

===Original===
It was high noon. From beyond drifted the sound of cries, the smell of smoke, the commotion of flight. The Rockingham had gone, the adjacent shops and bath houses with it; the Casino had fallen. Hurrying to the railway station beyond came people with handbags, wagons with trunks. From the air the caress had passed. There was panic in it.

But presently the flames showed less voluminous. After devouring all that they conveniently could they were subsiding. It was apparent that the worst was over. Then at last Fanny and her mother drove up.

From the veranda where he stood Annandale ran down to meet them. “I have your things,” he cried. “I have rooms for you also.”

===Chinese===
正午时分。从远处飘来阵阵呼喊声、烟火气，以及逃难的骚动。罗金厄姆没了，相邻的店铺与浴场也一并葬身火海；卡西诺也坍塌了。人们拎着手提包、驾着载衣箱的马车，匆匆往那头的火车站赶去。空气中那一缕抚慰已荡然无存。空气里弥漫着惊惶。

但不多时，火焰便不再那样铺天盖地。把唾手可得的一切吞咽殆尽之后，它们渐渐平息下来。最坏的情形显然已过去。终于，范妮和她母亲乘车赶到了。

安南代尔从他所站的游廊奔下去迎她们。「你们的东西在我这儿，」他喊道，「我也替你们订好了房间。」

===Original===
“Hobson is not in it with you,” said Fanny, when the tale of the bundles had been told. “I could kiss you. I would if mamma were not here.”

For that, ordinarily, Fanny would have been promptly sat upon. But here was the exceptional. Mrs. Price recognized it or appeared to. Instead of rebuking the girl and snubbing the man, Mrs. Price condescended to tell Annandale that he was “too good.”

This was very nice. Annandale felt over-rewarded. Then, shortly, the midday meal ensuing, he conducted mother and daughter to the restaurant, sat with them at table, ordered Ruinart cup and assumed family airs. Later, in a motor, he took Fanny to view the ruins, hummed her over the country and later still procured for her a lemon squash with plenty of raspberries in it, which she consumed on the porch, to the sound of the waves, by the light of the stars.

===Chinese===
「霍布森（Hobson）也敌不过你，」听罢了那一串包袱的来历，范妮说道，「我真想吻你。要不是妈妈在，我早吻了。」

搁在寻常时候，这话准得让范妮立刻被一顿训斥按下去。可眼下是例外。普莱斯太太也认出了这一点——至少装作认出。她没有斥责女儿、冷落那男子，反而屈尊对安南代尔说了一句「你太好了」。

这真是再好不过。安南代尔觉得自己受之有愧。随后不久，午饭时分到了，他引领这对母女去餐厅，陪她们入座，点了一壶 Ruinart 香槟潘趣，端出了一副家人的架势。再后来，他坐汽车带范妮去看了废墟，开车载她在乡间兜了一圈，再再后来，又给她弄来一杯加足了覆盆子的柠檬汽水，她就着海浪声、就着星光，在门廊上把它喝掉了。

===Original===
Meanwhile she had changed her pastel frock for another, which, if a bit rumpled in transit, became her wonderfully well.

Annandale commented on it. “By the way,” he suddenly interrupted himself to remark, “I have more of your things. I stuffed them in my pocket and forgot them entirely. I will go and fetch them now.”

“Don’t bother. Tomorrow will do. What are they, do you remember?”

“Money and jewelry. Rings and pins, I think. I am sure there were pins. One of them stuck in me.”

===Chinese===
这其间，她已把那件粉彩衣裙换成了另一件——这衣裳虽在搬运途中略微压皱了些，穿在她身上却再相宜不过。

安南代尔对此夸赞了几句。「对了，」他忽然话锋一转，「你还有别的东西在我手上。我揣进兜里，忘得一干二净。我这就去拿来。」

「别麻烦了。明天也成。是什么，你记得吗？」

「钱和首饰。戒指和别针，我想。我敢肯定有别针——其中一枚还扎了我一下。」

===Original===
“Any clothes?”

“Clothes!” Annandale echoed in surprise. “Why, no, are any missing?”

“My mother’s. They were in the room next to mine.”

“The Lord forgive me, I never thought of it.”

“It does not really matter. Only we will have to go to town tomorrow. Mamma has not a stitch.”

“The devil!” muttered Annandale in fierce self-reprobation. “Hang my stupidity. I am a fool.”

“You are nothing of the kind. If it were not for you I would not have a stitch either.”

===Chinese===
「衣裳呢？」

「衣裳！」安南代尔惊讶地应道，「哎呀，没拿，可有遗失？」

「我母亲的。它们就放在我隔壁那间屋子里。」

「主啊宽恕我，我压根儿没想到。」

「不打紧。只是我们明天不得不进城一趟了。妈妈连一针一线都没有了。」

「见鬼！」安南代尔狠狠地自我痛骂，「我这蠢劲儿！我是个傻瓜。」

「你才不是。要不是你，我也连一针一线都不剩了。」

===Original===
“That is all very well. But I have bungled matters dreadfully. I don’t know what your mother can think of me. I do know, though, that I wish she would let me replace the things which she has lost through my fault.”

In the sky a star was falling, swiftly, silently, like a drop of water on a windowpane. Fanny watched it. She had been lolling back in a chair. But at Annandale’s suggestion she sat up. “That is absurd,” she announced.

“Well, then, it would be only nice and fair of you to put me in a position where, without offense, I could do so.”

But Fanny was rising. “It is late,” she announced. “I must go.”

Annandale caught at her. “Say ‘Yes,’ ” he implored. “Or at least don’t say ‘No.’ Say something.”

“Something, then. There, let me be.”

At that Annandale, who still held her, held her yet tighter. “You are the dearest girl in all the world.”

===Chinese===
「话是这么说。可我把事情办砸得太厉害。我不知道你母亲会怎么想我。我倒确知，我盼着她能让我替她补上那些因我之过而损失的东西。」

天上有一颗星正坠落下来，飞快地、悄无声息地，像一滴水打在窗玻璃上。范妮望着它。她方才一直懒洋洋地仰靠在椅子上。可一听安南代尔这话，她便坐直了。「那太荒唐了，」她宣告。

「那么，你若能让我处在一个名正言顺、不至冒犯的位置去做这件事，那才公道，才体贴。」

可范妮已站起身。「不早了，」她宣布，「我得走了。」

安南代尔一把拉住她。「说『好』，」他恳求道，「至少别说『不』。说点儿什么吧。」

「那么，说点儿什么。好了，放开我。」

安南代尔仍抓着她，闻言把她抓得更紧了。「你是这世上最最可爱的姑娘。」

===Original===
Fanny gave him a little shove. “Don’t do that, anyone might see you.”

“Yes, and see too that you belong to me.”

“I am not so sure.”

“You shan’t go then till you are.” Annandale, as he spoke, planted himself uncircuitously before her.

“Oh,” said Fanny, in a little sugary, demure voice, “if you are going to use brute force—”

“I am.”

“Then I give in.”

“For keeps?”

“Don’t, there’s my mother.”

===Chinese===
范妮轻轻推了他一下。「别这样，会被人瞧见的。」

「是啊，也会瞧见你是属于我的。」

「我可说不准。」

「那你就别想走，等你拿准了再说。」安南代尔一边说，一边径直挡在了她面前。

「哦，」范妮用一种甜腻腻、矜持的声调说，「你要是打算动粗——」

「我是要动粗。」

「那我投降。」

「永久投降？」

「别，我妈妈在那儿。」

===Original===
In the doorway beyond, Mrs. Price had loomed. Fanny joined her. Annandale followed, denouncing himself to the lady for the oversight that noon. Yet, whether because of that oversight of his or because of some foresight of her own, so grim was Mrs. Price that Annandale, concluding that it would be more cheerful elsewhere, turned tail, ambled out to the road and across it to the sea wall, where he sat and kicked his heels and told himself that he was engaged.

In the telling he lost himself in impossibilities and wondered how it would fare with him and how with Sylvia could the past be mended and the old plans mature. For though Fanny allured, Sylvia enchained. Fanny was delicious. But he fancied that other men had found her so. He fancied that her heart had been an inn, and he knew that Sylvia’s was a home. Yet from that he was barred. To those that lack homes hotels are convenient.

===Chinese===
在更远处的门口，普莱斯太太的身影出现了。范妮走过去与她汇合。安南代尔跟在后面，为当日中午的疏忽向夫人不住自责。然而——不知是因他这疏忽，还是因夫人另有某种先见之明——普莱斯太太面色阴沉，安南代尔断定别处总比这里痛快，便掉头溜了出去，踱到路上，穿过马路来到防波堤前，在那里坐下，踢着脚后跟，暗自对自己说：他订婚了。

这样对自己说时，他迷失在种种不可能里，想着若往昔得以弥补、旧日计划得以成熟，他与西尔维娅的结局又当如何。因为范妮虽迷人，西尔维娅却将他牢牢缚住。范妮是甜蜜的。可他想象别的男人也曾尝过这甜。他想象她的心曾是一家客栈，而他深知西尔维娅的心是一个家。然而那个家已把他拒之门外。对无家可归者而言，旅店倒是方便。

===Original===
Across the way meanwhile Mrs. Price was very busy. In looming on the veranda it had seemed to her that her daughter and that man were occupied with certain ceremonies. Regarding them she attacked the girl at once.

“You have not taken him?” she began by way of reconnaissance.

That afternoon Fanny had visited ruins. There were others more personal that she was viewing then, the ruins of fair things not dead but destroyed.

===Chinese===
与此同时，马路对面，普莱斯太太正忙得不可开交。她在游廊上现身时，只觉得女儿和那个男人正在举行某些仪式。看在眼里，她立刻向女儿发难。

「你没有接纳他？」她以这句话作为试探的开场。

那天下午范妮去看过废墟了。此刻她端详的，还有另一些更为切身的废墟——那些美好的事物尚未死去、却已被毁的废墟。

===Original===
“Answer me,” Mrs. Price commanded.

The girl started. But she had been far away—in that lovely land where dreams come true and then, it may be, turn into nightmares. Through the dreams hand in hand with Loftus she had been strolling. Now she must put them all away.

“Answer me,” Mrs. Price repeated.

“I am afraid so.”

Into a misty and deserted parlor of the Inn Mrs. Price pulled the girl and there let fire.

“Afraid! You ought to be! What will your father say?”

===Chinese===
「回答我，」普莱斯太太命令道。

姑娘一惊。可她方才神游得正远——在那片美梦成真、而后或许又化作噩梦的可爱国土里。在那些梦里，她正与洛夫特斯手挽手漫步。如今她必须把它们统统抛开。

「回答我，」普莱斯太太重复。

「恐怕是这样。」

普莱斯太太把女儿拽进客栈一间雾蒙蒙、空无一人的起居室，在那里开了火。

「恐怕！你倒是该恐怕！你父亲会怎么说？」

===Original===
The father here projected was a gentleman who resided abroad and who seldom opened his mouth except to put something in it.

“And Fred!”

Fred was Fanny’s brother, a young chap whose opinions were of no value to anyone, himself included.

“And everybody!”

Everybody was the upper current of social life.

“And Sylvia!”

The earlier shots had not inflicted any visible damage, but this must have told.

“I shall have to write to her,” Fanny with unusual meekness replied.

===Chinese===
此处所说的父亲，是一位侨居海外的绅士，他难得张一次嘴——除非是要往里头送点东西。

「还有弗雷德！」

弗雷德是范妮的哥哥，一个其见解于任何人都无足轻重——于他自己也包括在内——的小伙子。

「还有所有人！」

所谓所有人，指的是社交圈的上层潮流。

「还有西尔维娅！」

先头几炮未见造成任何可见的损伤，这一炮想来是击中了。

「我得给她写信，」范妮带着一种异乎寻常的温顺答道。

===Original===
“Yes, do. Do by all means. Tell her you have taken her leavings. And why? Merciful heavens, why? If you were as staid and stiff as she I could understand. But a girl like you, with your tastes, your extravagances, a girl with a national reputation for beauty, to go and accept twenty-five thousand a year is—is—sinful, that’s what it is. Your own father has that, and on it we are out at elbows. It is just about enough for you to dress on. Oh, Fanny, Fanny!”

Hysterically the old lady waved her hands. “Oh, Fanny, I have so prayed that you would make a brilliant match. I have scrimped and saved that you might, and you go and take a blond beast of a pauper. It is too cruel!”

===Chinese===
「对，写吧。千万写。告诉她你接了她挑剩的。可这是为什么？老天慈悲，到底是为什么？你要是像她那样端庄刻板，我还能理解。可你这样的姑娘，带着你的趣味、你的挥霍，一个以美貌闻名的姑娘，竟去接受一年两万五千——这简直是——简直是——罪过，就是这个。你自己的父亲也才这个数，靠它我们已捉襟见肘。这钱刚好够你一人置办衣裳。哦，范妮，范妮！」

那老妇人歇斯底里地挥舞着双手。「哦，范妮，我是怎样地祈求过你能结一门显赫的亲事。我又是怎样地省吃俭用，好叫你如愿，结果你倒好，挑了个金发碧眼的穷光蛋。太狠心了！」

===Original===
Fanny winced. It was cruel. But the cruelty was not hers. It was Fate’s. She too had hoped for the very marriage her mother had so ardently desired. But Loftus had not cared. Occupied elsewhere he had sailed away. As well then Annandale as another.

“You see, you know,” she said in a wretched effort at smoothing things over, “he is quite a hero.”

But this was too much. Mrs. Price shook her head like a battle horse and fairly neighed.

“Because he saved your clothes? If it had been your life and you had said ‘Thank you’ it would have been ample. But your clothes! Not mine; the beast had not sense enough for that, but yours! I do hope you will give that as an excuse to Sylvia!”

===Chinese===
范妮瑟缩了一下。是狠心。可这狠心并非她的。是命运的。她也曾盼望过母亲所殷殷期盼的那门亲事。可洛夫特斯并不在意。他另有所图，扬帆远去了。那么，安南代尔也好，旁人也罢，都一样。

「你瞧，你是知道的，」她说道，凄然地想缓和局面，「他算得上一条好汉。」

可这话太过分了。普莱斯太太像一匹战马那样甩了甩脑袋，简直要嘶鸣起来。

「就因为他救了你的衣裳？倘若是救了你的命，你说一声『谢谢』也就足够了。可救的是衣裳！还不是我的——那蠢货还没那份见识救我的——是你的衣裳！我倒当真盼着你能拿这话去向西尔维娅做交代呢！」

## X A Victim / X 受害者

===Original===
Sylvia had gone from Newport. She was then at Lenox. It was there the previous autumn that her interest in Annandale had begun. The interest had so deepened that she gave him her heart. Never before had she given that to anyone. Annandale had taken it and then, one night, he had so bruised it that she thought it broken. He had written that he had not meant to. His letter had been full of regrets, of protestations, of bad grammar. Such things may palliate, but they do not cure. Only time can do that.

Time is a strange emollient. In its mysterious potency it softens without our knowledge. Suddenly a whisper, a breeze that passes, shows that it has done its work. With Sylvia time was having its will. Furtively she had found herself wondering, as Annandale had wondered, how it should fare with her, and how with him, could the past be effaced and the old days renewed. But those days were gone, she decided. Though into that decision a doubt would creep, not indeed concerning the departure, but concerning her attitude and the justice of it.

===Chinese===
西尔维娅已离开了纽波特。她那时正在莱诺克斯（Lenox）。头年秋天，正是在那里，她对安南代尔起了意。这份情意愈陷愈深，她终于把自己的心交给了他。从前她从未把心交给过任何人。安南代尔把它接了过去，随后，某夜，他竟把它磋磨得伤痕累累，她以为它已碎了。他写信说，他并非有意如此。那封信满是懊悔、满是辩白、满是语病。这些东西可以减轻痛楚，却不能治愈。唯有时间能治愈。

时间是一种奇异的抚慰剂。它那神秘的药力在我们不知不觉中施展软化之功。忽然，一声低语、一阵掠过的微风，便显露出它已奏效。在西尔维娅这里，时间正在自行其是。她暗自发现，自己竟像安南代尔所曾想的那样，琢磨起倘若往昔可以抹去、旧日可以重来，她自己结局将如何、他结局又将如何。可那段日子毕竟过去了，她这样断定。只是这个断定里会悄悄爬进一丝疑虑——倒不是对那份离去本身，而是对她自己的态度、以及这态度是否公道。

===Original===
Annandale had sinned. He had sinned wantonly, grievously. From an atmosphere of vice—an atmosphere from which, under pain of her displeasure, she had distinctly warned him—he had staggered to her, its pollution about him, reeking with drink, talking abundantly about nothing imaginable, and at her just remonstrance had become instantly irritable, refusing almost to leave the house.

So had his condition and the spectacle of it shocked her that, for awhile, memory of him and of it was repellent. In her own eyes she felt degraded. That men drank, she knew. But in her sphere of life they drank either moderately or else in haunts invisible to her. And it was precisely from such a haunt he had come, a shameless haunt, one that sullied her even to know of.

===Chinese===
安南代尔是犯了错的。他犯得放肆、犯得严重。从一处风月场的氛围里——一种她曾明白警告过他、若再涉足必惹她不悦的氛围里——他踉跄着来到她跟前，一身那地方的污浊，酒气冲天，滔滔不绝地说着些难以想象的胡话，待她正当地责备了几句，便立刻恼怒起来，几乎不肯从屋里离开。

他的那副模样、那副光景，把她惊得——有一阵子，关于他、关于那一幕的记忆都令她作呕。她觉得自己受了玷辱。男人喝酒，这她知道。可是在她那个圈子里，他们要么喝得有节制，要么就在她看不见的地方喝。而他正是从那样一个去处出来的，一个无耻的所在，单是晓得它的存在，便已脏了她。

===Original===
Yes, he had sinned, wantonly, grievously, almost unforgivably. Almost, she reflected, but perhaps not quite. In his letter of protest and regret he had told her that he remembered nothing, nothing whatever, absolutely nothing at all, save one vague, brief vision of herself. The rest, the beginning, the end, the inter-spaces were, he assured her, blank. At first she had thought that sheer nonsense. But, later, the earnest way in which it was put impressed her. Then on the heels of that communication there had followed one from Orr, endorsing what Annandale said, declaring that it was all quite possible, adding that, in certain temperaments, memory when influenced by toxics will play tricks stranger than the average mind can comfortably credit.

These letters she had not answered. Logically she could not admit the validity of the statements which they contained. But the heart has logic which logic does not know. Then, too, is there not that within us that prompts us to believe less what we should than what we wish? Sylvia’s reason, guided by her inexperience, refused at first to accept the idea that any sane man could act as Annandale had and afterward be oblivious of it. That remorse there should be was only natural, but that there should be no memory of anything whatever seemed to her absurd.

===Chinese===
是的，他犯了错，放肆地、严重地、几乎不可饶恕地犯了错。几乎，她思忖，但或许并不全然不可饶恕。在那封辩白与懊悔并陈的信里，他告诉她，他什么都不记得，什么都记不得，绝对什么都不记得，只除了一个模糊的、短暂的关于她的影子。其余的一切，开头、结尾、当中的间隙，据他保证，全是一片空白。起初她以为这纯属胡扯。可后来，他那恳切的语气打动了她。紧接着这封信之后，奥尔也来了一封信，证实安南代尔所言不虚，宣称这一切大有可能，并补充说，在某些气质的人身上，受了毒物影响的记忆会耍出一些比寻常头脑所能轻易采信的更为离奇的花招。

这些信她都没有回。从逻辑上讲，她无法承认信中所言的有效性。然而人心自有它一套逻辑，是逻辑本身所不晓得的。再者，我们心中岂不是有那么一种东西，怂恿我们不肯相信我们理应相信的，反倒去相信我们情愿相信的？西尔维娅的理性，受了她的涉世未深的指引，起初拒绝接受这种想法：任何一个神志清醒的男人怎能做出安南代尔所做之事、事后又一无所知。懊悔自是应有之义，可竟至对一切都毫无记忆，在她看来未免荒谬。

===Original===
But there was her cousin’s assurance to the contrary. Then imperceptibly, little by little, that assurance, filtering through the saddened girl, took possession of her, insisting on recognition, telling her that, though her lover had erred, yet, in erring, he was more to be pitied than condemned. Dominated by drink, which, Orr added, he had promised to renounce, he had gone to that haunt and, contaminated there, knew not what he did. But she, instead of realizing that, she who was to have been his in sickness and health, for better, for worse, she, in her pride, had dismissed him.

He had erred, Sylvia told herself, deeply, grievously, but so, too, had she. She had condemned when she should have condoned; she had spurned him when it was her solicitude that he needed.

===Chinese===
然而她表兄对此却作了相反的保证。于是，不知不觉地、一点一滴地，那份保证渗透过这伤心的姑娘，占有了她，非要她承认不可，告诉她：她的恋人虽有错，然而其错之中，他之堪怜甚于堪咎。奥尔又补了一句，他已答应戒酒；可是在酒的支配下，他去了那处所在，在那儿被沾染，不知自己做了什么。而她呢——她本该是他的，无论疾病健康、无论顺境逆境——可她非但没有看穿这一点，反倒出于骄傲，把他逐走了。

他犯了错，西尔维娅对自己说，错得深、错得重，可她自己又何尝不是。她本该宽宥，却施以了定罪；她本该给他关怀，却施以了鄙弃。

===Original===
At the sure cognition of that, it was as though from her eyes a bandage had fallen. Then at once in her tender conscience she beheld herself, detestable in pride, a girl without a heart, one of whom he, no doubt, was well rid of.

It was during the process of this awakening that the conflagration at Narragansett Pier occurred. Sylvia read of it. She read, too, of certain prowesses which the dismissed had displayed.

The account, very inexact as such accounts always are, was also highly colored, spun out for space purposes for much more than the space was worth. Had you not known better you would have taken it for granted that the heroism of Annandale was on a par with that of Leonidas at Thermopylae and even of Roosevelt at San Juan. It quite stirred you.

===Chinese===
一旦确确实实认识到这一点，便仿佛有一条蒙眼布从她眼前落下。随即，在她那柔嫩的良知里，她看见了自己——骄傲得可憎，一个没有心的姑娘，而他，毫无疑问，摆脱了她倒是件幸事。

正是在这一觉来的过程中，纳拉甘西特码头（Narragansett Pier）的那场大火发生了。西尔维娅读到了消息。她也读到了那个被她辞退的人所表现的若干英勇事迹。

那篇报道，正如这类报道向来那样，极不准确，而且渲染得花团锦簇，为了凑篇幅而注的水，远比那点版面所值的多。若非你知根知底，你定会想当然地认为，安南代尔的英勇足可与温泉关的列奥尼达（Leonidas at Thermopylae）比肩，甚至不亚于圣胡安（San Juan）的罗斯福（Roosevelt）。它确实叫你心潮澎湃。

===Original===
It stirred Sylvia. The paper fell from her. But the past returned. At once it seemed to her that it might be mended and the old days renewed. The hero of whom the paper told she knew now that she had wholly loved, and she knew, too, that wholly she still loved him.

Time had done its work ridiculously, inopportunely, yet effectively at last. But the gates of life are double. On one stands written “Too Soon.” On the other “Too Late.” It is unfortunate to get wedged between them. Of that fact Sylvia became rapidly aware. On the morrow she began a letter to Annandale. Before it was finished there came one from Fanny, announcing that she was to be Annandale’s wife.

===Chinese===
它搅动了西尔维娅。报纸从她手中滑落。可往昔回来了。一时间，她觉得它或许还能弥补，旧日或许还能重来。报上所说的那位英雄，她如今才明白自己曾全心全意地爱过他，她同时也明白，自己至今仍全心全意地爱着他。

时间完成了它的工作——办得荒唐、办得不是时候，可终究办成了。然而生命之门有两扇。一扇上写着「太早」。另一扇上写着「太迟」。被卡在两扇之间是不幸的。这一层西尔维娅很快便觉察到了。第二天她动笔给安南代尔写信。信尚未写完，范妮的信先到了，宣告她将与安南代尔成婚。

===Original===
In certain crises of the emotions there is a certain sense of unreality. Even as Sylvia read what Fanny said she could not grasp it. When presently she did, she could not believe it. But there it was. Then immediately she experienced the agony which comes when we battle in dream with the intangible and the dread, when we know it is dream and yet feel it is death.

“It is all my fault,” she cried. She found but that. At the moment she was in that condition which precedes the great commotion of tears, when the strangulation of agony is subsiding and contracted nerves distend. But the tears did not come. The pain was infinite. There was a weight which she felt not without but within, a weight so heavy that she thought she could not bear it. It racked her. Only her mind was active. “It is my fault,” she repeated. Then she added, “And my cross.”

===Chinese===
在某些情感的紧要关头，会有某种不真实之感。西尔维娅读着范妮的话时，竟一时无法领会。待领会过来，又不能置信。可那信就在那里。随即她尝到了那种痛苦——我们在梦中与那无形与可怖之物搏斗时所尝到的痛苦，明知是梦，却感到它是死亡。

「这都是我的错，」她叫道。她只找到这一句话。此刻她正处在泪潮大作之前的那种状态——痛苦的窒息感正退去，紧缩的神经正鼓胀开来。可眼泪并没有来。痛却是无穷无尽的。她感到一种分量，不在身外，而在身内，那分量如此沉重，她以为自己受不住了。它折磨着她。唯有她的心思还在动。「是我的错，」她重复道。然后又添了一句，「也是我的十字架。」

===Original===
From a crisis such as this, in a nature such as hers, the soul issues as from an orgy. It has supped on sorrow. It is fed. It ceases to look back. It looks forward, marveling indeed that it should look at all, yet looking. Life’s burdens are more bearable than the despairful think. Until the eyes are closed and the heart no longer beats, in some way, somehow, they can be carried.

Sylvia took up her cross. It was leaden. But in the effort she was aided. Pride helped her. The assistance of pride may be poor, yet is it not better than none? To Sylvia it was useful. It enabled her to answer Fanny’s letter.

“You have my congratulations, Fanny dear,” she wrote, “all of them, my best and warmest, and so has Arthur too. Please say so to him and tell him that, in marrying by dearest friend, he and I must be dear friends also.”

Then the tears did come, swiftly, like the ripple of the rain. On the table where she sat she put her head down and sobbed, paroxysmally, as sobs a child.

===Chinese===
像她这样的性情，经过这样一场危机之后，灵魂走出来时，犹如从一场纵欲里走出来。它已饱饮了悲伤。它餍足了。它不再回首。它向前望去——自己居然还能望，都觉得惊奇，可到底还在望。生命的重担，比绝望者所想的更可承受。只要双目未合、心脏未停，总有什么办法、总有什么路子，可以把它扛下去。

西尔维娅背起了她的十字架。它是铅铸的。可她在这桩苦功中得了助力。骄傲助了她。骄傲的助力或许寒酸，可总好过没有吧？对西尔维娅，它是有用的。它使她得以回复范妮的信。

「我祝贺你，亲爱的范妮，」她写道，「把全部的祝贺都给你，我最衷心、最热切的那一份；阿瑟也有。请你转告他，并告诉他，他娶了我最亲爱的朋友，那么他和我从此也必是挚友。」

随后眼泪果然来了，来得飞快，像雨的涟漪。她把头伏在面前那张桌上抽噎起来，一阵阵地，像个孩子那样抽噎。

## The General Sessions / 一般刑事法庭

## I Disenchantment / I 幻灭

===Original===
“*Il segreto*—”
Marie’s voice rang out, clear and fluid, scattering notes through the room, filling it with them, charging the air with melody, then, like a chorus entering a crypt, it sank in diminishing accords and, sinking, died slowly away.

The *segreto* indeed! The secret of happiness was remoter now than when, under the teaching of the ex-first lady, she had first attacked the score. But her voice had improved. It was fuller, more resonant and ample.

Marie, too, had improved. In face and figure beauty had developed. Her manner was securer, her eyes more grave, her smile less frequent. The bud had blossomed.

===Chinese===
「*Il segreto*（那秘密）——」

玛丽的嗓音响起来，清亮而流畅，把音符撒满整间屋子，让它们充盈其间，使空气都蓄满了旋律；继而，像一支歌队没入墓穴，它在一组组渐弱的和弦里沉下去，沉下去，缓缓地消散了。

那 *segreto*（秘密）呀！幸福的秘密，如今比从前——比她在那位前首席女伶（ex-first lady）门下初攻这份乐谱那时——还要渺远了。可她的嗓音倒是长进了。它更饱满、更洪亮、更丰盈了。

玛丽本人也长进了。面庞与身段都生出了美。她的举止更从容，眼神更凝重，笑容少了些。花苞绽放了。

===Original===
In the process a year had gone. From high Norman downs she had watched the summer pass. Autumn had met her in the Elysian Fields. There the wolfish winter had approached. At the first bite there had been a flight to Havre, the return to New York. Now it was spring again. Through the open windows of the Arundel came the city’s hum and with it the subtleties and enticements of May.

A year had gone. But there are years that count double. There are others so vast that in them you may have evolved a world, seen it glow and subside. The solitudes of space appal. The solitudes of the heart may be as endless as they. In those where Marie loitered a world had had its birth and subsidence, a world with gem-like hopes for stars, a world lighted by a sun so eager that its rays had made her blind. There had been aspirations, gorgeous and tangental as comets are. There had been the colorless ether of which dreams are made. For cosmic matter there was love. A year had gone. In it, these wonders had formed and fled.

===Chinese===
在这过程里，一年过去了。她曾从诺曼底（Norman）的高地凝望夏日逝去。秋天在香榭丽舍（Elysian Fields）与她相遇。在那里，豺狼般的冬天逼近过来。寒冬第一口咬下，便是一次逃往勒阿弗尔（Havre）的奔波，随之是返回纽约。如今又是春天了。从阿伦德尔（Arundel）公寓敞开的窗子涌进来城市的嗡鸣，以及随之而来的、五月里那种种幽微的诱惑。

一年过去了。可有些年份是要按双倍来算的。还有些年份如此浩瀚，在里头你简直可以演化出一个世界，眼看它发光、又眼看它沉寂。太空的寂寥令人胆寒。人心的寂寥，也可以与它一般无边无际。在玛丽所踯躅的寂寥里，便有一个世界诞生过、又沉寂过——一个以宝石般的希冀作星辰的世界，一个被一颗如此炽烈的太阳照亮的世界，那太阳的光芒烈得刺瞎了她的眼。那里有过种种渴望，绚烂而横逸，一如彗星。那里有过构成梦境的那种无色的以太。而作为这宇宙的质料的，是爱。一年过去了。在这些奇迹里，它们成形了，又遁走了。

===Original===
Marie got from the piano. It had no secret to tell. But there was another which the year had revealed, a secret which, at first opaque and obscure, little by little had taken shape and changed from an impossibility into a monstrous fact. Marie had begun by disavowing it. She had disowned it, would have none of it. But disavowals cease. In certain conditions we get used to monsters. The soul makes itself at home with what it must. The monster to which Marie was accustoming herself was the knowledge that her lover had lied.

In departing with him from the den of the ex-first lady it was not merely with faith and trust, but with absolute certainty that marriage, if delayed, was only postponed; that a week, a month at the furthest, would see her his wife.

===Chinese===
玛丽从钢琴前起身。它没有什么秘密可告诉她。然而这一年却揭示了另一个秘密——一个起初晦暗、模糊的秘密，渐渐地，它显出了轮廓，从一桩不可能之事，变成了一桩骇人的事实。玛丽起初是拒不承认的。她否认它、摈弃它、不愿与它有任何瓜葛。可否认终有尽头。在某些境况里，人是会习惯怪物的。灵魂总能在它不得不承受的东西里安顿下来。玛丽正在让自己习惯的那头怪物，便是——她明白她的情人撒了谎。

当初随他从前首席女伶的巢穴出走时，她不仅是怀着信仰与信赖，更是怀着绝对的笃定：婚姻即便推迟，也不过是延宕；至多一星期、一个月，她便会成为他的妻子。

===Original===
On the way she had stopped and wired to Gay Street, telling her father not to worry, that she had gone to be married, that she would write to him soon.

Whether he had worried she could only surmise. But soon she had written, enclosing a photograph of Loftus, one which she had colored, an excellent likeness that displayed his chiseled features, wonderful eyes and thin, black mustache with a perfection of precision that was lifelike. Above it she wrote: “Marie’s Husband.” It would please her father, she was sure, and in the letter she told him prettily, in a little, cajoling way which he loved, that while, for the moment, he must not know where she was, yet shortly she was planning to come and surprise him—to surprise him more than he could ever imagine, and show him that he could be very, very proud of her, but prouder still, much, much prouder of the man she had married.

===Chinese===
半路上，她停了下来，给盖伊街（Gay Street）发了封电报，告诉父亲不必挂念，说她去结婚了，说很快就会写信给他。

他究竟有没有挂念，她只能揣测。但她很快便写了信，信里附了一张洛夫特斯的照片——一张她亲手上了色的照片，一张极传神的肖像，把他那如雕如刻的五官、那双妙不可言的眼睛、那两撇细细的黑胡髭，精确到了栩栩如生的地步。她在照片上方写道：「玛丽的丈夫。」她确信这会令父亲欢喜；信里她又娇嗔地——用他素来所爱的小小哄骗口吻——告诉他，眼下他还不能知道她在何处，但不久她便打算回来给他一个惊喜——一个他万万想象不到的惊喜，让他晓得他可以为她、为她骄傲得不得了，但更要紧的是，为她所嫁的那个人骄傲得更加、更加不得了。

===Original===
The plan, delightful to her, first the illness of her lover’s mother, then the lady’s absence from town, prevented her from at once effecting. Then, greatly to her uneasiness, she found that the plan must be yet further delayed. Mrs. Loftus had gone to her manor on the Hudson, where, her son declared, he could not take Marie “like that.” Financially it was stupid to rush things. Gradually his mother must be prepared. Moreover, as preparation could be decently managed only in town, to which she would not now return until autumn, it would be a good idea to run over to Europe.

So spoke Royal Loftus. It was all false as an obituary. Financially he was entirely independent of his mother, who, at the time, was not at her manor, but just around the corner and never better in her life. But Marie, wholly infatuated, quite willing to believe that the moon was made of green cheese if only he took the trouble to so inform her, accepted it all for gospel.

===Chinese===
这个计划令她满心欢喜，可先是她情人母亲的病、继而那位夫人离开了城里，使她无法立刻成行。随后，令她大为不安的是，她发现这计划还得再往后推。洛夫特斯太太已去了哈德逊河畔（Hudson）的庄园，据她儿子说，他没法「就这样」把玛丽带去。从钱财上讲，操之过急是愚蠢的。他母亲总得慢慢有所准备。况且，准备工作只有 在城里 才办得体面，而她如今不到秋天不会回城，所以，不如趁机到欧洲去转一圈。

罗伊尔·洛夫特斯便是这么说的。这一切，虚假得像一则讣告。钱财上，他对母亲完全独立；至于他母亲，当时并不在庄园，而就在街角附近，身体之好前所未有。可玛丽已痴迷到极点，只要他肯费心告诉她月亮是绿奶酪做的，她也欣然相信——她把这一切都奉为福音。

===Original===
The delay, of course, was a deep disappointment. She felt it, and felt it acutely. But in Europe she supposed that people would not know, and would not care a rap if they did, Loftus hastened to assure her.

To his project, therefore, she yielded. Presently she was glad that she had. The journey itself was a joy. At the Arundel he had come and gone. Often she had been lonely. Often she had sat through hours that limped themselves away, waiting for him, waiting fruitlessly. But during the journey and after it, on the high Norman downs, always she had him with her. Therein was the joy.

===Chinese===
这种耽搁，自然是深深的失望。她感受到了，感受得敏锐。可是在欧洲，她想，人们是不会知道的；纵然知道了，也不会放在心上——洛夫特斯赶紧这样向她保证。

于是她依了他的安排。不久她倒庆幸自己依了。这趟旅行本身就是一桩乐事。在阿伦德尔公寓，他来来去去。她时常是寂寞的。她时常枯坐过一个个拖着步子走完的钟头，等他，徒劳地等。可旅途中、以及旅行之后，在诺曼底的高地上，他始终陪着她。快乐便在这里。

===Original===
The places, new to her and fragrant, to which he took her interested her very much, but very much, too, as accessories might. It was from him that their real charm emanated. He also enjoyed himself, but less rapturously, in a fashion more detached. He found time to busy himself with the news of the world, with menus, with wines—occupations which to her were extraordinary. Marie did not know what she ate; as for the world, it was sublimated in him, a fact which she confided to him—of which, if she had not, he would have been perfectly aware and which he accepted at first as but a proper tribute to himself, but which ended by boring him distinctly. An excess of anything disagrees with the best.

===Chinese===
他带她去的那些地方，于她是新鲜的、芬芳的，让她颇感兴趣，但那兴趣的程度，也就像对待种种点缀一样。这些地方真正的魅力，是从他身上散发出来的。他也玩得开心，却没那么忘情，玩得更带一种置身事外的派头。他还能腾出空来，忙忙碌碌地关心天下大事、关心菜单、关心葡萄酒——这些营生，在她眼里都是稀罕的。玛丽不知道自己吃了什么；至于这世界，它早已在他身上升华了——这一层她曾对他倾诉（纵然她不说，他也再清楚不过），他起初把它当作理所应当的敬意照单全收，到头来却被它腻味得不轻。凡事一过量，再好的也败胃口。

===Original===
The first symptoms of indigestion declared themselves in Paris. They had there a large suite in a big hotel. So large was the suite that frequently Marie could not find Loftus in it. He was off, returning when he saw fit, refusing to be questioned, yawning at reproaches, but otherwise perfectly civil, agreeing with her that it was not nice to be left alone, yet leaving her alone whenever he felt like it.

On the Norman downs the fresh fragrance of life had put a higher color on her cheeks, marking them with the flush of happiness and health. But in this game of hide and nowhere to seek her face became pallid as the curious white sky which in autumn stretches itself over Paris. Then stealthily, like a wolf, winter approached. The cheerlessness of it Loftus hated, as all New Yorkers do. To Marie, however, it was welcome. It meant a return to the Arundel, where she felt that the marriage so long delayed could not be further postponed.

===Chinese===
消化不良最初的征兆是在巴黎发作的。他们在当地一家大旅馆里租了一套很大的套房。那套房大到玛丽常常在里面找不到洛夫特斯。他人不见了，爱什么时候回来就什么时候回来，拒绝被盘问，对责备只以哈欠回应，除此之外倒十分客气——也肯附和她承认把她一人撂下不好，可一有机会便照样把她撂下。

在诺曼底的高地上，生活清新的芬芳曾给她双颊添上一层更浓的色泽，烙上了幸福与健康的红晕。可在这场无处可寻的捉迷藏里，她的脸变得苍白起来，像秋天铺展在巴黎上空那种奇异的惨白的天。接着，冬天像豺狼一般悄悄逼近。它的萧瑟，洛夫特斯和所有纽约客一样深恶痛绝。然而对玛丽，它却是受欢迎的。它意味着重返阿伦德尔公寓，而她觉得，在那里，这场耽搁已久的婚姻总不能再推下去了。

===Original===
The illusion was pleasant but not permanent. On reemerging in the noise and sunshine of New York Loftus ceased to bother himself with the invention of excuses. He told Marie that his mother would not listen to anything of the kind, a statement which, while frank, was not exact. Mrs. Loftus had never heard of it, or for that matter, of the girl, and Loftus saw no reason whatever why she should. Yet if not frank, he was patient. Marie, on the other hand, took it all very hard. Humiliation possessed her. By day it confronted her, spectrally. At night it came to her, sat by her side, plucked at her sleeve, awoke her. It was a thing she could not get away from, could not forget; what is worse, she could not understand. It tortured her, and concerning it she tormented him constantly, displaying a persistence that was annoying and pathetic—the persistence of a child. It was as such that he treated it with yawning indifference, quite as though it were but a whim which, other things intervening, she would forget.

===Chinese===
这幻觉是惬意的，却并不长久。一回到纽约的喧嚣与阳光里，洛夫特斯便懒得再费心编造借口了。他直截了当地告诉玛丽，他母亲不会听这一类的话——这说法虽坦率，却并不准确。洛夫特斯太太压根儿没听过这事，甚至也没听过这个姑娘，而洛夫特斯也看不出有任何理由要让她知道。他纵然不够坦率，倒还算有耐心。玛丽呢，则把这一切承受得很苦。屈辱攫住了她。白天，它像鬼影般横在她面前。夜里，它来到她跟前，坐在她身旁，扯她的袖子，把她惊醒。这是她逃不开、忘不掉的一样东西；更糟的是，她无法理解。它折磨着她，为着这事她也无休止地折磨他，表现出一种既恼人又可怜的执拗——一个孩子的执拗。他便把孩子当孩子待，打着哈欠、漫不经心，仿佛这不过是一桩一时兴起的小念头，旁的事一插进来，她自然就忘了。

===Original===
Other things did intervene. Among them was an adventure in Central Park. One afternoon a brougham in which she was driving crossed a victoria where sat a remarkably pretty woman with Loftus at her side.

Marie’s eyes filled. Had he struck her he would have hurt her far less. When next she saw him she told him so. The idea amused him. He was not a ruffian, only a cad. Like the whim, he waved the little tragedy away.

“That was Mrs. Annandale,” he announced unabashedly, “a very old friend of mine. I have known her all my life.”

===Chinese===
旁的事果然插进来了。其中之一，是中央公园（Central Park）里的一桩偶遇。某日下午，她乘坐的双座四轮马车与一辆四轮敞篷马车交会而过，那敞篷车里坐着一位极其漂亮的女人，洛夫特斯正坐在她身旁。

玛丽的眼睛立刻湿了。这一下，比他当真打她一记还伤得厉害。下一次见他时，她这样告诉了他。这想法倒把他逗乐了。他不是恶棍，只是个无赖。像打发那桩小念头一样，他把这出小悲剧一挥手挥开了。

「那是安南代尔太太，」他不慌不忙地宣告，「我的一个很老的老朋友。我打小就认识她。」

===Original===
“Mrs. Annandale!” Marie exclaimed. “Not the wife of the Mr. Annandale whom you brought here last year?”

Loftus stared at her. He did not understand. Yet then, neither did she.

“Why,” she continued, “you told me he was to marry a dark young lady.”

“Yes,” said Loftus, fumbling as he spoke for a cigarette. “But I told you also not to use that expression. Say girl or young woman. If you want to be fantastic, say young gentlewoman, but never young lady. You are right, though. Annandale was to have married a Miss Waldron, but she threw him over and he married somebody else.”

===Chinese===
「安南代尔太太！」玛丽叫起来，「莫不是去年您领到这儿来的那位安南代尔先生的太太？」

洛夫特斯盯着她。他不明白她的意思。可话说回来，她自己也不明白。

「哎呀，」她接着说，「您不是跟我说过，他要娶一位肤色黝黑的 young lady（年轻女士）吗？」

「是啊，」洛夫特斯一边说，一边摸索着一支烟，「可我也跟你说过，别用那个说法。要说 girl（姑娘），或者 young woman（年轻女子）。要想文绉绉的，就说 young gentlewoman（年轻淑女），可千万别说 young lady。不过你说得对。安南代尔本是要娶一位沃尔德伦小姐（Miss Waldron）的，可她甩了他，他便娶了别人。」

===Original===
To Marie all this was inexplicable. She did not understand how a man thrown over by one girl could so speedily marry another. She did not understand, either, what Loftus could be doing with her. To her mind driving presupposed an intimacy which acquaintance might explain but did not excuse. The matter perplexed her, and not unnaturally. It is only through our own heart that it is possible to attempt to read the heart of another. In her heart Marie knew that nothing earthly could induce her to appear as intimate with a man as Loftus had with that woman. Yet, though she knew that, she knew also that many of her views, like many of her expressions, were not in tune with the tone of the set in which Loftus moved.

===Chinese===
对玛丽而言，这一切都不可解。她不明白一个男人被一个姑娘甩了，怎么还能这么快娶另一个。她也不明白，洛夫特斯和那个女人在一起做什么。在她看来，同乘一车这件事本身就预设了一种亲密——相识或许可以解释，却不能为之开脱。这事令她困惑，而且也并不反常。要揣度另一颗心，唯有通过我们自己的心才有可能。在她自己的心里，玛丽深知，世间没有任何东西能诱使她在一个男人面前，表现得像洛夫特斯与那个女人那样亲密。然而，她虽明白这一点，却也明白自己的许多看法——正如自己的许多说法——与洛夫特斯所交往的那一圈子人的调子，并不合拍。

===Original===
