The absence of watch and money suggested a theory. That was enough.

===Chinese===
然而，虽然那姑娘所代表的那条线索不得不放弃，但还有一种说法仍在。甚至有两种说法。头一种，是劫财。

洛夫特斯被发现时，身上连一个五分的硬币都没有。阔佬们理应随身携带的那一卷钞票——而他们既然处处赊账，便从不随身携带——也没有。寻常该有的怀表，也没有。一个阔佬，尤其是有闲阔佬，要怀表何用，警方和报纸一概懒得细想。怀表与钱财的缺席，足以引出一种说法。这便够了。

===Original===
The theory, however, like all theories, had its defects. Loftus had been found within the park, a few feet from the fence. The shooting might have occurred from without, but unless the assassin had a key or a ladder or a balloon or wings he could not possibly have got in to go through him. Eliminating ladder, balloon and wings, a key the assassin could not have had unless he were a resident in the neighborhood, the agent of a resident, or a caretaker of the park itself. People of this order are as eliminable as balloons and wings.

The theory therefore had its defects. It had, though, this in its favor—the lock of one of the gates might have been picked. It had something else in its favor. It suited the Loftus clan.

===Chinese===
然而这说法，一如所有说法，自有其破绽。洛夫特斯是在公园里头被发现的，离栅栏不过几步。开枪也许是从外头开的，可除非凶手有钥匙、有梯子、有气球、或者长了翅膀，否则他不可能钻进去搜他的身。把梯子、气球和翅膀一并排除，钥匙这东西凶手是不可能有的——除非他是附近的住户、某住户的代理人、或公园本身的看守。这一类人，和气球、翅膀一样可以排除。

所以这说法有破绽。不过它有一点好处——某一扇门的锁也许被撬过。它另有一点好处：它合洛夫特斯家族的意。

===Original===
Mrs. Loftus, though childless now, was not otherwise alone. Behind her were all the Loftuses, a contingent of relatives socially eminent, ponderable politically, super-respectable, synonymous with the best. To them the death of Royal, however dismal, was not disgraceful—not disgraceful, that is, assuming that it was a footpad’s work. On their escutcheon it put a mourning band but not a blackening blot. That blot they feared. They had cause to. The dark, donjuanesque story about Marie Leroy might have been followed by other stories darker still, dirtier if possible, that would begrime them all.

The footpad theory they accepted therefore at once. Had they been able, had circumstances favored them, had the man, for instance, been shot in some way or in some place unknowable to the police, they would have arranged to have had him die decorously, if suddenly, of some genteel complaint, of appendicitis or pleuropneumonia. Then there would have been no stories, no extras, no pictures, no notoriety, no fear of that blot.

The fear subsisting, they accepted the footpad theory, glad to find it ready-made, declining to consider any other, desisting from further effort, hushing the matter as well as they could, refusing, though urged, to offer a reward.

===Chinese===
洛夫特斯太太虽然如今膝下空空，却并非孑然一身。在她身后，是整个洛夫特斯家族——一支社会地位显赫、政治上有分量、德高望重、与「最上等人」同义的亲眷。在他们看来，罗伊尔之死虽则凄惨，却并不丢人——所谓不丢人，前提是这是拦路强人所为。这事给他们的家徽添上一道黑纱，而非一个抹黑的污点。那污点，他们怕。他们有理由怕。关于玛丽·勒鲁瓦的那段阴郁的、唐璜式的故事，后面也许还会跟着别的故事，更阴郁的、若可能更下作的，足以把他们一大家子统统抹黑。

于是他们立刻接受了拦路强人这一说法。倘若他们做得到，倘若情形允许，譬如说，倘若那人是被以某种方式、在某个警方查不到的地方打死的，他们便会安排他体面地——虽然是猝死——死于某种体面的病症，阑尾炎啦、胸膜肺炎啦。那就没有故事了，没有号外了，没有照片了，没有恶名了，没有对那污点的恐惧了。

恐惧既然挥之不去，他们便接受了拦路强人这一说法，乐得现成，拒不考虑任何别的说法，不肯再费功夫，把这事尽量捂住，纵然有人劝，也不肯悬赏。

===Original===
Yet, though the theory suited them it did not satisfy the public. It was too tame. They demanded something else. That demand the press, as was its duty, attempted to supply. Through methods unfathomably vidocqesque, the young gentleman connected with the Chronicle—one of the most enterprising sheets—discovered more about Loftus dead than Loftus living could himself have known. They discovered that in the panic he had dropped a bagatelle of five millions, and announced that he had committed suicide. But while at the autopsy it was not demonstrated that Loftus could not have shot himself, at the inquest it was shown that the obligatory instrument had not been found. Even to vidocqesque young gentlemen the suicide theory ceased then to appeal.

But that only deepened the mystery. To dissipate it and, at the same time, to display an endearing pro bono publicanism, the Chronicle offered a reward of five thousand dollars for such information as would lead to the arrest and conviction of the assassin.

Immediately there was a clue.

===Chinese===
然而，这说法合他们的意，却填不了公众的胃口。它太寡淡了。他们要别的。这要求，报纸责无旁贷地设法去满足。凭着深不可测的维多克（Vidocq）式手段，《纪事报》（Chronicle）——最敢闯的一份报纸——的一位年轻先生，查出了比洛夫特斯活着时自己所知的更多的事。他们查出，在那场恐慌中他赔掉了区区五百万，于是宣布他是自杀。然而，虽然尸检未能证明洛夫特斯不可能自击一枪，审讯时却表明，那件必不可少的家伙并未找到。于是即便对维多克式的年轻先生们，自杀说也再无吸引力。

可这一来，谜更深了。为驱散这谜、同时也为了显示一番讨人喜欢的「为公众利益」的热忱，《纪事报》悬赏五千元，征集能导致凶手被捕定罪的消息。

立刻便有了一条线索。

===Original===
It was Harris who produced it. Under the guidance of a reporter he was led to the office of the Chronicle, where the young gentleman turned him over to the managing editor quite as though the clue were his own.

“Here, Mr. Digby, is a party that knows who shot Loftus.”

Mr. Digby was a small man with a big beard, very well dressed, remarkably civil.

“Yes,” he said. “And who did?”

“Mr. Arthur Annandale.”

===Chinese===
献出这条线索的是哈里斯。在一名记者的引导下，他被领进了《纪事报》编辑部，那位年轻先生把他移交给了主编，那神情仿佛这条线索是他自己的一般。

「这位是，迪格比先生，一位知道是谁打死洛夫特斯的人。」

迪格比先生是个小个子、大胡子、衣冠楚楚、彬彬有礼的人。

「好啊，」他说，「是谁？」

「阿瑟·安南代尔先生。」

===Original===
Mr. Digby smiled. He did not believe it. But it stirred him pleasurably. The Chronicle stood for the people. Annandale represented the predatory rich. Besides, it was in front of Annandale’s house that Loftus had been found. At once he saw scoops, extras, headlines. Also the possible libel. Meanwhile at a glance he had taken Harris in.

“You are in his employment?”

“Yes, sir,” Harris, amazed at such perspicacity, replied. “I am the butler.”

“And you saw him do it?”

“No, sir, but I heard him say he would.”

“When?”

“The night Mr. Loftus was shot.”

“To whom did he say it? To you?”

“To Mrs. Annandale, sir.”

===Chinese===
迪格比先生笑了。他并不信。但这事让他乐不可支。《纪事报》代表人民。安南代尔代表掠夺成性的富人。况且，洛夫特斯正是在安南代尔家门前被发现的。他立刻看见了独家、号外、大字标题。也看见了可能的诽谤官司。与此同时，他一眼已把哈里森看了个透。

「你受雇于他？」

「是的，先生，」哈里斯惊诧于这种洞察力，答道，「我是他家管家。」

「你亲眼看见他干的？」

「没亲眼看见，先生，可我听见他说他要干。」

「什么时候？」

「洛夫特斯先生被打死那天夜里。」

「他对谁说的？对你？」

「对安南代尔太太说的，先生。」

===Original===
“Oho! How was that?”

“It was after dinner, sir. I was in the dining-room. The second man was with me cleaning up. On the floor under the table he found a necklace. I took it in through the hall to the drawing-room. Mrs. Annandale was there with Mr. Annandale. When I was just at the door I heard him say, ‘I’ll kill Loftus.’ I went in and gave him the necklace.”

“But why?” Mr. Digby interrupted. “What was he going to kill him for? What was the motive?”

“Mr. Loftus had just gone, sir. He had been dining with us. He and several others.”

“Well?”

“Well, sir, when I was in the hall I heard Mrs. Annandale say as how she wanted a divorce.”

“Aha!” exclaimed Mr. Digby. “The plot thickens. Was she in love with Loftus?”

“She was that, sir. Anyone could see it.”

“Then what?”

“Mr. Annandale went upstairs, came down again and went out.”

“Did you attach any importance to his going upstairs?”

“He went to get his pistol, sir.”

“Oho! He had a pistol, had he?”

“Yes, sir. A 32-calibre. I bought it for him myself.”

“That is a very good story,” said Mr. Digby, who was a judge.

===Chinese===
「哦！怎生的经过？」

「是晚饭过后，先生。我在饭厅里。二把手男仆跟我一起在收拾。他在桌子底下的地板上捡到一串项链。我端着它穿过门厅送进客厅。安南代尔太太正和安南代尔先生在那里。我刚好到门口时，听见他说：『我要宰了洛夫特斯。』我进去，把项链递给他。」

「可这是为什么？」迪格比先生打断，「他要杀他做什么？动机是什么？」

「洛夫特斯先生刚走，先生。他和另外几位在我们家吃的晚饭。」

「然后呢？」

「然后，先生，我在门厅里听见安南代尔太太说她要离婚。」

「啊哈！」迪格比先生叫道，「戏越走越深了。她爱洛夫特斯？」

「那可不，先生。谁都看得出来。」

「再然后？」

「安南代尔先生上了楼，又下来，出去了。」

「他上楼这件事，你觉得有什么要紧？」

「他是去取手枪的，先生。」

「哦！他有手枪？」

「有的，先生。一把 32 口径的。还是我替他买的。」

「这故事很精彩，」迪格比先生说——他是个鉴赏家。

## VII Held Without Bail / VII 不准保释

===Original===
The theories and clues in the now celebrated case Orr related to Sylvia one after another as they reached him through different channels. To the story of Marie Leroy she listened, her face averted, without a word. The footpad theory she dismissed. It was absurd. But the suicide theory impressed her. Even to her mind it was not logical. Loftus was too cavalier, too supremely indifferent, to make it plausible. On the other hand, it disposed of the whole matter. Moreover, as she put it to Orr, what is suicide but the sinful end of a sinful life? “Who knows,” she asked, “what sudden remorse he may have experienced that last night when he was alone there in the park?”

“Suicide,” Orr had replied, “is assassination driven in. It is the crisis of a preexisting condition, a condition wholly pathological, one which remorse may complicate but which it cannot directly induce. There was nothing whatever the matter with Loftus. He may have been sinful, as you express it, but he was sound. Besides, the man had no more conscience than a tomcat.”

===Chinese===
奥尔把如今这桩名案的种种说法与线索，经由不同渠道一一传到西尔维娅耳中。关于玛丽·勒鲁瓦的故事，她侧着脸听完，不发一语。拦路强人说她斥之不理。荒谬。可自杀说却打动了她。即便在她看来，它也不合逻辑。洛夫特斯这人太轻佻、太彻头彻尾地无所谓，叫人难以置信。但从另一方面讲，它把整件事一笔勾销了。况且，她对奥尔说，自杀岂不正是罪孽一生的罪孽结局吗？「谁知道，」她问，「他最后一夜独自在公园里时，可曾猛然间生过什么悔恨？」

「自杀，」奥尔回答，「不过是向内刺的谋杀。它是一种早已存在的病状的危机——一种彻头彻尾的病理状态，悔恨或可使之复杂，却不能直接引发它。洛夫特斯哪儿也没什么毛病。他也许如你所言是有罪的，但他康健得很。再说，这人的良心，比一只公猫多不到哪儿去。」

===Original===
Nevertheless Sylvia clung to the theory. She had no other. Hopelessly she hoped that time would verify it. But she suffered acutely. Orr’s account of Fanny’s attitude frightened her. What frightened her most was the tale that Harris told. The latter she learned from the press.

Meanwhile she had gone to Mrs. Loftus. The old lady had not recognized her, or, rather, had mistaken her for someone else. “My boy is away, Fanny,” she said, her head shaking as she spoke. “He is away. I don’t know where.” She began to whimper.

Sylvia, too, had wept. It was pitiful. The proud, arrogant woman Fate had reduced to a cowering crone.

===Chinese===
然而西尔维娅仍抱住这说法不放。她别无所依。她无可奈何地盼着时间能证明它。但她痛苦极了。奥尔所述范妮的态度令她胆寒。最令她胆寒的，是哈里斯讲的那一番话。那她是读报读来的。

这其间她也去过洛夫特斯太太那里。老太太没认出她，或者更确切地说，把她错认作了别人。「我的孩子出门去了，范妮，」她说，说话时头不住地摇，「他出门去了。我不知道他在哪里。」她开始啜泣。

西尔维娅也落了泪。那情形真是可怜。那高傲、倨傲的妇人，被命运折损成一个瑟缩的老妪。

===Original===
Meanwhile also Sylvia had tried to see Fanny. But at the hotel where Mrs. Price had been stopping she was informed that both were away. An address was given her to which she wrote. For a time no answer came. Finally from a different address Mrs. Price replied saying that Fanny was ill and asking that their whereabouts be a secret. In spite of the little threat Fanny was not anxious to be subpoenaed.

But that was much later, long after Harris had told the story which Mr. Digby declared to be very good.

This opinion, editorial and offhand though it was, was immediately and officially endorsed. For the story had a double merit. It supplied not merely a clue but a case. A very clear case, too. There was the antecedent threat, the opportunity, the instrument, everything even to the motive which was reasonable enough. The inevitable ensued. Annandale, arrested, was held without bail.

===Chinese===
这其间西尔维娅也设法见范妮。可在普莱斯太太下榻的旅馆，人家告诉她说母女俩都外出了。给了她一个地址，她按址写信去。有一阵子没有回音。终于，普莱斯太太从另一处地址回了信，说范妮病了，请她们的下落务必保密。虽有那一番小小的威胁，范妮可并不急于收到传票。

可那是许久以后的事了，远在哈里斯讲完那番迪格比先生称「很精彩」的故事之后。

这一句虽属随口的编辑式评断，却立刻得到了官方的正式背书。因为这故事有双重好处。它不仅提供了一条线索，更提供了一桩案子。一桩十分清楚的案子。有先前的威胁、有机会、有凶器，样样齐全，甚至连动机都合情合理。随之而来的自是不可避免的结局。安南代尔被捕，不准保释。

===Original===
At the news of that Sylvia shuddered. Time touched her. Her eyes ringed themselves with sudden circles. The shuddering passed, but the rings remained. She became whiter, harder, more resolute, divining dimly that somewhere, somehow, there was a duty to be performed. What the duty was to be the press disclosed. Against Annandale was public opinion. There he was convicted instanter. At the injustice, or what seemed to her the injustice, of that she revolted.

But Orr, whom Annandale had immediately retained, dosed her with a platitude. “Public opinion be hanged,” he said. “What is it but the stupidity of one multiplied by the stupidity of all. Vox populi, vox stulti. The majority is always cocksure and dead wrong.”

===Chinese===
闻此消息，西尔维娅打了个寒战。时间在她身上留下了印迹。她的眼眶忽然添了一圈青晕。寒战过去了，青晕留下了。她变得更白、更硬、更决然，隐约觉出在某处、以某种方式，有一桩本分须尽。这桩本分是什么，报纸告诉了她。舆论是与安南代尔为敌的。在那里，他当场便被定了罪。对这一不公——或在她看来是不公——她愤然反抗。

但已被安南代尔立刻聘用的奥尔，却拿一句陈词滥调喂她。「舆论见鬼去吧，」他说，「它算什么，不过是一个人的愚蠢乘以所有人的愚蠢罢了。Vox populi, vox stulti（民声即愚声）。多数派永远自以为是，又永远错得离谱。」

===Original===
In spite, though, of general stupidity there were people sufficiently indulgent to accord Annandale the benefit of extenuating circumstances. The reputation of Loftus, which left rather a little to be desired; the coupling of his name with that of Annandale’s wife; the report that for his sake the latter had been preparing to leave her husband; the further report that for the convenience of both Marie Leroy had been shipped abroad; these things reduced the case in the minds of the indulgent to what the French call a crime passionnel, and which, as such, is psychologically and even legally defensible.

But French views are not our views. Besides, admitting their validity, that validity was impaired by the attitude which Annandale assumed. He omitted to admit, and thereby for the time being waived the right to plead, the circumstances advanced in his justification. When charged had he said, “Oh, yes, I did it, and so would you or any other man,” there, don’t you see, might have been an excuse. But not a bit of it. Up and down he denied that he was the culprit.

===Chinese===
然而纵有这般普遍的愚蠢，也还有些人宽容大度，肯把「情有可原」的便宜给安南代尔。洛夫特斯的名声——多少有些不大体面；他的名字与安南代尔太太的名字连在一起；据传后者正为他打算离开丈夫；又据传为了两人方便，玛丽·勒鲁瓦被打发出国——这些事，在宽容者心中，把这桩案子缩小成法国人所谓的 crime passionnel（情杀），而情杀这等事，从心理学上、甚至从法律上，都是可辩的。

但法国人的见解并非我们的见解。况且，纵然承认其有效，这份有效性也被安南代尔自己所持的态度折损了。他没有承认——因而暂时也放弃了据此辩护的权利——那些为他开脱的情形。被控告时，他若说一句：「哦，是的，是我干的，换作你或任何别的男人也会一样，」那你看，岂不就有了借口。可他半句也没有。他上上下下矢口否认自己是凶手。

===Original===
A denial such as that has, though, its merits. It puts on the prosecution the burden of proof. Moreover, if you have done anything you should not have it is only common sense to say that you have not done it, to say it in spite of facts, in spite of evidence, in spite of everything and everybody. For if you own up, there you are, while if you don’t then no matter what is advanced you may succeed in raising a doubt and in planting it among the jury. But in this case the denial was more serviceable than ordinarily it might have been for the reason that thus far no one had been produced who could say they had been about while Annandale was at it.

These points Orr set before Sylvia. The sophistry of them displeased her. She did not like it, and said so.

“It will get him off, though,” Orr confidently replied. “Unless,” he hastened to add, “a witness to the act itself should pop up. Then, barring a miracle, he is a goner. But otherwise I will get him off. It may take a year or two, but I’ll do it.”

===Chinese===
然而这种否认自有其好处。它把举证之责推给了控方。再者，你若做了什么不该做的事，说你没做，本就是常识——不顾事实、不顾证据、不顾一切、不顾所有人地说你没做。因为你要是认了，那便完了；你要是不认，那么无论对方提出什么，你都可能成功地在陪审团里种下一颗疑心。而本案中，这否认比寻常更有用，因为至今为止，还没有谁能站出来说，安南代尔干那事时他在场。

这些道理，奥尔一一摆给西尔维娅。其中的诡辩令她不快。她不喜欢，也说了出来。

「不过这能救他出来，」奥尔满怀把握地答，「除非——」他赶紧补一句，「——有人亲眼目击那行为本身冒了出来。那样的话，除非奇迹，他是没救了。但若不然，我能救他出来。或许要花上一两年，可我会做到。」

===Original===
“I don’t want you to get him off,” Sylvia scornfully retorted. “I want him vindicated.”

“You see, though,” Orr with unruffleable calm continued, “if a witness should pop up, a witness, let us say, whom I cannot discredit, vindication will be difficult. It will be difficult to make twelve imbeciles in a pen believe that when Annandale shot Loftus—”

“He never shot him,” Sylvia cried.

“My dear cousin,” Orr with the same unruffleable calm pursued, “the beauty of your faith is wonderful. You must come to court and inject it among the jury. Faith that used to move mountains may yet move men. But I doubt it. I doubt that it could make them credit the incredible, the fact patent to me as it should be to you, that though Annandale shot Loftus he was, and for that matter still is, totally unconscious of it.”

===Chinese===
「我不要你救他出来，」西尔维娅轻蔑地顶回去，「我要他昭雪。」

「可是你看，」奥尔以不动如山的从容接着说，「倘若真冒出一个证人——比方说，一个我没法拆穿的证人——那昭雪便难了。要叫围栏里的十二个傻子相信：安南代尔开枪打洛夫特斯的时候——」

「他从没打他，」西尔维娅叫道。

「亲爱的表妹，」奥尔以同样不动如山的从容追下去，「你这信心的美，真是妙不可言。你得上法庭去，把它注射到陪审团身上。从前能移山的信心，说不定还能移人。可我怀疑。我怀疑它能叫他们采信那不可信之事——一件对我、对你都应该昭然若揭的事实：安南代尔虽然打了洛夫特斯，他对这事却是、且至今仍是全然无意识的。」

===Original===
“He never shot him.”

“My dear Sylvia, forgive me. He did. Though what I can say for him and, if needful, I shall say, is that he did not mean to. The intent is the essence of crime. There was no intent here. Of his own free will the man would not hurt a fly. But that night he was not a free agent. He was not even a conscious agent. Of all the cells of his brain but one was awake. In that cell was an incitement inciting him to kill. When the other cells awoke that one cell fell asleep. It has been dormant since then. Only through hypnosis could it awaken. In the interim he knew no more than a somnambulist what he was about. His condition, though, was not somnambulistic, it was a case of psychical epilepsy, a malady superinducible in certain natures by various poisons, of which anger is one and alcohol another.”

===Chinese===
「他从没打他。」

「亲爱的西尔维娅，恕我直言。他打了。但我能为他辩、必要时也会为他辩的是——他并非有意。意图是犯罪的本质。本案没有意图。凭他自己的意志，这人连一只苍蝇也不肯伤。可那一夜他不是一个自由的行为者。他甚至算不得一个有意识的行为者。他脑子所有的细胞里，只有一个醒着。那一个细胞里盛着一道煽动他去杀的指令。其余细胞醒来时，那一个细胞便睡了过去。从那以后它一直蛰伏。唯有催眠才能唤醒它。在那之前，他对自己做过什么，并不比一个梦游者知道得更多。不过他的状态并非梦游，而是一桩精神癫痫——这种病在某些天性的人身上，可由种种毒物诱发，其中愤怒是一种，酒精又是一种。」

===Original===
Orr paused. He looked at his cousin. Incredulity, something else besides, was in her face. He affected not to notice it. “Now,” he ran on, “go with a story like that to the average jury. Of course, if need be, I shall have experts, the very best experts, to substantiate it. But the prosecution will have other experts, experts who will be just as good, to deny the possibility of any such thing. In that event it will be only a pleasure to mix them up a bit and to show by their own testimony that they know no more than the law—I don’t say allows but—pays them for. Do you mind if I smoke?”

They were seated in the sombre parlor in Irving Place. Meditatively Orr lit a cigarette. Meditatively Sylvia contemplated him.

“Would it not be better,” she presently asked, “to show that Loftus committed suicide?”

“Yes, in the event that the pistol is found. It is rather late, though, for that.”

===Chinese===
奥尔顿了顿。他看着表妹。她脸上有不信，还有别的什么。他装作没瞧见。「你想，」他接着说，「拿着这么一套说辞去见寻常的陪审团。当然，必要时我会请专家——最好的专家——来坐实它。可控方也会请别的专家，一样好的专家，来否认这种事的可能性。到了那种地步，把他们搅一搅、借他们自己的口供证明他们所知的并不比法律——我不说法律所容许的，而说法律付他们报酬的那部分——更多，那便只是一件乐事。我抽支烟你介意吗？」

他们坐在欧文广场那间幽暗的起居室里。奥尔若有所思地点上一支烟。西尔维娅若有所思地端详着他。

「不如证明洛夫特斯是自杀，」她过了一会儿问，「岂不更好？」

「是啊，万一那把手枪找得到的话。不过这事如今已嫌迟了。」

===Original===
Sylvia bent forward. “Melanchthon,” she said, “I have heard you say—have I not—that everything is possible?”

“Indeed you have and you will hear me again.”

“Then why not ask Miranda?”

Orr looked about for a cendrier; finding one he put his cigarette in it. “You mean the medium. Do you know, I would in a minute, were it not that it will be a long time, perhaps years, before she or any other spook could call Loftus up. When a man is snuffed out as abruptly as he was, he is so stunned and confused that it is quite a while before he can sufficiently collect his wits to reply to any communications from these latitudes. It is tedious that it should be so. The spirit world needs remodeling. But there you are. By the way, where are you to be this summer?”

===Chinese===
西尔维娅俯身向前。「梅兰希顿，」她说，「我听你说过——不是吗？——凡事皆有可能？」

「你听过的，往后还会再听。」

「那为何不去问米兰达？」

奥尔四下里找一只 cendrier（烟灰缸）；找着了一只，便把烟搁了进去。「你是说那位灵媒。你可知道，我倒愿意立刻去问，要不是她或别的什么鬼魂，得过好久——也许是好几年——才能把洛夫特斯招上来。一个人被这样猝不及防地掐灭，他是那样惊骇、那样糊涂，要好一阵子才能把神志收拢得足以答复这边的任何讯息。它竟如此，未免令人厌烦。灵界需要改造一番。可事实就是如此。对了，今年夏天你打算去哪儿？」

===Original===
Sylvia made a gesture. She did not know. It was then June. Fashion had fled. Fifth Avenue was empty. The town was an oven. In that oven the girl would have preferred to remain. But at the preference her mother had rebelled. Against Newport Sylvia had rebelled also. She was in no mood for its gaiety. Finally a little place on Long Island suggested itself. Ultimately there they went.

It was in this place that Sylvia heard from Mrs. Price of Fanny’s illness. Fanny had disappointed her exceedingly. That she could have so much as contemplated the step which she had in view seemed to Sylvia unspeakable. Her threat, too, in regard to testifying against her husband was in the circumstances but a flagrant avowal of love for the other man. Yet, for that love, how had she been punished! Perhaps now she repented of it. Perhaps now in her illness she needed someone to whom she could unburden her heart. At the thought of that Sylvia wrote at once to Mrs. Price asking might she not come to her. But to this Mrs. Price replied that Fanny after an attack of nervous prostration was now down with typhoid, though with every prospect and assurance of recovery. When she was up again, then, if Sylvia would come, it would, Mrs. Price added, be nice of her.

===Chinese===
西尔维娅做了个手势。她不知道。那时是六月。时髦人都已出逃。第五大道空空荡荡。城里像个烤箱。在那烤箱里，姑娘倒宁可留下来。可母亲不依。西尔维娅也不肯去纽波特。她无心享乐。终于，长岛上一处小地方被想起。末了她们便去了那儿。

正是在这地方，西尔维娅从普莱斯太太信中得知范妮病了。范妮令她大失所望。她竟曾动念要走那一步——在西尔维娅看来，这简直不可言说。她那番要出庭指证丈夫的威胁，在这种情形下，也不过是赤裸裸地承认了她对另一个男人的爱。然而，为着这份爱，她受了怎样的惩罚！也许她如今悔了。也许她在病中正需要一个可以倾吐心事的人。一念及此，西尔维娅立刻给普莱斯太太写信，问她可否让她过去。可普莱斯太太回信说，范妮先是犯了神经衰弱，如今又染上了伤寒，虽则康复在望、保无大碍。等她好起来能下床了，那时西尔维娅若肯来，普莱斯太太补了一句，那便再好不过。

===Original===
There is a saying trite yet true that we should hasten to cherish those whom we love lest they leave us forever before we have loved them enough. There is another saying less true and more trite that of those that do leave only good should be said. To Sylvia presently these sayings recurred. Two days after the receipt of the letter from Mrs. Price she read in the papers that Fanny was dead.

The paper fell from her. For an hour, which passed as only such hours do pass, incomprehensibly, without consciousness of time, she sat, still and stricken.

Through raveled skeins of thought of which the tangled threads refused to wholly straighten, she blamed herself for all that had occurred. Not indeed for Loftus. The man, his life, his death, everything concerning him was abhorrent to her. Of him, other than that pity which can mingle with disgust, she had no concern whatever. But when she should have stood most steadfastly by Annandale she had turned from him. Had he not implored her forgiveness, and did she not know that all that God requires is that forgiveness be asked? But no. She had been too proud and that pride she had nursed until it was too late, until Annandale had married, with this double tragedy for climax.

===Chinese===
有句老话虽滥却真：我们当赶紧去珍惜所爱之人，免得他们在我们爱够之前便永远离去。还有一句老话，没那么真，却更滥：对于已逝者，唯当称善。这两句话，此刻在西尔维娅脑中重现。收到普莱斯太太的信两天后，她在报上读到：范妮死了。

报纸从她手中滑落。有一个钟头——那种钟头只能以那种钟头所特有的方式度过，不可解地，毫无时间之感——她坐着，一动不动，呆若木鸡。

透过一缕缕乱成一团、怎么也理不顺的思绪，她把发生的一切归咎于自己。当然不是为洛夫特斯。这人、他的生、他的死、关于他的一切，都令她厌恶。除了那可以与厌恶相掺的怜悯之外，她对他再无半点挂念。可她本该最坚定地站在安南代尔身旁时，她却背过了身。他岂不曾恳求她宽恕？她岂不知道神所要求的，不过是请人宽恕？可她偏不。她太骄傲了，而这骄傲她一直豢养着，直到太迟——直到安南代尔娶了亲，终以这双重悲剧作结。

===Original===
It was all her fault, Sylvia told herself. All her own. Had she not abandoned Annandale he would have had no cause to threaten, Fanny would have lived, there would have been no shock to debilitate her and leave her a prey to disease. Fanny’s death was at her door.

Companioned by these thoughts for an hour she sat, still and stricken. When she aroused herself it seemed as though before her two figures stood. One said “I am Duty,” the other, “I am Grief.”

A message from the latter she imparted to Mrs. Price. Many messages not similar but cognate that lady received. Fanny had been very popular. Her popularity the rumor connecting her with Loftus had necessarily impaired. The arrest of her husband for shooting the man, and for shooting him, as it was generally understood, on her account, impaired it still more. In the upper circles the scandalous may be relished, but it is not endorsed. Had Fanny lived, those circles would have visited their displeasure in not visiting her at all. But death is a peacemaker. It comes and where there was war is a truce. By the worldly Fanny was immediately forgiven and by them as quickly forgot.

===Chinese===
这一切都是她的错，西尔维娅对自己说。全是她一个人的。她若不曾抛弃安南代尔，他便没有威胁的理由，范妮便不会死，便不会有那一击令她虚耗、沦为疾病的猎物。范妮的死，要算在她账上。

这些念头作伴，她坐了一个钟头，一动不动，呆若木鸡。她回过神来时，仿佛眼前立着两个人影。一个说「我是本分」，另一个说「我是悲恸」。

后者的口信，她转达给了普莱斯太太。那位太太收到许多这样并非雷同、却彼此相通的口信。范妮一向广得人心。可把她与洛夫特斯连在一起的那则传言，难免伤了这份人缘。她丈夫因枪杀那人——而且据众人的理解，是因她才枪杀——被捕，更伤了几分。在上流圈子里，丑闻可以津津乐道，却不得认可。范妮若活着，那些圈子本会以全不登门来施加他们的不悦。可死亡是和事佬。它一来，战火之处便成休战。世俗之人立刻宽恕了范妮，又同样迅速地把她忘掉。

===Original===
It was in July that she died. In September Sylvia returned to town. At once she asked Orr to arrange for her a visit to Annandale in the Tombs.

To that he objected. “You know,” he said, “that you will have to testify against him.”

“Against him!” Sylvia repeated with an air of utter surprise.

“Why, yes. He was here that night. He has admitted it. You will be asked to tell what he said.”

In Sylvia’s eyes both disdain and acquiescence surged. “And what of it?”

“But,” Orr exclaimed, “there is the threat. He made it in the presence of Harris and repeated it in yours.”

“He did nothing of the kind.”

“But you told me so.”

“You are mistaken. I know nothing of any threat whatever.”

“Oh,” said Orr with a bow, “this is magnificent.”

===Chinese===
她是在七月里死的。九月，西尔维娅回到城里。她立刻请奥尔替她安排去「坟墓」（the Tombs）探视安南代尔。

对此他反对。「你知道，」他说，「你将不得不出庭指证他。」

「指证他！」西尔维娅一脸彻底的惊讶重复道。

「是啊。那一夜他在你家。这一点他承认了。你会被问及他说了什么。」

西尔维娅眼里既涌起轻蔑，也涌起顺从。「那又如何？」

「可是，」奥尔叫道，「还有那威胁。他是在哈里斯面前说的，又在你面前重复。」

「他没做这种事。」

「可你是这么告诉我的。」

「你记错了。我什么威胁都不知道。」

「哦，」奥尔鞠了一躬，「这真是了不起。」

===Original===
But he meant heroic. In view of the girl’s nature it was certainly that. What is more, it was helpful. With Fanny out of the way, the only one left that could testify to any threat was Harris, and Annandale’s word was quite as good as his, better even, for the value of the servant’s testimony would be weighed in scales in one of which would be the Chronicle’s dollars.

Orr said as much to Sylvia, but apparently his views did not seem to her very novel. It became obvious to him that she had thought it all out for herself.

“Besides,” she presently and irrelevantly continued, “I am to blame. If I had not been stupid with him, there would have been nothing to threaten about.”

That, Orr thought, was rather putting the dots on the i’s. But he did not mind. He was pleased with her. His respect for her had increased. Had she been the kind of a cousin to permit such a thing there and then he would have kissed her.

===Chinese===
可他指的是英勇。以这姑娘的性情而言，确乎英勇。更有甚者，这大有助益。范妮既已不在，剩下唯一能为任何威胁作证的便只有哈里斯，而安南代尔的话和他的一样管用，甚至更管用——因为那仆人证词的分量，要在一只秤盘里盛着《纪事报》银元的天平上称量。

奥尔把这话向西尔维娅说了，可显然她并不觉得他的见解有什么新鲜。他看得出，这一切她早已自己想清楚了。

「再者，」她过了一会儿，不着边际地接着说，「是我不好。要不是我当年对他犯蠢，便没有什么可威胁的了。」

这话，奥尔想，未免是在 i 上点了一点。但他并不介意。他为她高兴。他对她的敬意更增几分。她若是那种肯让人当场这么做的表妹，他当时便会吻她。

===Original===
Yet some reward he felt was her due. As a result the interview which she asked he presently arranged. Under conditions which to her were as tragic as they were humiliating she saw Annandale in the visitors’ room at the Tombs. The room itself was not absolutely appalling, and though there was a keeper present, he was quite out of earshot, very oblivious, extremely civil and, parenthetically, handsomely paid.

Orr awaited her at the door. When she rejoined him her eyes were wet.

Orr looked at her. A little tune occurred to him. “Sylvia, Sylvia, I’m a-thinking—” But after all, he reflected, Fanny is dead.

Instantly the girl reddened and very distantly, her head in the air, announced, “We are betrothed.”

===Chinese===
然而他觉得，她总该有些酬报。于是她所求的那次探视，他不久便安排妥了。在一种于她既悲惨又屈辱的氛围里，她在「坟墓」的探视室见了安南代尔。这屋子本身倒并不绝对骇人，虽然有一名看守在场，却远在听觉之外，十分健忘，极其客气，而且——附带一提——报酬颇丰。

奥尔在门口等她。她回到他身边时，眼眶是湿的。

奥尔看着她。他脑中浮起一支小调：「西尔维娅，西尔维娅，我在想——」可毕竟，他想，范妮死了。

姑娘立刻红了脸，远远地、高高仰着头，宣告：「我们订婚了。」

===Original===
“Ah,” said Orr, “ah, indeed! The engagement will be rather long, I fear.”

“Oh, Melanchthon, don’t say that. Arthur is as innocent as you are. I know you don’t believe it, but—”

Orr interrupted her. “It is not a question of what I believe. Independent of your interest in the man he is my client. I owe him a duty. That duty is to get him off, or to do my best to.”

“I know you will,” Sylvia fervently replied; “I feel it. So does Arthur. Besides, the only one we have to fear is Harris.”

Orr smiled grimly. “Harris, I understand, is not very well.”

“Not well? What do you mean?” the girl wonderingly inquired.

“I mean,” he enigmatically answered, “that next week when I have him on the stand I propose to give him a little medicine.”

Then he smiled again, grimly as before, with an air of personal satisfaction.

===Chinese===
「啊，」奥尔说，「啊，真的！我怕这婚约会拖得颇久。」

「哦，梅兰希顿，别这么说。阿瑟和你一样清白。我知道你不信，可是——」

奥尔打断她。「我信什么并不相干。撇开你对这人的关切不谈，他是我的委托人。我对他有一桩本分。这桩本分便是救他出来，或竭力去做。」

「我知道你会，」西尔维娅热切地答，「我感觉得到。阿瑟也感觉得到。再说，我们唯一要怕的只有哈里斯。」

奥尔冷酷地笑了笑。「据我所知，哈里斯不太舒服。」

「不舒服？你什么意思？」姑娘诧异地问。

「我的意思是，」他语带玄机地答，「下星期我让他上证人席时，打算给他吃一点药。」

然后他又笑了，和方才一样冷酷，一脸私心满意的气色。

## VIII The Defendant to the Bar / VIII 被告至庭

===Original===
“Hats off!”
Through the great white room the cry vibrated, followed instantly by another:

“Hear ye, hear ye, all ye having business with the Court of the General Sessions of the City and County of New York, draw near, give attention and ye shall be heard.”

Within the Bar, restless as hyenas awaiting their prey, roamed the district attorneys. Against that Bar, crouching there, were Orr and his associate counsel, restless too, but prepared to spring. To the rear were reporters, the flower of newspaperdom, handsome young men dressed to the ears in resplendent collars and astounding cravats. Back of them were the spectators, a solid mass, ladies of every degree except the high one and, with or without them, men whom you would recognize as first-nighters, others whom you would not recognize at all. To the right of the Bar were witnesses for the prosecution, experts in various matters of which gastronomy evidently was one. To the left was the jury, and above, beneath the amber panoply of the Bench, the Recorder sat, an ascetic Solon.

The atmosphere of the room, high ceiled, close packed, was Senegambian. Without you could see, within you could feel, the heat and eagerness of the autumnal sun.

===Chinese===
「脱帽！」

这一声喊穿透了那间高大的白色厅堂，紧跟着又是一声：

「听着，听着，凡与纽约市县一般刑事法庭（Court of the General Sessions）有公干者，上前，留神听，尔等必得闻。」

法庭围栏之内，地区检察官们像一群伺候猎物的鬣狗，焦躁地踱来踱去。围栏外，伏在那儿的，是奥尔和他的搭档律师，同样焦躁，却蓄势待发。再往后是记者们——新闻界之花——一帮英俊的年轻人，衣领光辉夺目，领结骇人听闻。他们身后是旁听席，黑压压一片，各等的太太小姐，唯独没有最高一等的那一档，陪她们或不陪她们的，有你一眼认得出的「首演常客」，也有你全然认不出的别人。法庭右首是控方证人，种种行当的专家，其中显然有美食学一门。左首是陪审团，再上方，法官席那一派琥珀色的陈设之下，坐着法官（the Recorder），一位苦行僧式的梭伦（Solon）。

这间高顶、拥挤的厅堂，其空气是塞内冈比亚（Senegambian）式的。外头你能看见、里头你能感到，秋日骄阳的那股燥热与迫切。

===Original===
“Arthur Annandale to the Bar!”

Into the court, as though it were a theatre, the defendant strolled, perfectly groomed, the Tombs pallor on his face but none of its dust on his coat, an air of tranquil boredom about him. At his heels was a keeper. He shook hands with Orr, sat down beside him, turned and gave his hat to the keeper, turned again and looked over to a gated enclosure at the right of the Bench where, in a sort of proscenium box, Sylvia sat with her mother.

The entire settings were those of a play. With this difference, it was real, a drama of mud and blood without orchestral accompaniment. After months of preparation, after days of talesmen baiting, on this Indian Summer forenoon the curtain was rising. The jury it had been a job to get. A full hundred were examined, cross-questioned, challenged and rejected before the dozen were boxed. When the last, the twelfth, a cadaverous individual, was accepted the stage was set.

===Chinese===
「阿瑟·安南代尔至庭！」

被告走进法庭，仿佛走进一家戏院，他修饰得无可挑剔，脸上带着「坟墓」里待出来的那种苍白，外套上却一尘不染，一副气定神闲、百无聊赖的神气。身后跟着一名看守。他与奥尔握手，在他身旁坐下，转身把帽子递给看守，又转过身，朝法官席右边一处有栏的小隔间望去——在那里，像坐在一个台口包厢里似的，西尔维娅和她母亲并肩而坐。

整个布景正如一出戏。区别只在于：它是真的，一出土与血的戏剧，没有乐队伴奏。经数月的筹备，经数日的陪审员遴选折磨，在这小阳春的上午，帷幕升起。陪审团是费了好大力气才凑齐的。整整一百人被盘问、反诘、否决、淘汰，方才凑齐这一打。待最后一位——第十二位，一个面如死灰的人——被接受，戏台便搭就了。

===Original===
“May it please the Court; Mr. Foreman and gentlemen of the jury.”

With three bows and these rituals, Peacock opened for the State, outlining the case of the People, describing the crime, detailing the motive, summarizing the evidence, expressing the wish that the jury would believe the defendant innocent until his guilt had been proved, but declaring that, personally, for his own part, of that guilt he was thoroughly convinced.

Before he had finished Orr was at him. “I object to the District Attorney prejudicing the jury against this gentleman, my client.”

That gentleman did not appear to heed. From Sylvia and her mother he had turned to look at the spectators, from them to fabulous beasts that climbed the fluted columns on the walls.

The objection was not sustained.

“And I object to Your Honor’s ruling,” Orr with a bulldog look threw up at the Bench.

Peacock proceeded. “There, gentlemen, is the crime, there too, the motive. To finish the picture evidence will be adduced.”

===Chinese===
「愿法庭见谅；陪审团首席先生，各位陪审员先生。」

三鞠躬加上这一套仪节之后，皮科克代表控方开场，勾勒人民一方的案情，描绘罪行，详述动机，概述证据，表示希望陪审团在被告罪证被证实之前以他为无罪——却又宣称，就他个人而言，对这桩罪，他是彻底信了的。

他话未说完，奥尔便扑了上去。「我反对地区检察官对陪审团施加成见、加害这位先生，我的委托人。」

那位先生似乎并不在意。他先望过西尔维娅和她母亲，转而望向旁听席，又从旁听席望到壁上那几根凹槽石柱、以及攀缘其上的怪兽。

那异议未被采纳。

「那么我也反对法官阁下的裁决，」奥尔以一副斗牛犬的神气朝法官席甩上去。

皮科克继续。「诸位，那便是罪行，那便是动机。为了把这幅画补全，证据即将呈上。」

===Original===
He sat down. Then getting up, he called the first witness for the People, the Gramercy Park caretaker, who had found the body. The witness was succeeded by others, by the policeman on the beat, by the coroner’s physician, by experts and servants.

By turn Orr took them in hand. With some he was curiously perfunctory. Of the caretaker, a meagre old man, with shifty eyes, who appeared very uncomfortable, he asked but four questions.

“When you found the body what did you do?”

“Ran and got the policeman, sir.”

“Where did you get him?”

“On Lexington Avenue and Twenty-third Street, sir.”

“Did you find him at once?”

“No, sir, I had to hunt a bit.”

“Between the time you found the body and the time you got back how many minutes would you say had elapsed?”

“About ten or fifteen minutes, sir.”

“That’s all,” said Orr.

===Chinese===
他坐下。继而又起身，传控方头一位证人——那位发现尸体的格拉默西公园看守。这位之后又是别人，巡逻的警察、验尸官的医师、各色专家与仆役。

奥尔逐一接过他们。对有的证人，他敷衍得令人称奇。对那个看守——一个瘦干的老头，眼神闪烁，显得浑身不自在——他只问了四个问题。

「你发现尸体之后做了什么？」

「跑去叫警察，先生。」

「你在哪儿找到他的？」

「莱克星顿大道和二十三街的交口，先生。」

「你立刻就找到他了吗？」

「没有，先生，我找了一阵。」

「从你发现尸体那一刻到你回来，你说中间过了几分钟？」

「大约十到十五分钟，先生。」

「完了，」奥尔说。

===Original===
It was not much. Yet with the policeman, a fat man with a red face and a blue nose, he was even briefer.

“When you reached the park with the last witness, how did you get in?”

“Walked in, sir,” the man answered with a grin.

“The gate was open was it?”

“Yes, sir.”

“That will do,” said Orr.

It was not much either. But with other witnesses, notably with the experts, he fought, he fought with them, fought with Peacock, fought with the Court, would have fought with more had there been more to fight, fought pertinaciously, step by step, reducing testimony to nothing.

Meanwhile the courtroom shimmered with silks. Wanderers from Fifth Avenue who never in their lives had been in the General Sessions before begged and badgered their way there. It is great fun to see a man tried for his life. But when you have known him, when in addition elements supersensational blend like a halo about him, what more could be decently asked? Yet one thing disappointed. It was regrettable that the prisoner was not in chains, that he could sit there and yawn with every appearance of being at a matinee, a keeper for lackey behind him.

===Chinese===
不多。可对那个警察——一个红脸蓝鼻子的胖子——他问得更短。

「你跟刚才那位证人到公园时，你怎么进去的？」

「走进去的，先生，」那人咧嘴一笑答道。

「门是开着的，是吗？」

「是的，先生。」

「行了，」奥尔说。

也不多。可对别的证人，尤其是对那些专家，他大打出手，跟他们打，跟皮科克打，跟法庭打，倘若还有更多可打的，他还会打更多，他锲而不舍，步步紧逼，把证词一一化为乌有。

这其间法庭里丝绸闪烁。从第五大道流浪来的那些人，平生从未涉足一般刑事法庭，如今却央求、强挤着挤了进来。看一个人受生死之审，原是极大乐事。可倘若你还认识他，倘若再加上一些耸人听闻的元素像光环一样缭绕着他，还能体面地再奢求什么呢？唯有一桩令人扫兴。可惜被告未上镣铐，他能坐在那里打哈欠，活脱一副日场戏的样子，身后一名看守权充跟班。

===Original===
Otherwise the fun, if not fast, was furious. Peacock would ask a question, the lips of a witness would part but before more than a fraction of a syllable could issue Orr would hold him up, hold up the prosecution, hold up the Court. Generally he was overruled. But no overruling abashed him. He arose from opposition refreshing. There were times when Sylvia thought him bowed to the earth, utterly routed, hushed for good. But not a bit of it. At the moment when his ammunition seemed exhausted and his defeat assured, from an arsenal of books before him he pulled weapons wherewith not merely to renew the fight but to win.

In the course of one objection he was commanded by the Bench to sit down. He protested. The Recorder declined to listen to him further, reiterating the order that he be seated. Then with the air and manner of a little boy sent for misbehavior from the room, Orr half turned, hesitated, turned back, and through the exercise of guile unique and his own, succeeded in reengaging the Court in conversation, protesting his respect, denying his contumacy and presently he was continuing the very objection because of which he had been told to sit down. He did sit down, but long after, when he was ready, when he had succeeded in having his say and his way. Then when at last he did sit down it was with an air of mastery that would have become Napoleon at Marengo. At the moment he was not a lawyer merely, he was an actor, quasi-Shakespearian, a compound of irony and good humor, Falstaff and Mercutio in one.

===Chinese===
除此之外，这乐事虽说不密集，倒也激烈。皮科克问一个问题，证人嘴唇才一张，连半个音节还没漏出来，奥尔便把他叫停，把控方叫停，把法庭叫停。一般他的异议都被驳回。可没有哪一次驳回能叫他难堪。他从对抗中恢复过来，越发精神。有时西尔维娅以为他被压得抬不起头、彻底溃败、就此噤声。可没那回事。就在他弹药似已耗尽、败局已定之时，他从面前那一座书的兵工厂里抽出武器，不仅重开战火，更且夺胜。

有一次提异议时，法官席命他坐下。他抗议。法官拒绝再听他讲，重申命他就坐的命令。于是奥尔——带着一个因顽皮被赶出屋子的小男孩的神气——半侧过身，迟疑一下，又转回来，凭着一份独一无二、属于他自己的狡狯，成功地重新把法庭拖入对话，一面申明自己的敬意，一面否认自己不敬，不多时便又开始继续那桩因之被命坐下的异议。他确是坐下了——可那是许久之后，在他自己预备好了、在他成功地既说了话又遂了愿之后。待他终于坐下，那一副主子的气派，便是拿破仑在马伦哥（Marengo）也不为过。此时他不止是一名律师，他是一名演员，近乎莎士比亚式的，是反讽与好脾气的混合体，福斯塔夫（Falstaff）与茂丘西奥（Mercutio）合而为一。

===Original===
All this, however, was, to vary the metaphor, but the preliminary canter. That Loftus had been killed was shown and admitted. But it had not been shown nor was it admitted that the defendant was the man. This defect a star witness was to repair. The star was Harris.

Yet, though a star, he looked ghastly. Whether ill or not, he was at least ill at ease. The smug, household-servant air had gone. He seemed to have come from turmoils in Tatterdemalia. He was bruised, dirty, unshorn. But the story which he had brought to the Chronicle he repeated, with embellishments at that. After retailing the tale, precising the motive and elaborating on it, he declared that the love of the defendant’s wife for Loftus was common talk—evidence which, though hearsay, Orr indifferently let pass.

Then, after identifying a pistol as the property of Annandale—an exhibit marked A which Peacock had already tried but, held up by Orr, had not wholly succeeded in fitting to the crime—Harris swore that on the night of the murder, at five minutes after twelve, in the room which he occupied at the top of Annandale’s house and which overlooked Gramercy Park, he heard a shot; that going to the window he looked out, that he could distinguish nothing, but that going then to the hall he heard someone coming in the house and looking down saw the defendant enter.

===Chinese===
然而这一切，换个比喻说，不过是开场前的试跑。洛夫特斯被杀一事，已被证明、被承认。可被告便是那人——这一点既未被证明，也未被承认。这一缺陷，将由一位明星证人来修补。这位明星便是哈里斯。

可这位明星虽是明星，看上去却惨白得很。他到底是病了还是没病，至少是不自在。那一脸自满的家仆神气已荡然无存。他像是从破衣烂衫之国（Tatterdemalia）的动荡里走出来。他青一块紫一块、一身脏、胡子拉碴。可他带到《纪事报》的那番话，他原原本本又说了一遍，还添油加醋。讲述完毕、坐实了动机并加以铺陈之后，他宣称被告之妻对洛夫特斯的爱乃人尽皆知——这一证据虽属传闻，奥尔却漠然放行。

随后，在指认一把手枪为安南代尔所有之后——这是一件标作 A 号的证物，皮科克曾试图把它配到罪案上，却被奥尔拦下，未能完全得手——哈里斯发誓说，谋杀发生那夜，十二点零五分，在他所住的安南代尔宅顶楼那间可俯瞰格拉默西公园的屋里，他听见一声枪响；他走到窗前往外看，什么也辨不清；于是他走到门厅，听见有人进屋，往下一看，看见被告进来。

===Original===
“Ha!” said Orr, taking him in hand, or rather, by the throat. For he made no attempt at ordinary amenities. He questioned him ferociously, with an air of personal hatred, with an air of saying, “Damn you, I have got it in for you now.”

“What is your name?” he asked.

“Richard Harris, sir.”

Orr pounded on the table in front of him. “Your name! Your name! I want your name, not something that you have made up like the rest of your rubbish. How many times have you been in jail? You were once employed in Hill Street, Berkeley Square, by the Duchess of Kincardine. When you absconded from there, where was it that the police caught you? Answer me.”

===Chinese===
「哈！」奥尔接过他——或者说，掐住他的喉咙——说道。因为他无意讲寻常的客套。他凶狠地盘问他，带着一种私人的憎恨，一副「该死的，这回我可逮着你了」的神气。

「你叫什么名字？」他问。

「理查德·哈里斯，先生。」

奥尔在面前的桌上擂了一拳。「你的名字！你的名字！我要的是你的名字，不要你像其余那些垃圾一样瞎编。你进过几回监狱？你曾在金卡丁公爵夫人（Duchess of Kincardine）手下、伯克利广场（Berkeley Square）的山街（Hill Street）做事。你从那儿卷款潜逃后，警察是在哪儿抓住你的？回答我。」

===Original===
From behind the rail objections exploded like shell. But through the running fire of them Orr held his own, sandbagging the man with one charge after another, charge of theft, charge of forgery, but particularly of boasting the week before, in a Sixth Avenue saloon where grooms and footmen congregate, that he could testify to anything that he was paid for.

From ghastly Harris turned vermilion. The flush retreating left him livid. Had the fluted columns with their fabulous beasts fallen on him he could not have been more limp. At one question he swayed like an animal hit on the head. At another he hissed like a snake. There were times when he tried to hide from view. It was a curious example of the biter bit.

“That’s all,” said Orr with tigerish cheerfulness at last.

===Chinese===

围栏后面，异议像炮弹一般炸了开来。可在这一片连珠炮火里，奥尔稳如磐石，一桩接一桩地朝那人砸沙袋——盗窃的指控、伪造的指控，但尤其是一桩：一个星期之前，在第六大道一家马夫和男仆聚集的酒馆里，他夸口说，凡是给了钱的，他什么都能作证。

惨白的哈里斯转成了猩红。那红潮一退，留下他一脸铁青。便是那几根带怪兽的凹槽石柱塌在他身上，他也瘫不到这般田地。有一个问题，他像一头被击中脑袋的牲口那样摇晃。另一个问题，他像一条蛇那样嘶嘶。有那么几回，他恨不得钻进地缝。这真是「咬人者反被咬」（the biter bit）的一桩奇例。

「完了，」奥尔终于露出虎一般的欢快。

===Original===
He had done him. He had given him the medicine. He had more in reserve. Peacock meanwhile had once jumped at Orr, his fist raised. Once he gave him the lie direct. Once he accused him of suborning. But Orr in sandbagging the witness with one hand, had another free for the prosecution. He was gluttonish, giving as good as was sent, very often better.

The Recorder, dismayed at the slugging, protested. “A human being is on trial for his life. I cannot try a case where only counsel are heard.”

Immediately Orr supplied him with a diversion. One after another witness for the defense scaled the stand, sleuths from overseas, experts and servants.

===Chinese===
他把他收拾了。他给他吃了那副药。他还留着后手。这其间皮科克曾朝奥尔扑过去，拳头高举。曾当面斥他撒谎。曾指控他收买证人。可奥尔一手砸沙袋般地砸那证人，另一只手却腾出来对付控方。他是个饕餮，来多少还多少，时常还更多。

法官见这场互殴，颇感骇然，开口抗议。「一个活人正为他的性命受审。我不能审一桩只听见律师说话的案子。」

奥尔立刻给了他一个转圜。辩方证人一个接一个登上证人席——海那边来的侦探、专家、仆役。

===Original===
In his corner before them Orr prowled. At the witnesses for the prosecution he had roared, sometimes he had bounded at the Bar, sometimes when a move of his succeeded he raised his right hand and looked at it as though surprised that it was not blood red. But now with his own witnesses he was serene, entirely calm, refreshingly civil.

That civility awoke in Peacock the hyena. The first witness Orr produced, a man who, as it afterward appeared, had had a rough and tumble with Harris that morning in the corridor, he partly devoured. What was left of him he sent to the Tombs. As fast as witnesses could be produced he ate them up. It was terrific. You could not help feeling that there are safer places than the witness stand in a murder trial, that you ran the risk of being killed yourself, talked to death if nothing worse.

===Chinese===
在他那一角，奥尔在他们面前踱来踱去。对控方证人他曾咆哮，有时扑到围栏前，有时一招得手便举起右手端详，仿佛惊奇它竟不是血红的。可如今面对自己的证人，他安详，完全沉静，客气得令人耳目一新。

这一份客气，倒激起了皮科克身上的鬣狗。奥尔所传的头一位证人——此人后来才晓得，那天早上在走廊里刚和哈里斯有过一场恶斗——被他吞掉了一半。剩下的那点，他送进了「坟墓」。辩方证人传上来一个，他便吃一个。这真是骇人。你不由得感到：一桩谋杀案的受审中，确有比证人席更安全的地方；你随时有送命的危险，退一万步，也会被话说到死。

===Original===
“Don’t go at him like a common scold,” Orr engagingly pleaded at one stage of the game. “Why browbeat and bully a witness as you do?” he expostulated at another. “That’s all, my friend,” he said to one witness, “and let me apologize for the District Attorney’s remarks.” From his tone and manner never in the world would you have thought him the man who, but a little before, had so thoroughly sandbagged Harris.

Meanwhile questions coarse as oaths, answers frank as sword thrusts, clashed and resounded. One and all Orr’s charges were substantiated. The testimony was damning to Harris, infecting everything he had said. From behind the rail Peacock volleyed and thundered. But truth when you get at it is a stubborn thing. So far as Harris was concerned there it stood and there too, during the production of it, Orr stood, quite like an Angora lapping milk. You could hear him purr. The eyes of Sylvia glistened like mica. Now and again Annandale laughed outright.

===Chinese===
「别像只泼妇骂街似的扑他，」在战局某处，奥尔和颜悦色地求情。「你为何这般威吓、欺凌证人？」在另一处他规劝。「完了，朋友，」他对一位证人说，「让我替地区检察官的话向你致歉。」看他这副口气与神情，世上再没人会想到，就是他，不多一会儿之前，把哈里斯收拾得那样彻底。

这其间，粗如咒骂的提问、利如剑刺的回答，铿锵交击。奥尔的一桩桩指控，桩桩坐实。证词对哈里斯是毁灭性的，把他所说的一切都染上了毒。围栏后面，皮科克又轰又雷。可真相一旦追到，是顽强的。就哈里斯而言，它就立在那里；而在它被呈出的整个过程里，奥尔也立在那里，活像一只舔奶的安哥拉猫。你能听见他呼噜。西尔维娅的眼睛闪闪发亮，像云母。安南代尔时不时纵声大笑。

===Original===
It is always insufficient to be innocent of a given charge. You must appear so. Annandale did not. Alternately he was bored and buoyant. But not dejected, never depressed. He did not seem to feel that his life was at stake. That is the attitude of the habitual ruffian. But sentiment was veering. Public opinion is a wave that thinks, thinks again, changes its mind, volatile as a woman. At the opening everybody knew that Annandale was guilty. Now nobody was quite so sure.

The Recorder caressed his beard. “I think,” he announced, “that I will give the jury a recess.”

===Chinese===
仅仅无辜于一桩指控，向来是不够的。你还得显得无辜。安南代尔并不。他时而无聊，时而意气风发。但绝不沮丧，从不颓唐。他似乎不觉得自己的性命系于一线。这原是惯犯无赖的态度。可舆情正在转向。舆论是一股会思想的浪潮，想了又想，改了主意，像女人一样善变。开庭之初，人人都知道安南代尔有罪。如今没谁那么肯定了。

法官抚了抚胡须。「我想，」他宣告，「我给陪审团一次休庭。」

## IX The Twelfth Juror / IX 第十二位陪审员

===Original===
Tumultuously the session was resumed. At the door was a riot. There a squad of police fought back surging nondescripts clamoring for admission, fighting for entrance to the continuous show. A woman fainted. Another had her gown torn off. One man retired with a blackened eye.

During the recess Orr got for a moment with Sylvia and Mrs. Waldron. “Aren’t you hungry?” he asked.

Sylvia took his hand and pressed it. In her eyes was victory, in her face delight. “I never knew before how Protean you are. You have won.”

Orr tossed his head. “Not by a long shot. Besides, there is the jury. Eleven look imbecile and the twelfth looks ill. There is no telling at all what they will do or will not. But aren’t you to eat anything?” He turned to Mrs. Waldron. “Aren’t you hungry?”

===Chinese===
庭审继续，一片喧嚣。门口简直在闹事。一队警察在那里抵挡汹涌的杂色人等，他们吵着要进场，争着挤进这场连台戏。一位女士晕了过去。另一位被扯掉了裙子。一个男人退出场时，一只眼睛乌青。

休庭间，奥尔得空与西尔维娅和沃尔德伦太太相处了片刻。「你们不饿吗？」他问。

西尔维娅握住他的手紧了一紧。她眼里是胜利，脸上是欢喜。「我从前竟不晓得你这般千变万化。你赢了。」

奥尔扬了扬头。「差得远呢。再说，还有陪审团。十一个看着像傻子，第十二个看着像有病。他们要做什么、不做什么，全然说不好。可你真的不吃点东西？」他转向沃尔德伦太太。「您不饿吗？」

===Original===
“Very,” said the lady, “but I can’t do a thing with Sylvia. I—”

She would have said more, but the jury had filed in. The judge was entering, preceded by the cry “Hats off!”

Orr slipped back to his corner, to which Annandale, with his matinee air and the keeper for usher, had already returned. For a moment Orr bent to him, then to his associates but briefly. Bending again to Annandale he told him to take the stand.

The move, wholly unexpected, unusual, almost exceptional in murder cases, created an impression that was excellent, a sense of admiration for the fearlessness of the defense. From the prosecution came low growls of content. They were to be fed at last. In anticipation they licked their chops.

===Chinese===
「饿得很，」夫人说，「可我对西尔维娅毫无办法。我——」

她还想往下说，可陪审团已经鱼贯而入。法官进场，前头一声「脱帽！」

奥尔溜回他那一角，安南代尔——带着他那副日场戏的神气，看守权充引座员——也已回到那里。奥尔俯身向他低语片刻，又简短地向搭档交代几句。再俯向安南代尔，让他上证人席。

这一手全然出人意料，不寻常，在谋杀案中近乎罕见，所造的印象绝佳——为辩方的胆色生出一片赞叹。控方那边传来低低的、满意之声。他们总算要被喂上一口了。他们提前舔起了嘴唇。

===Original===
But the excellence of the impression dwindled. In the direct, Annandale denied, of course, that he had committed the murder, denied that he had ever contemplated it, swearing that to the best of his recollection he had made no threat at all.

“To the best of your recollection,” Orr repeated after him. “Now please tell me, had anything occurred that night to impair your memory in any way?”

“Well—er—yes. Yes. I had been drinking.”

“Had you any animosity toward the deceased?”

“Toward Loftus? None whatever. On the contrary, he was my best friend.”

Peacock jumped. “I ask that that be stricken out.”

Quietly Orr continued: “Had you known Loftus long?”

“All my life.”

“Was he a friend of yours?”

“An intimate friend.”

===Chinese===
可这绝佳的印象随之减色。直接询问中，安南代尔当然否认自己犯下谋杀，否认自己曾动过此念，发誓说他凭记忆所及，根本没说过什么威胁。

「凭你的记忆所及，」奥尔跟着他重复。「那么请告诉我，那一夜可有什么事，在某种意义上损害了你的记忆？」

「嗯——呃——有。有。我喝了酒。」

「你对死者可有怨恨？」

「对洛夫特斯？毫无。恰恰相反，他是我最好的朋友。」

皮科克跳起来。「我请求把这一句划掉。」

奥尔不动声色地继续：「你认识洛夫特斯很久了？」

「一辈子。」

「他是你的朋友？」

「挚友。」

===Original===
Orr turned to Peacock. “Your witness.”

Peacock jumped again. “You say that on the night of the murder you had been drinking. Were you drunk?”

Paternally the Recorder looked over and down. “The witness need not answer that unless—”

Annandale interrupted him. “I am much obliged to Your Honor, but really I have nothing to conceal. I was drunk, deplorably so.”

“Habit of yours, is it?” Peacock snapped.

Annandale took a monocle from a pocket, screwed it in his eye, looked through it at Peacock, smiled at him, with an air of fathomless good fellowship, answered: “Dear me, no. Is it one of yours?”

“Oho!” cried Peacock, pocketing the insult but pouncing at the point, “you were drunk on this occasion only. Got drunk for it, did you?”

===Chinese===
奥尔转向皮科克。「你的证人。」

皮科克又跳起来。「你说谋杀那夜你喝了酒。你醉了吗？」

法官慈父一般俯视下来。「证人可以不作答，除非——」

安南代尔打断他。「多谢法官阁下，可我实在没什么要隐瞒的。我醉了，醉得一塌糊涂。」

「是你的老习惯吧，嗯？」皮科克厉声道。

安南代尔从口袋里取出一只单片眼镜，旋进眼眶，透过它望着皮科克，朝他一笑，一副深不可测的交情气派，答道：「天哪，不。倒是您的老习惯吗？」

「哦嗬！」皮科克叫道，把那句冒犯揣进口袋，却扑向那要害，「你只这一回醉了。是为这事特意喝醉的，是吗？」

===Original===
“No,” Annandale blandly and confidentially replied. “You see, don’t you know, it was the day of the panic. I had dropped a good lot of money—a good lot, I mean, for me—and, as the saying is, I tried to drown my sorrows.”

“But you found that they could swim, didn’t you? Now, tell me, among these sorrows was not the greatest the one to which your former butler has testified, your late wife’s desire for a divorce in order that she might marry Loftus? Is it not a fact that she told you so, and that you then said, ‘I’ll kill him, I’ll kill Royal Loftus like the dog that he is’?”

“I recall no such conversation.”

“What, then, was the nature of the conversation that passed between you and your wife on this particular evening?”

“I don’t remember.”

“The conversation and the threat to which your butler has sworn may therefore have occurred without your now recalling it. Is that not so?”

===Chinese===
「不，」安南代尔和气而推心置腹地答，「你想，不是吗，那天正是恐慌之日。我赔了一大笔钱——一大笔，我是说，对我而言——于是，俗话说，我试图借酒浇愁。」

「可你发觉那些愁会游泳，是不是？那么，告诉我，在这些愁之中，最大的那桩，岂不正是你前管家所证的那一桩——你亡妻为了能嫁给洛夫特斯而要离婚？她岂不是对你明说了，而你当时岂不是说：『我要宰了他，我要像宰一条狗那样宰了罗伊尔·洛夫特斯』？」

「我不记得有过这样的对话。」

「那么，那一晚你和你妻子之间所发生的对话，是什么性质？」

「我不记得。」

「那么，你管家所发誓的那番对话与威胁，可能确实发生过，只是你如今记不起来。是不是这样？」

===Original===
“Everything is possible, you know,” Annandale answered with a phrase unconsciously borrowed of Orr. “But I doubt it very much for the reason—”

“Here,” interrupted Peacock. “I don’t want your doubts or your reasons or your haha airs. I want answers from you, direct answers. Where did you go and what did you do after your threat?”

To this Orr objected. A wrangle ensued. Orr was sustained. Peacock reconstructed his question. Annandale answered that he had gone to Miss Waldron’s, but that he remembered nothing else.

“Is this yours?” Peacock suddenly asked, producing the pistol marked exhibit A.

“Probably,” said Annandale, looking, not at it, but at the ceiling.

“That’s all.”

===Chinese===
「凡事皆有可能，你知道的，」安南代尔答，无意中借了奥尔一句话。「不过我深表怀疑，理由是——」

「打住，」皮科克打断，「我不要你的怀疑、你的理由、你那些哈哈的派头。我要的是你的回答，直接的回答。你威胁之后去了哪里、做了什么？」

对此奥尔提出异议。一番争辩。奥尔被支持。皮科克重组问题。安南代尔回答说他去了沃尔德伦小姐家，但其余一概不记得。

「这是你的吗？」皮科克忽然问，掏出那把标作 A 号证物的手枪。

「大概是，」安南代尔说，眼睛并不看它，却望着天花板。

「完了。」

===Original===
Annandale got from the stand. Others succeeded him there, experts for the defense, men who recited their qualities and degrees as though they were eating truffles to the sound of trumpets. One after another they testified that liquor can ablate memory partially, wholly; can ablate it regarding events antecedent and subsequent to a rememorated point between; can, moreover, leave the subject in a condition apparently normal yet actually in a state of trance.

“Do you really regard these people as experts?” Peacock with pitying contempt asked of Orr. Then at once in rebuttal were other experts, equally pleased with themselves, humorously disposing of psychical epilepsy, affecting to regard it as a medicolegal myth. Among the spectators the usual jest circulated. The mendacious were subdivided into liars, damned liars, expert witnesses. Yet there you were. But not Orr. Tortuously he involved the deponents in helpful contradictions, smiling at them, at Peacock and the jury, smiling with an air of saying “You see what confounded idiots these imbeciles are.”

===Chinese===
安南代尔下了证人席。继他上去的是别人，辩方的专家们，这些人诵读自己的资历与学位，仿佛在喇叭伴奏下吃松露。他们一个接一个作证：酒能部分地、或全部地抹除记忆；能抹除某一追忆点之前后的事件；更且能令当事人处于一种看似正常、实则出神的状态。

「你当真把这些人物当作专家？」皮科克带着怜悯的鄙夷问奥尔。紧接着反驳方也搬来了另一些专家，一样地自我得意，戏谑地把精神癫痫打发掉，装作视之为法医学上的神话。旁听席上流传着那则老笑话：说谎者被细分为——说谎的人、该死的说谎的人、专家证人。可你能奈何。奥尔却不然。他七拐八绕地把这些证人卷入有助于己的矛盾之中，冲他们、冲皮科克和陪审团微笑，那微笑带着一股「你瞧这些傻子有多该死的蠢」的气色。

===Original===
But the session was closing. One more witness remained to be called.

“Miss Waldron, will you take the stand?”

With the charming manner of the thoroughbred New York girl Sylvia circled the room. It was refreshing to see her, refreshing to hear the way in which she corroborated what Annandale had said.

“But,” objected Peacock, “you had just gone from his house; what did he go to yours for?”

“To restore a string of pearls.”

“Did he repeat to you anything that he had said to his wife?”

“Had he attempted to I should have refused to listen.”

“Was he drunk?”

“I cannot say. I have never seen anyone in that condition.”

“Did he make any threats regarding Loftus?”

“A gentleman does not make threats.”

“Miss Waldron, I will thank you to answer me directly. Did he or did he not?”

“He did not.”

“You swear to that?”

“I do.”

===Chinese===
可庭审将近尾声。还有一位证人待传。

「沃尔德伦小姐，请上证人席？」

西尔维娅以纯种纽约姑娘那种迷人的气度绕场而过。看她是一种清新，听她证实安南代尔所言，也是一种清新。

「可是，」皮科克反驳，「你方才从他家里出来；他到你家去做什么？」

「归还一串珍珠。」

「他可曾对你重复过他对他妻子说过的任何话？」

「他若试图这样做，我也会拒不听闻。」

「他醉了吗？」

「我说不上。我从未见过那种状态下的人。」

「他可曾就洛夫特斯发出任何威胁？」

「绅士不作威胁。」

「沃尔德伦小姐，劳驾你直接回答我。他作了，还是没有？」

「没有。」

「你敢发誓？」

「我发誓。」

===Original===
It was perjury, of course. Yet if a girl may not perjure herself like a lady for the man she loves things have come to a pretty pass. That idea apparently struck Peacock.

“Prior to the defendant’s marriage you were engaged to him, were you not?”

“I was.”

“Are you engaged to him now?”

Very prettily and gracefully, without embarrassment, rather with pride, Sylvia answered: “I am.”

“That’s all,” said Peacock. “The State rests.” But as he said it he looked at the jury and sighed, sighed audibly, much as were he adding, “You may judge the value of her testimony from that.”

===Chinese===
这自然是伪证。可若一位姑娘为了她所爱的人而不能像淑女一般发一回伪证，那世道就太不成话了。这一念头显然也击中了皮科克。

「在被告结婚之前，你与他订过婚，是不是？」

「是的。」

「你如今与他订婚了吗？」

十分娇美而优雅地，毫不窘迫，反而带着骄傲，西尔维娅答：「是的。」

「完了，」皮科克说，「控方停止举证。」可他说这话时，望着陪审团叹了一声，叹得出声，仿佛在补一句：「诸位大可从这一句掂出她证词的分量。」

===Original===
The resting, however, was but figurative. In a moment the summing up began, a summing which, at first passionless as algebra, dealt with technical points.

“Gentlemen,” said Peacock turning again to the jury, “the evidence in this case is of the kind known to you perhaps as circumstantial. Evidence of this nature can lead and often does lead to a conclusion more satisfactory than direct evidence can produce. Circumstances cannot lie any more than facts can. Unless we resort to them it is in vain that we attempt to detect and to punish crime. Crime shuns the light of day. It seeks darkness. It courts secrecy. The assassin moves stealthily. He calls no witness to see him shoot his victim down. If you wait for an eyewitness you grant impunity to crime. It is true, and probably you will be so told by counsel for the defense, that there are cases in which the innocent have been convicted. Yet if men have been erroneously convicted on circumstantial evidence, so have they been convicted on direct testimony also. That is not, though, a reason for declining to accept such testimony. The possibility of error exists alike. But because men may err do they refuse to act? Because wheat may be blighted does the farmer refuse to sow? No, gentlemen. Until we have means of knowledge beyond our present faculties we must accept this kind of evidence or grant practical immunity to crime.”

===Chinese===
然而这「停止」不过是比喻。不多时，结案陈词开始——这陈词起初如代数一般不动声色，处理的是技术要点。

「诸位，」皮科克又转向陪审团说，「本案的证据，或许你们知道，属于所谓间接证据（circumstantial evidence）。这类证据能、且往往能，导出一个比直接证据更令人满意的结论。情形不会说谎，正如事实不会说谎。除非借助它们，我们侦查与惩罚犯罪便都是徒劳。罪行躲避日光。它寻求黑暗。它巴望隐秘。凶手悄悄行动。他不会叫一个证人来看他如何击倒被害者。你们若要等一个目击证人，便是给罪行以豁免。不错，辩方律师大概也会对你们这样说：确有过无辜者被定罪的案例。可若有人因间接证据而被误判，那么因直接证词而被误判的也有。然而这并非拒绝接受此类证词的理由。出错的可能两者皆有。可人因为可能出错就不行动了吗？农夫因为麦子可能遭病害就不播种了吗？不，诸位。在我们拥有超越今日能力的求知手段之前，我们必须接受这一类证据，否则便等于实际地纵容犯罪。」

===Original===
The exordium concluded, Peacock warmed to his work. What he said he seemed to literally tear from his mouth. It was an arraignment not delivered but hurled, headlong, with the force and rush of a cavalry charge. Before it Orr’s points sank overwhelmed. To replace them with others of his own Peacock made new ones, evolving them with a fire and lucidity that was pyrotechnic. They were like bombs exploding before the jury’s eyes. He arraigned the defendant, arraigned the defense, stampeded their tactics, denounced Annandale’s manner, which he declared to be that of a hardened criminal, and pictured him as a jealous husband who, in accordance with a plot long premeditated, had first lured his victim to his house, then following him thence had murdered him in the darkness, but who now swore that he was drunk and remembered nothing. “Assuming that he was drunk,” Peacock shouted, “his intoxication was a feigned disguise, assumed for the purpose and legally an aggravation of his dastard crime.”

Beneath, in the unlovely street, an organ was tossing a jig. The jolts of it mounted to the court, fusing with Peacock’s voice, adding their vulgarity to his own, and it was to the wretchedness of them that he said at last: “My duty is done.”

===Chinese===
开场白既毕，皮科克渐入佳境。他说出的话仿佛是从嘴里硬撕下来的。这是一篇控诉，不是讲出来的，而是头朝下摔出来的，带着骑兵冲锋般的力与势。奥尔所立的各点在它面前一一沉没。皮科克为以己之点取代之，又造出新的点子，以烟火般的炽烈与明快一一演化。它们如一颗颗炸弹在陪审团眼前炸开。他控诉被告，控诉辩方，冲散他们的策略，痛斥安南代尔的态度——他断言那是一个惯犯的态度——并把被告描摹成一个嫉妒的丈夫，依照一项蓄谋已久的阴谋，先把被害人诱到家中，再尾随其后在黑暗中将他谋杀，如今却发誓说自己醉了、什么都不记得。「就算他醉了，」皮科克吼道，「他的醉态也是一番伪装，为此目的而装出来的，在法律上是他那卑劣罪行的加重情节。」

底下，在那条不美的街上，一架手摇琴正抛出一支吉格舞曲。它那颠簸的节拍升上法庭，融进皮科克的嗓音，把自身的俗气加到他的俗气里去——正是在这两者的可憎之中，他终于说出：「我的职责尽了。」

===Original===
He had scored points by the dozen. In as many seconds Orr had their heads off by half.

“Harris, gentlemen, is the rock of the People’s case. His hand fashioned it. Without him it crumbles. Let me array for you Harris against Harris.”

Leisurely Orr began, showing the man’s hand for what it was, not dirty and disreputable merely, but discredited.

“Apart from that hand where is the promised evidence? Where is it? Where is that evidence? Gentlemen, not a bit of evidence have you had, not a molecule, not a minim, not a mite. At best or at worst any evidence producible against this defendant would be circumstantial. In telling you the value of such testimony the District Attorney has been good enough to leave it to me to explain that testimony of this character must, to be conclusive, exclude every other reasonable hypothesis. The District Attorney has further told you that circumstances cannot lie. Of all his statements that one and that one alone is correct. Circumstances cannot lie. But witnesses can. It is from them that circumstances are obtained. And though they furnish a million circumstances, what are these circumstances worth if they themselves are unsound? How unsound that reptile Harris is, you have, I believe, been enabled to judge. But even otherwise, even though the testimony of that saurian seem to you probable, I may remind you that the most probable things often prove false, for the reason that if they were exempt from falsity they would cease to be probable; they would be certain.

===Chinese===
他立下的论点以打计。同样多的秒数之内，奥尔把它们砍掉了一半脑袋。

「诸位，哈里斯是人民一方这桩案子的岩石。是这只手塑成了它。没有他，它便崩塌。让我为您排开——哈里斯对哈里斯。」

奥尔不慌不忙地开始，把那人的那只手揭示为它本来的样子——不止是肮脏不堪、声名狼藉，更是信誉全无。

「撇开那只手不论，应许的证据在哪里？在哪里？那证据在哪里？诸位，您连一星半点证据都没见到，没有一个分子、一滴、一毫。往好里说或往坏里说，能拿出来的、针对这位被告的任何证据，都只能是间接证据。地区检察官既已告诉您此类证词的价值，便也慷慨地把解释之责留给了我——此类证词若要成定论，必须排除其他一切合理的假说。地区检察官又告诉您，情形不会说谎。他所有的陈述之中，唯有这一句、且只此一句，是对的。情形不会说谎。可证人会。情形正是从他们那里得来的。纵使他们供出一百万种情形，他们本身若站不住脚，这些情形又值几何？那条爬虫哈里斯有多站不住脚，我相信您已能判断。可即便不然，纵然那条蜥蜴的证词在您看来似乎可信，容我提醒您：最可信的事往往证明是假的——理由是，它们若当真免于虚假，便不再是『可信』的了；它们便是确定的了。

===Original===
“Now what certainty has the District Attorney brought you? Instead of excluding every other reasonable hypothesis, he has opened the door to a dozen hypotheses infinitely more reasonable than his own. Except that the obligatory instrument does not appear to have been found, he has adduced nothing to show that the deceased did not commit suicide. He has adduced nothing to show that he was not robbed. The caretaker has testified that he was away from the park ten or fifteen minutes. The policeman who returned with him has testified that when he got there the gate was open. In the interim anyone may have entered, gone through the suicide, bagged his pistol for further booty and away.

“No, the District Attorney has not excluded these hypotheses, he has confined himself to picturing this defendant as a husband jealous of the deceased. But assuming that he was, how many other husbands may not have been jealous of him also? The bullet in evidence, the bullet extracted from the brain of the deceased, is one which, from a calculation of its lands and grooves, may or may not have come from a thirty-two calibre pistol. Anyway a thirty-two calibre pistol is among the exhibits. But how many more such pistols are there in this great city? The ownership of one is not a proof of crime. Nor is the fact that the body of the deceased was found in front of this defendant’s residence proof either. On the contrary. The park wherein it lay is a parallelogram, and a body in it would be practically in front of every other house in the square. How many jealous husbands reside in these houses I am not competent to say, but I am competent to tell you that the prosecution might just as well have arraigned any other resident there as this defendant; yes, and better, were it not for Harris.”

===Chinese===
「那么地区检察官给您带来了什么确定之物？非但没有排除其他一切合理假说，他反而为十二种比他自己的假说合理得多的假说敞开了大门。除了那件必不可少的凶器似乎不曾被找到之外，他举不出任何东西证明死者没有自杀。他也举不出任何东西证明死者没被劫。看守作证说他离开公园有十到十五分钟。随他回去的警察作证说他到时大门是开的。在这一段空当里，任何人都可能进入、搜过自杀者的身、把他的手枪当作额外的战利品装走、扬长而去。

「不，地区检察官并未排除这些假说，他只满足于把这位被告描摹成一个嫉妒死者的丈夫。可就算他是，又有多少别的丈夫可能同样嫉妒死者？作为证物的那颗子弹——从死者脑中取出的那颗——据其膛线与凹槽的测算，可能、也可能不是出自一把三十二口径的手枪。反正一把三十二口径的手枪是在证物之列。可这座大城里这样的手枪还有多少把？拥有一把并不能证明有罪。死者尸体在被告住所前被发现，同样不能证明有罪。恰恰相反。尸体所在的公园是个平行四边形，里头一具尸体几乎就在广场上每一户人家的门前。这些房子里住着多少嫉妒的丈夫，我没资格说；但我有资格告诉您：控方大可去指控那儿的任何一位住户，正如指控这位被告一样；是的，甚至更该如此——若不是因为有哈里斯。」

===Original===
Orr paused. “Reptile,” he cried. “Knave, fraud, thief, liar—”

But the Court admonished him that his time was up. Without a murmur, in the middle of a sentence, he sat down. It was another point that he had scored.

“Gentlemen—”

The Recorder’s charge to the jury followed, a charge clear, undeclamatory, without literature or bias, in which they were instructed regarding the law and left to determine the facts.

The jury filed out. The Recorder evaporated. Annandale sauntered away. Into adjacent corridors the great room emptied itself.

===Chinese===
奥尔顿了一顿。「爬虫，」他叫道，「无赖、骗子、窃贼、说谎的——」

可法庭告诫他，他的时间到了。他毫无怨言，话说到半句，便坐下了。这又是他立下的一分。

「诸位——」

继而是法官对陪审团的指示——清楚、不事铺张、无文采亦无偏倚的一番指示，其中向他们讲明法律，把事实的裁定留给他们自己。

陪审团鱼贯而出。法官如雾散去。安南代尔踱步走开。这间大厅把自己倒空进了相邻的走廊。

===Original===
Orr, stationing associates on guard, went over to Sylvia, urging her to go.

But Sylvia refused at first to budge. The jury, she declared, would be back in five minutes.

“It may be five hours,” said Orr. “You had far better go home. No? Well then I will take you to my offices and have something brought in.”

“Is it far?” Sylvia warily asked. But presently she assented, stipulating however that Annandale should be brought there the moment he was freed.

Orr tossed his head. “That may not be for years, until after an appeal. I have not an idea what the jury will do. But I know one thing: the last of the lot, the twelfth, looked at me during my summing up with something that was a cross between a sneer and a scowl.”

“Yes,” Mrs. Waldron interjected, “I noticed him. But it seemed to me that he was not listening. It seemed to me that he was in pain. But do, Sylvia, let us go. It is cruel of you. I am starving.”

===Chinese===
奥尔安排搭档们守着，自己走到西尔维娅跟前，劝她走。

可西尔维娅起初不肯动。陪审团，她宣称，五分钟就会回来。

「也许是五个钟头，」奥尔说，「您还是回家去为好。不肯？那么我带您到我事务所，让人送点吃的进来。」

「远吗？」西尔维娅小心地问。但她不久便答应了，只一个条件：安南代尔一获释便立刻送到那里。

奥尔扬了扬头。「那也许是好几年后的事了，要等上诉之后。我毫无头绪陪审团会怎样。但有一件事我知道：那帮人里头最后一个，第十二个，在我作结案陈词时望着我，神情介乎冷笑与怒目之间。」

「是啊，」沃尔德伦太太插话，「我也留意到他。可我觉得他不是在听。我觉得他在痛。可是西尔维娅，咱们走吧。你也太狠心了。我饿坏了。」

===Original===
In Orr’s neighborly offices shortly the lady was fed. Sylvia too ate something. Orr himself would have bolted a bite, but he had to hurry away, though promising as he did so everything that Sylvia asked, promising to stand on his head if she wished it.

Once back in the court he found it still empty. In the corridors reporters and idlers lounged, speculating on the verdict, prophesying that the deliberations of the jury would be brief. But time limped. An hour passed, two hours, three. Enervated and empty Orr went down and out to a little restaurant across the street. Presently it was reported that the jury were coming in. Orr hurried back, but however he hurried, he was late. The court had refilled. As he entered he heard someone say:

“Not guilty.”

Abruptly the room hummed like a wasps’ nest. There were raps for order, commands for silence, threatened punishments for contempt.

===Chinese===
在奥尔的事务所——就近的几间屋子里——那位夫人不久便被喂饱了。西尔维娅也吃了一点。奥尔自己本想囫囵吞一口，可他得赶紧回去，虽如此仍答应西尔维娅所求的一切，答应她若要他倒立都成。

他一回到法庭，发现它仍空着。走廊里，记者和闲人们游荡，揣测着裁决，预言陪审团的评议不会久。可时间拖着脚走。一个钟头过去了，两个，三个。奥尔又虚又空，下楼穿过街到对面一家小饭馆。不久有人来报：陪审团要回来了。奥尔赶回，可无论他怎么赶，还是迟了。法庭已重新挤满。他一进门便听见有人说：

「无罪。」

整间屋子陡然像一窝黄蜂般嗡嗡作响。敲槌声命令秩序，命令肃静，以惩处藐视法庭相胁。

===Original===
The hubbub subsided, the Recorder thanked and dismissed the jury. He turned to Peacock. “Are there any further charges against the prisoner?”

“There are none, Your Honor.”

The Recorder nodded at Annandale. “You are discharged.”

Orr tried to get at him. But at that moment the crowd interfered. In making a circuit to reach Annandale, he found himself among the departing jury. They had all left the box, all save the twelfth, who apparently had stumbled.

About them reporters circled. The foreman was relating that they had been practically unanimous for conviction, but that one of them, the twelfth, had insisted so obstinately on the poverty of the evidence that with him finally they had voted to acquit.

“But where is he?” the foreman interrupted himself to ask. “Where is the twelfth juror? Where is Durand?”

===Chinese===
喧嚣平息，法官向陪审团致谢并解散之。他转向皮科克。「对被告可还有别的指控？」

「没有了，法官阁下。」

法官朝安南代尔点了点头。「你被开释了。」

奥尔想挤到他跟前。可那一瞬间人群挡住了他。他绕一圈想接近安南代尔，却发觉自己陷在了正在离场的陪审员中间。他们都离开了陪审席，唯独第十二位例外——他显然绊了一跤。

记者们围着他们打转。首席陪审员正在讲述：他们其实几乎一致主张定罪，可有一个人——第十二位——固执地坚持证据之薄弱，使他们最终随他投票无罪。

「可他在哪儿？」首席陪审员打断自己的话问道，「第十二位陪审员在哪儿？迪朗（Durand）在哪儿？」

===Original===
Then only was it seen that he was still in the box, crouching there, his face ashen where it was not violet, a hand held to his side.

In a moment he was surrounded. To those nearest he looked and gasped.

“Give him some brandy,” a reporter suggested. But now into the little group Peacock had forced his way. Orr edged nearer.

The juror gasped again. “I am dying,” he groaned. “It is my heart. Send for a priest. I killed him. I am the man.”

Skeptically Peacock sniffed. “You killed whom?”

“He is delirious,” the reporter exclaimed.

“I killed him,” Durand repeated.

“But whom? And why?” Peacock, bending a bit, impressed in spite of himself, inquired.

Slowly, laboriously, painfully at that, Durand from a pocket drew a picture.

===Chinese===
这时人们才看见，他仍在陪审席里，蜷在那里，脸上一处青灰、一处发紫，一只手按着肋下。

一眨眼他便被围住了。他望着身边最近的人，喘着气。

「给他来点白兰地，」一名记者建议。可这时皮科克已挤进了这一小圈人。奥尔也挨近了些。

那位陪审员又喘了一口。「我要死了，」他呻吟，「是心脏。去请一位神父来。我杀了他。我就是那个人。」

皮科克将信将疑地吸了吸鼻子。「你杀了谁？」

「他在说胡话，」那记者叫道。

「我杀了他，」迪朗重复。

「杀了谁？又是为何？」皮科克略略俯身，不由自主被打动了，探问道。

慢慢地、费力地、痛楚地，迪朗从一只口袋里掏出一张照片。

===Original===
“Curse him,” he muttered. “There he is. He disgraced my perle, my daughter Marie, but she wrote me where to find him and I did; I found him in the park and I shot him there, through the head, through the h-head,” he stammered and clutched at his heart.

From his hand the picture had slipped. Orr edged closer, stooped for it, recovered it, then in heightening wonder stared. The picture was a colored photograph that displayed the chiseled features, wonderful eyes and thin black mustache of one whom he had known. Above it was written “Marie’s Husband.”

“It is Loftus,” he exclaimed.

Peacock wheeled. “Loftus,” he cried. Instantly to question further, he turned to the juror again.

But even as he turned he saw that the trial was over. Spasmodically the man’s mouth had twitched, his head had fallen; before a higher court he had gone.

===Chinese===
「该死的他，」他喃喃道，「就在这里。他辱了我的珍珠（perle）、我的女儿玛丽，可她写信告诉我去哪儿找他，我便找了；我在公园里找到他，我就在那里开枪打了他，打穿他的头，打穿他的——头，」他结巴着，一手抓向自己的心口。

照片从他手里滑落。奥尔挨得更近，俯身拾起，一看之下，愈发惊奇地瞪大了眼。那是一张上了色的照片，显示出他所认识的某人的面容——如雕如刻的五官、妙不可言的眼睛、两撇细细的黑胡髭。照片上方写着：「玛丽的丈夫。」

「是洛夫特斯，」他叫道。

皮科克旋过身。「洛夫特斯，」他叫。立刻要进一步追问，他又转向那位陪审员。

可他还没转过去便看见，审判已经结束了。那人嘴一阵痉挛，头一垂；他已去了另一个更高的法庭。

===Original===
Peacock, the marvel of it upon him, turned anew to Orr. Foes while the battle was raging, the two men now were like the commandants of opposing forces who, the conflict ended, meet and embrace.

Peacock rubbed his eyes. “What this confession means, Orr, you as well as I appreciate.” Instinctively his voice had sunk into that undertone which Death, when it comes, exacts. “Yes,” he continued, “Annandale is not merely acquitted, he is cleared. For that, believe me, I am glad. As for Loftus, he got from that dead father only what he deserved.”

To this Orr, about whom the marvel of it all still also clung, assented. “Justice,” he replied, “is rarely human, but sometimes it is divine.”

He would have said more perhaps, but Annandale was approaching. Obviously the latter was as yet wholly unaware of this new climax to his case. He was looking doubtfully around.

===Chinese===
皮科克，在这桩奇事的震撼下，又转向奥尔。两人战时是敌手，此刻却像两支敌军的指挥官，战事既毕，相逢而拥抱。

皮科克揉了揉眼。「这番自白意味着什么，奥尔，你和我一样明白。」他的声音本能地低下来，低到死亡降临时所要求的那一种低语。「是的，」他接着说，「安南代尔不仅仅被开释，他是被还了清白。这一点，相信我，我是高兴的。至于洛夫特斯——他从那位已死的父亲那里得到的，不过是他应得的。」

对此，奥尔——奇事同样缭绕着他——表示赞同。「正义，」他答，「很少是属人的，可有时它是属神的。」

他也许还会多说几句，可安南代尔正走过来。后者显然对自己案子这新的高潮还全然不知。他犹疑地四下张望。

===Original===
“I can’t find my hat,” he announced. Then at once, detecting the unusual in the attitude of those that stood about, his eyes followed theirs to the box from which court officers, long trained to the lugubrious, swiftly and silently were removing the corpse.

A keeper appeared. In his hand was the hat. Annandale took it, his eyes still following the body that was being removed.

“There,” said Orr abruptly, “there is the man that killed Loftus. But come,” he added. “Sylvia is waiting. Goodbye, Peacock. We have both had a lesson in presumptive proof.”

Astonishment lifted Annandale visibly like a flash. “What!” he exclaimed. “What! What’s all this?”

Then Orr, a hand on his arm, led him away, and as they passed from the General Sessions, told him what had occurred.

===Chinese===
「我找不到我的帽子，」他宣告。随即，他便察觉到周围那些人神态有异，他的眼睛随着他们的目光望向陪审席——那里，几名对惨事早已训练有素的法警，正迅速而无声地搬走那具尸体。

一名看守出现。手里拿着那顶帽子。安南代尔接过来，眼睛仍跟着那具被抬走的尸体。

「喏，」奥尔突然说，「那就是杀洛夫特斯的人。可走吧，」他又添一句，「西尔维娅在等。再会，皮科克。我们俩都上了一课——关于推定证据的一课。」

惊愕像一道闪电，明显地把安南代尔整个人掀了起来。「什么！」他叫道，「什么！这是怎么回事？」

于是奥尔，一只手搭在他臂上，领他走开。当他们走出一般刑事法庭时，奥尔把方才所发生的一切，告诉了他。

## X The Verdict / X 裁决

===Original===
In the days of the Doges there was a Gold Book in which the First Families of Venice shone. In New York there is also a Gold Book, unprinted but otherwise familiar. The names that appear there have earned the cataloguing not from medieval prowess, but from money’s more modish might.

At the Metropolitan Opera House, two years and a fraction after the trial, the Gold Bookers were on view—men who could have married the Adriatic, dowered her too, whose signatures were potenter than kings. There also were women fairer than the young empresses of old Rome, maidens in thousand-dollar frocks, matrons coroneted and tiaraed. On the grand tier they sat, a family-party air about them, nodding to each other, exhaling orris, talking animatedly about nothing at all. Into their boxes young men strolled, lolled awhile, sauntered away.

In one of these boxes was Sylvia, looking like an angel, only, of course, much better dressed. Behind her was Annandale. They were quite an old couple. They had been married fully a year. In the box with them was Orr.

===Chinese===
昔日威尼斯总督治下，曾有一本金册（Gold Book），名门望族皆熠熠其间。纽约也有一本金册，虽未刊印，却为人熟知。登入其中的名姓，所以获此登录，并非凭中世纪的武功，而是凭金钱那更为摩登的势力。

庭审之后两年又几个月，在大都会歌剧院（Metropolitan Opera House），金册中人均在座可见——这些人本可以娶亚得里亚海为妻，并陪上一份嫁妆，他们的签名比诸王更有力。还有比古罗马年轻皇后们更美的妇人，身着千元衣裙的少女，戴冠冕与头饰的贵妇。他们坐在大包厢层，一副家宴的气派，彼此点头，吐着鸢尾花香，热烈地谈着些全无所谓的事。年轻男子踱进他们的包厢，歪一会儿，又踱出去。

在其中一只包厢里，是西尔维娅，看上去像个天使——当然，衣裳讲究得多。她身后是安南代尔。他们已经是一对老夫妻了。结婚满一年了。与他们同包厢的还有奥尔。

===Original===
On the stage a festival was in progress, a festival for ear and eye, the apogee of Italian art, a production of Aïda. A quarter of a century and more ago when that opera was first given in Cairo, there was an accompanying splendor more lavish than it, or any other opera, has had since. But it was difficult to fancy that even then there was a better cast. Before the tenor had completed the opening romanza he had enthralled the house. Good-looking, as tenors should be, stout as tenors are, he suggested Mario resurrected and returned.

“Celeste Aïda!” he sang, and it was celestial. Then at once Amneris, enacted by a debutante, appeared and the house was treated to what it had not had since Scalchi was in her prime, a voice with a conservatory in the upper register, a cavern in the lower and, strewn between, rich loops of light, of opals, flowers, kisses and stars.

===Chinese===
台上正进行着一场盛宴——耳与眼的盛宴，意大利艺术的极顶，一出新排的《阿依达》（Aïda）。四分之一个世纪多以前，这部歌剧在开罗首演时，曾有一番比它、或比任何别的歌剧此后所经历的都更铺张的盛饰相配。可即便彼时，也难想象会有更好的阵容。男高音还未唱完开场的那首小咏叹调（romanza），全院已被他攫住。他相貌英俊——正如男高音所该是——身材敦实——正如男高音所正是——他叫人想起复活的、归来的马里奥（Mario）。

「圣洁的阿依达（Celeste Aïda）！」他唱道，而那的确是圣洁的。紧接着阿姆内丽丝（Amneris）登场——由一位新秀扮演——全场得以领受自斯卡尔基（Scalchi）全盛以来再未有过的一份好东西：一副在高音区有如温室、在低音区有如洞穴的嗓音，其间撒满了光的丰盈回环、欧泊、花朵、亲吻与星辰。

===Original===
Princess she was and looked, yet, despite the glory of her raiment, rather a princess in a drawing-room than the daughter of a Pharaoh in a Memphian crypt. She seemed pleased, sure of her charm, and she pleased and charmed at sight. The house, the most apathetic—save Covent Garden—in the world, and, musically, the most ignorant as well, rose to her.

Sylvia turned to Orr. In his gloved hand was a program. “What a dear!” she murmured. “Who is she?”

Orr, before answering, looked at Annandale. The latter’s eyes were on the roof. He may have been drinking the song, unconscious of the singer. But it is more probable that his thoughts were elsewhere, though hardly in the Tombs, where, during his relatively brief sojourn, he had lived at the relatively reasonable rate of a hundred dollars a day.

===Chinese===
她是公主，看上去也是。然而尽管她衣饰辉煌，与其说她是孟菲斯陵墓中某位法老之女，不如说她更像客厅里的一位公主。她似是自得，确信自己的魅力，而她也一望便取悦、迷住了众人。这座歌剧院——除考文特花园（Covent Garden）外——是世上最无动于衷的，论音乐也是最无知的，此刻却为她起立。

西尔维娅转向奥尔。奥尔戴着手套的手里拿着一本节目单。「真可爱！」她低语，「她是谁？」

奥尔回答之前，看了看安南代尔。后者的眼睛正盯着天花板。他也许正在痛饮那支歌，对唱歌的人浑然不觉。可更可能的是他的心思在别处——虽不至于在「坟墓」里：他在那里短暂羁留期间，是以每日一百元这个尚称合理的价位过活的。

===Original===
“A debutante,” Orr answered. “She is billed as Dellarandi.”

The curtain fell. The box was invaded. Men indebted to Mrs. Annandale for dinner, or who hoped to be, dropped in. Orr got up and went out.

The second act began. There was an alternating chorus. During it Amneris sat mirroring her beauty in a glass. Presently her voice mounted, mounting as mounts a bird and higher. She was joining in the incomparable duo that ensues. It passed. A march, blown from Egyptian trumpets, followed, preluding the dance of priestesses which precedes the tenor’s return. As that progressed the leader of the orchestra shook like an epileptic. From his own musicians, from those on the stage, from chorus and singers, he drew wave after wave of melody, a full sea of transcendent accords that bathed Sylvia with harmony, filtered through her, penetrating blissfully from fingertips to spine.

===Chinese===
「一位新秀，」奥尔回答，「节目单上署名黛拉兰迪（Dellarandi）。」

幕落。包厢被闯入。欠安南代尔太太一顿饭的、或巴望被请的男人们，陆续进来。奥尔起身出去。

第二幕开始。一段交替的合唱。其间阿姆内丽丝坐着，对镜自照自己的美貌。不久她的声音升起来，像一只鸟那样升，升得更高。她正加入紧随其后的那段无与伦比的双人唱。它过去了。一段进行曲从埃及式小号中吹出，继之而来的是女祭司的舞蹈，先于男高音的返场。进行之际，乐队指挥像癫痫发作般摇晃。他从自己的乐手、从台上的乐手、从合唱与歌者那里，抽出一浪又一浪的旋律——一片超越和弦的汪洋，将西尔维娅浸在和谐里，滤过她，从指尖到脊梁，极乐地贯穿而过。

===Original===
Delightedly she turned to Annandale. The visitors had gone. Orr was entering. In his bulldog face was an expression vatic and amused.

“Yes,” he resumed, seating himself at Sylvia’s side, “she is billed as Dellarandi, but I knew her as Marie Leroy.”

Sylvia started, her lips half parted, her eyes dilated with surprise.

Annandale bent forward. “What is it?” he asked.

“Amneris, the contralto. Do you know who she is?”

“I know she is a devilish pretty woman. What about her?”

“She is the girl whose father was the twelfth juror in your case.”

Annandale, who had been standing, literally dropped with astonishment in a chair. But Sylvia was insatiable. She could not ask enough, she could not get the answers quickly enough in reply. Orr, however, knew very little, odds and ends merely that he gathered in the lobby, summarily that the girl had married Tambourini, the music teacher, and was regarded as destined to be one of the great queens of song.

===Chinese===
她欣喜地转向安南代尔。客人们已走。奥尔正进来。他那张斗牛犬的脸上，是一种先知而好玩的神情。

「是啊，」他在西尔维娅身旁坐下，接着说，「节目单上她是黛拉兰迪，可我从前认识的她是玛丽·勒鲁瓦。」

西尔维娅一惊，嘴唇半张，眼睛惊得睁大。

安南代尔俯身向前。「怎么了？」他问。

「阿姆内丽丝，那位女低音。你知道她是谁吗？」

「我只知道她是个漂亮得要命的女人。她怎么了？」

「她就是那位父亲在你那案子里任第十二位陪审员的姑娘。」

安南代尔本站着，闻言竟一屁股惊坐在椅子里。可西尔维娅还想知道得更多。她问得不够多，得到的回答来得不够快。然而奥尔所知也甚少，不过是在门厅里东鳞西爪听来的，简而言之：那姑娘嫁了声乐教师坦布里尼，被人视为注定将成为歌坛伟大的女王之一。

===Original===
So interested were all three that the third act was barely noticed. It took the melting beauty of the final duo to distract them from the debutante. But the witchery of that aria would distract a moribund. It was with the bewildering loveliness of it in their ears that they moved out from the box.

“Terra addio!” Orr repeated from it as they descended the stair.

“No, not addio,” said Sylvia; “that poor girl may have said farewell to many hopes, but there are other and better ones for her now. I feel that she must have suffered terribly, and because of that suffering we should acquit her of what she did.”

“That is the verdict, is it?” said Orr.

“That is my verdict,” Sylvia answered. Then touching Annandale’s arm she looked up at him and added, “It is yours, too, dear, is it not?”

===Chinese===
三人皆兴致勃勃，第三幕几乎没怎么看。直到末后那段双人唱的销魂之美，才把他们从那新秀身上分了心。可那支咏叹调的魔力，连垂死之人也会被分去神。他们是在耳中那一份令人迷惑的可爱里走出包厢的。

「大地，再见了（Terra, addio）！」下楼时奥尔从其中重复道。

「不，不是 addio，」西尔维娅说，「那可怜的姑娘也许曾向许多希望告别过，可她如今有了别的、更好的希望。我觉得她必定受过极大的苦，正因那份苦，我们应当宽恕她从前所做的一切。」

「这便是裁决，是不是？」奥尔说。

「这是我的裁决，」西尔维娅答。然后她触了触安南代尔的手臂，仰起脸望着他，又添一句，「这也是你的，亲爱的，是不是？」
